returned the floor is some $k_1\le k_0-1$, and the hypothesis is
opened at the image $k_1$ or $0$ and the new goal at $[k_1]$, both at the floor
$k_1$. By \cref{cert:lem:fresh} the name $k_1\in[k_1,k_0)$ is fresh for the
readings of $a$, of the members of $g$, of the major and of the opened matrix,
which are closed at $k_0$, and these readings are unchanged at the floor $k_1$.
The proof of \cref{cert:prop:terminal} for $L$ uses of its eigenparameter only
this freshness and the closedness of the hypothesis and the new goal at the floor
of the continuation, and it uses of the opening of the matrix only that it is
made at a floor at which the goal is closed and that does not admit the
eigenparameter, which holds since $k_1<k_0$. So the step is the inference of
$L$ with $k_1$ as eigenparameter, and the induction hypothesis applies to the
continuation, whose context members are closed at $k_1$.

(b) Take the certificate of $L$ given by \cref{cert:prop:terminal}, and translate
it along the run of $L$, keeping an injective map $\nu$ from the names that $L$
has allocated to names that $P$ has allocated: where $L$ allocates its
eigenparameter $n$ after a returning major call, $P$ uses the floor $k_1$ left
by the corresponding major call, and $\nu$ is extended by $n\mapsto k_1$; every
negative scalar of a later packet is replaced by its image under $\nu$. The
range of $\nu$ before the extension consists of names not below $k_0>k_1$, so
$\nu$ stays injective. A canonical tree contains only allocated names; a tree
closed at the floor of $L$ is mapped to a tree closed at the floor of $P$ at the
corresponding point, and an injective renaming of names commutes with openings
and preserves literal equality, the arity tests and membership. Every call,
including a call that closes the neutral major by membership, returns and
decrements the floor, so a name is available at every allocation. The run of
$P$ on the translated certificate therefore follows the run of $L$ call by call.
\end{proof}

The order is essential: an introduction opens its matrix at the witness before
the major call reserves the eigenparameter, so the witness cannot name it; the
package records that a stream certificate whose witness is $\Suc z$ for the name
$z=-1$, at the entry floor $0$, is rejected after two records, before its neutral
major is read. Accepted stacks of $L$ and $P$ differ in the
names of parameters, so only the accepted goals are claimed equal; in the
certificate of an induction with a used hypothesis the final floor is $-281$
against $-140$, one reservation per call. The programs are $L_{\mathrm r}$
(\texttt{code/inherited\_tokens240.py}, $240/266/551$) and $L'_{\mathrm r}$
(\texttt{code/inherited\_ast261.py}, $242/261/563$), against $L$; both read one
record per line and carry the double dagger. The earlier package also contains
a Lean model of the order of reservations on abstract call trees; it is not part
of the formalization of the repository, and the proof above does not use it.

\begin{proposition}[Tail call for the continuation]\label{cert:prop:tail}
In the global stream program of \cref{cert:prop:leaf}, replace the loop by
a recursive call on the new goal $O(a;[k])$ and the context extended by the new
hypothesis, evaluating the opening of the new goal before that of the
hypothesis. The resulting program accepts the same stream certificates with
the same consumption of packets and the same final global floor.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
An opening at a fixed floor reads the floor, allocates new arrays and makes
no assignment to a global name and no read of the input, so two successful
openings at one floor commute, and if either fails both orders reject before a
further packet is read. The global floor is decremented once, after the major
call, in both programs, and is not restored by a returning call. A loop
iteration of the control and a call of the new program begin in the same
state; every returning call returns the null value, so the expressions around
recursive calls are unchanged. Induction on the consumed packets gives the
claim in both directions.
\end{proof}

The program, of \pkg{smaller\_presentations} (\texttt{code/checker\_tokens.py}),
has $242/269/606$ against $243/271/616$. Python does not eliminate tail calls,
so the physical recursion limit is reached sooner.

\subsubsection*{Compositions on the leaf programs}

\begin{proposition}[Compositions]\label{cert:prop:leaf-compositions}
Each of the following programs has the accepted canonical goals of its
control; (c) and (d) have exactly the runs of their controls, and every input
accepted by (a) or (b) is accepted by its control with the same run.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The program of $474$ bytes of \pkg{computation\_leaves} with the
selector $\mathrm{bool}(m\lor 3\bmod\lvert a\rvert)$ written
$\mathrm{any}((m,3\bmod\lvert a\rvert))$ ($272/308/473$;
\pkg{vacuous\_specialization}, \texttt{compact/checker473.py}, and
\pkg{smaller\_presentations}, \texttt{code/checker\_bytes.py}, with identical
text).
\item The program of $477$ bytes of \pkg{head\_conversion} with the same change
($274/306/476$; \pkg{476\_byte\_presentation}, \texttt{compact/compact476.py},
\pkg{export\_specialization}, \texttt{compact476/code/compact476.py}, and
\pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements}, \texttt{code/checkers/compact\_checker.py}).
\item The whole-input local three-field program of \pkg{three\_field}
($255/271/606$) with the floor argument rewritten as in
\cref{cert:prop:stream-rewrites}(a) ($255/269/605$;
\pkg{ast269\_successor\_rigidity}, \texttt{checker/checker\_ast269.py}, and
\pkg{selective\_normalization}, \texttt{compact/code/checker269.py}, with
identical text).
\item The programs of \pkg{whole\_json\_refinement} above, against the stream
programs of \pkg{computation\_leaves}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a), (b): the argument of \cref{cert:prop:eager-selector}(a),(b): where
$3\bmod\lvert a\rvert$ is defined the two selectors have the same truth value,
its evaluation changes no state, and the decrement follows it in both; at every
selector of a canonical run the goal is a canonical formula or an opened matrix,
of arity $1$, $2$ or $3$, and neutral majors return at membership before the
selector. (c): the proof of \cref{cert:prop:stream-rewrites}(a) uses only that
$k\le 0$ before the decrement and that the program applies no exact-type test to
internal trees; both hold under the whole-input reader, which is not involved.
(d): the framing argument above.
\end{proof}

\pkg{checker\_refinements} delivers the programs (b) and (c) once more
(\texttt{checkers/byte476/checker476.py} and
\texttt{checkers/ast269/checker269.py}, with the texts of
\pkg{476\_byte\_presentation} and \pkg{ast269\_successor\_rigidity}); its proof
of (c) relates the internal Boolean \texttt{False} of the rewritten floor
expression to the integer $0$, as in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:tuple-stream}.
The compact part of \pkg{root\_conversion\_raw} delivers the program (b) a fifth
time (\texttt{compact/checker476.py}, byte for byte the text of
\pkg{checker\_refinements}), with the control of $477$ bytes of
\pkg{head\_conversion} and the proof of (b): on a successful run the remainder
$e=3\bmod\lvert a\rvert$ is defined, and for every value $m$ and every defined
$e$ the truth values of $m\lor e$ and of $\mathrm{any}((m,e))$ agree; at every
selector of a canonical run the goal is a nonempty canonical formula or opened
matrix, because a neutral goal $[\,]$ returns at membership. Its contract states
the public grammar as the least class containing every equation, $\D{x}{y}B$ for
a compound public $B$, and the guarded $\D{x}{y}$ of \cref{cert:def:U}; this is
the grammar of that definition. It also delivers a readable expansion
($307/362/1847$, \texttt{compact/readable\_checker.py}) whose agreement with the
program is supported by $396$ executions of $66$ cases and is not proved.

\pkg{smaller\_presentations} records a rewriting that must not be made: in
the candidate, writing $O(t;[s,u,t],3)$ if $s$ else $u$ as
$s\land O(t;[s,u,t],3)\lor u$ confuses an opened right side equal to the scalar
$0$ with a missing value. The goal $\It{2}{1}{u}{0}=1$, whose left side is $0$,
then has the candidate $[[[s],u,0],u]$ with $s$ and $u$ encoding $1$, and the
rewritten program accepts it, while the program of (a) rejects it.

\subsubsection*{Rewritings of the validated programs}

\pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements} and \pkg{476\_byte\_presentation} rewrite
the native programs of \cref{cert:prop:late-compositions} and the program of
$621$ bytes; their controls are the programs (e), (f), (g) and (h) of that
proposition ($321/371/618$, $334/380/596$, $330/380/638$, $340/385/621$) and the
program (d) ($323/369/622$).

\begin{proposition}[Rewritings of the validated programs]\label{cert:prop:abstraction}
Each of the following changes preserves the runs of the program on inputs of
integers and arrays, except (iv), which has the relation of
\cref{cert:prop:eager-selector}(a),(b) to its control.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The product $(d\bmod 2)\cdot B$ of the parity with a Boolean $B$ written
as the bitwise conjunction $d\mathbin{\&}B$.
\item In the leaf condition of the first opening, the exact-integer test
``the leaf is identical with its conversion to an integer'' followed by
$a\bmod 2=[a>1]$ replaced by ``$a$ is not the object \texttt{False}'' followed
by $(a\mathbin{\&}1)=[a>1]$.
\item The import of the reader as a name, assignment expressions in place of
an assignment followed by its only use, the opener applied where it is bound,
and, in a procedure, an early return on membership replaced by the negated
test around the rest of the body.
\item The selector $\mathrm{bool}(m\lor e)$ with $e$ the test
$\lvert a\rvert\bmod 2=u$ written $\mathrm{any}((m,e))$.
\item The extension $g\mathrel{+}=[0]$ of a list written
$g\mathrel{+}=\texttt{b'\textbackslash x00'}$.
\item The flag of the first opening given the default value $1$, with the
literal $0$ passed at the final call.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) For an integer $d$, $d\mathbin{\&}\texttt{False}=0=(d\bmod 2)\cdot
\texttt{False}$ and $d\mathbin{\&}\texttt{True}=d\mathbin{\&}1=d\bmod 2$, also
for negative $d$; both sides evaluate $d$ and then $B$ once, without effect.
(ii) The condition is evaluated in the first opening, at floor $0$, after the
range test $0\le a<d$, or inside a guard $v\le p$ that holds whatever $p$ is
when $v=0$. On the values of the reader that reach it: for an exact integer
$a\ge 0$ both conditions are $a\bmod 2=[a>1]$, since $a\mathbin{\&}1=a\bmod 2$;
\texttt{False} fails both; \texttt{True} fails the old exact-integer test, and
in the new condition $\texttt{True}\mathbin{\&}1=1\neq[\texttt{True}>1]$; a
float fails the old test and makes $a\mathbin{\&}1$ undefined; strings,
\texttt{null} and objects fail the range test. So the opener retains the same
leaves and rejects the others. The new test is an identity with a singleton
of the language, so the qualification about one runtime of the old test
(\cref{cert:thm:context-phase}) is removed. (iii) Each rewriting evaluates the
same expressions in the same order and binds the same objects before their
first use. (iv) \Cref{cert:prop:eager-selector}. (v) Every reachable context is
an ordinary list, and an in-place extension by a byte string appends its byte
values, here the integer $0$; the identity test of exact integers is true for
every exact integer and cannot separate the two zeros. (vi) The first call
receives $v=1$ in both programs, the major call passes $v\defeq 0$ explicitly,
and the final call now passes the literal $0$; no expression reads the
defaults of the function, which serves as the missing image.
\end{proof}

The programs of \pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements} are, with changes (i)--(iv):
$317/364/617$ (native, (i), (ii), on (e)), $321/358/622$ (native, (i)--(iii), on
(e)), $332/373/594$ (native, (i), (ii), (iv), on (f)), $328/377/634$ (strict,
(i), on (g)), $335/368/643$ (strict, (i), (iii), on (g)) and $340/382/616$
(strict, (i), (iv), on (h)); the native programs satisfy the goal contract
G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ and the strict programs the typed contract T/ed. The
programs of \pkg{476\_byte\_presentation} are, with (v) or (vi):
$319/369/622$ (on (e)), $321/367/626$ (on (d)), $328/378/642$ (on (g)), all with
(v), and $332/379/594$ (on (f)) and $338/384/619$ (on (h)), with (vi). The
change (v) costs four bytes and saves tokens and nodes.

\subsubsection*{Uniform zero in the validated native programs}

\pkg{smaller\_presentations} transfers the recoding $R$ of
\cref{cert:sec:uzero} to the validated native programs: the public goal is
$B^{\sharp}=R(B)$ (\cref{cert:def:zero-alias}), and the proof phase opens with
$O^{+}$, written \texttt{a or 1} in the retained-scalar case. Let $N_1$ be the
program $332/373/594$ above with the leaf condition of the first opening
replaced by $v\le d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$ preceded by a test of exact integers
(``$a$ is $+a$'', or ``$a$ is not \texttt{True}'' with $a\mathbin{\&}1$), and with
$O^{+}$ in the proof phase; and let $S_1$ be the program $340/382/616$ with the
leaf condition replaced by $v\le d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$, without a test of
exact integers, which its test of the printed form makes unnecessary, and with
$O^{+}$ in the proof phase.

\begin{proposition}[Uniform zero, validated]\label{cert:prop:uzero-native}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $S_1$ accepts only inputs that satisfy $J$ and whose goal satisfies
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}(a,0)$; $N_1$ accepts only goals that satisfy $J$ and
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}(a,0)$.
\item If $S_1$ accepts, or $N_1$ accepts an input whose consumed packets
satisfy $J$, then the reading of the goal, in which the scalar $1$ reads as
$0$, is a closed theorem of $\mathcal A$.
\item For every closed theorem $B$ of $\mathcal A$ in the language with
equality and $D$, both programs accept a certificate with goal $B^{\sharp}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) With the flag $1$ and the floor $0$, a leaf is retained exactly when
$0\le a<d$, the depth $d$ is odd and $a$ is odd: the bitwise conjunction is $1$
exactly then. \texttt{True} fails ``$a$ is $+a$'', since $+\texttt{True}$ is the
integer $1$, and fails ``$a$ is not \texttt{True}''; \texttt{False} has even
parity; a float makes $d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$ or $a\mathbin{\&}1$ undefined.
The arity conditions are those of the control, and \cref{cert:lem:scope} gives
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$; as in \cref{cert:prop:native-int}(b), the array test
$a\cdot 0\neq 0$ of $N_1$ traverses a nonempty string of an admitted length
without end, which is not an acceptance. For $S_1$ the test of the printed
form gives $J$
(\cref{cert:thm:repr,cert:prop:gate-or}).

(b) After the first opening the program is the kernel of its control with
$O^{+}$, and the soundness proof of that kernel
(\cref{cert:prop:context-first}(a) in its native form, with
\cref{cert:prop:native-first,cert:prop:eager-selector}) uses only
\cref{cert:lem:opening,cert:lem:exposure,cert:lem:fresh,cert:lem:zeroinstance},
which hold for $O^{+}$ (\cref{cert:lem:plus-opening}). The source of a
transport may now be the true-valued scalar $1$; the major $[1,u]$ then reads
$0=\upsilon$, and the step is an instance of \rulename{Tr} of $\mathcal A$ with
the source $0$. In an introduction the field $u$ is $0$ or $[\,]$, since a
true-valued $u$ selects the computation branch; the mode test selects progress
only at a matrix of even arity, which is an equation or has the reading $0=0$,
and the goal $\D{x}{y}(0=0)$ is a theorem. A goal $[\,]$ reaching the
introduction is unpacked into a call with two arguments; it reads $0=0$, so
the conclusion is derivable whatever the continuation. The closed goal is the
reading of $B^{\sharp}$ (\cref{cert:lem:recoding}).

(c) Compile a derivation of $B$ in $\mathcal A$ with $R$ applied to the
expression fields and the packets $[[R(s),R(u)],R(s),R(u),R(F)]$ for
\rulename{Tr}, $[[R(B'),R(B'),R(B')],0,R(u),R(B')]$ for \rulename{QE} with
matrix $B'$, $[R(\D{x}{y}B'),0,0,0]$ for \rulename{DE},
$[0,0,R(a),R(h)]$ and $[0,R(n),R(a),R(h)]$ for \rulename{I0} and \rulename{IS},
and $[0,0,[\,],R(w)]$ and $[0,0,0,R(w)]$ for \rulename{DI}-case and
\rulename{DI}-progress. By \cref{cert:lem:truthy} every source $R(s)$ and every
initializer $R(a)$ is true-valued, so transport from $0$ selects the equation
branch and every computation, including \rulename{IS} at the predecessor $0$,
selects the computation branch, without the repair of
\cref{cert:thm:certzero}. Openings commute with $R$
(\cref{cert:lem:recoding}), every selector sees a nonempty canonical formula,
and the run follows the compiled derivation as in
\cref{cert:thm:uzero-complete}. The goal $B^{\sharp}$ satisfies
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$, and canonical certificates satisfy $J$.
\end{proof}

% ed.: the package states the raw cases of the introduction (empty goal,
% neutral matrices of even arity) in evidence/uniform_zero_proof.txt and not in
% its report; they are included in (b). Its report compares the programs with
% the controls of 594 and 616 bytes, which encode zero by the scalar 0.
The programs are $328/372/581$ ($N_1$ with ``$a$ is $+a$'',
\texttt{code/checker\_native.py}), $328/369/587$ ($N_1$ with the explicit
exclusion of \texttt{True}, \texttt{code/checker\_native\_portable.py}) and
$334/378/608$ ($S_1$, \texttt{code/checker\_strict.py}). The test ``$a$ is
$+a$'' relies on the measured runtime, in which unary plus returns an exact
integer itself and turns a Boolean into an integer. The package compares them
with the programs of $594$ and $616$ bytes. Their goals are the encodings
$B^{\sharp}$, so, as for \pkg{zero\_alias}
(\cref{cert:prop:zero-alias}), they satisfy neither the completeness of their
controls nor its converse, and they are listed under their own codes. With the
encoding of the controls they share the calculus, the four-field format and
the kernel. The package also notes that the administrative markers keep their
roles: the neutral major, the source marker of closure elimination and the
counter flag of a zero computation stay $0$, progress is selected by $0$ and
cases by $[\,]$, so a converter must recode the expression fields only.

\subsection{An even offset of the depths}\label{cert:sec:offset}

This subsection follows \pkg{offset472} and, for the uniform terminals in the
shifted encoding, \pkg{468\_presentation}, \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf},
\pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf}, \pkg{scalar\_constancy} and
\pkg{procedure\_presentation}. The program of $473$ bytes of
\cref{cert:prop:leaf-compositions}(a) opens with the eager opener $E_k$, whose
fallback evaluates the opened image also at an even depth
(\cref{cert:lem:eager}). At the formula root, of depth $0$, the scalar $0$ lies
outside the range $[k,0)$ for every floor $k$, so its opening consults the
missing image and fails. For this reason that program, like the program of
$477$ bytes of \pkg{head\_conversion}, uses the empty array as neutral resource
and as neutral major; replacing only the initial context $[[\,]]$ by $[0]$ gives
a program that rejects every certificate with a neutral major. \pkg{offset472}
moves every depth instead, so that the formula root lies above the scalar $0$.

\begin{definition}[Shifted reading]\label{cert:def:offset}
Let $e\ge 0$. The \emph{$e$-shifted reading} is the reading of
\cref{cert:def:reading} with the following changes. A term position below $p$
slots has the depth $2e+3+2p$, and a formula position the depth $2e+2p$. At a
term position below $p$ slots a numeric scalar $r$ reads as $0$ if
$0\le r<2e+3$, as the slot $j$ if $2e+3+2j\le r<2e+5+2j$ and $j<p$, as the
parameter named $r$ if $k\le r<0$, and as the hole otherwise. Arrays, dummy
slots and annotations are read as in \cref{cert:def:reading}, and the child of
an array of arity $r$ at depth $d$ lies at depth $d+5-r$. The
\emph{$e$-shifted canonical encoding} is the encoding of \cref{cert:def:enc}
with the slot $j$ written $2e+3+2j$. The $e$-shifted openers are $O_k$ of
\cref{cert:def:opener} and $E_k$ of \cref{cert:sec:leaves} with the image
validated at the root term depth $2e+3$ instead of $3$.
\end{definition}

For $e=0$ these are the definitions of \cref{cert:sec:codec}. The canonical
encoding contains, besides arrays, only the scalar $0$, negative names and
slot codes, which are the scalars of value at least $3$; so the $e$-shifted
canonical encoding of an expression is obtained from its canonical encoding by
adding $2e$ to every scalar of value at least $3$.

\begin{lemma}[Shifted codec]\label{cert:lem:offset}
Let $e\ge 0$. \Cref{cert:lem:opening,cert:lem:exposure,cert:lem:canonical,cert:lem:fresh,cert:lem:eager,cert:lem:dummy-erasure,cert:lem:leaves}
hold for the $e$-shifted reading, encoding and openers, with every depth that
they mention increased by $2e$: the formula root has depth $2e$, the root term
depth is $2e+3$, and a closed goal $[B]$ opens its witness at the depth
$2e+2$. If $e\ge 1$, then for every floor $k\le 0$ the scalar $0$ is retained by
$O_k(\cdot;\bot,2e)$ and by $E_k(\cdot;\bot,2e)$, is closed at every floor, and
reads $0=0$ at the formula root.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The proofs of the cited lemmas use the encoding through four facts only.
(i) The parity of the depth of a position is its sort. (ii) The child depth
$d+5-r$ adds two slots at a unary array, changes the sort at a binary one and
adds one slot at a ternary one. (iii) At a term position of depth $D$ below
$p$ slots, the range $[k,D)$ of the opener is the disjoint union of the
interval $[k,0)$ of parameters, of the interval of $0$, and of the intervals of
the $p$ slots, ordered from the innermost to the outermost; hence the scalars
in range are exactly those that do not read as the hole, and reading at the
depth $D-2$ turns exactly the interval of the outermost slot into the hole.
(iv) An image validated at the root term depth has, below $q$ of its own
binders, only scalars in the range of the root term depth plus $2q$, which read
as $0$, as parameters or as its own binders at every term position, below any
number of further slots. In the $e$-shifted reading $D=2e+3+2p$, the interval
of $0$ is $[0,2e+3)$, the slot intervals are $[2e+3+2j,2e+5+2j)$ and the root
term depth is $2e+3$; facts (i) to (iv) hold with the same arguments. The
interval of parameters does not depend on $e$, so the freshness lemma and its
proof are unchanged, and the proof of the canonical opening lemma uses the slot
codes only through (iii). For the last statement, $0\in[k,2e)$ for $k\le 0$
exactly when $e\ge 1$, a scalar at a formula position has no hole and reads
$0=0$, and a retained scalar is not replaced by either opener.
\end{proof}

Let $P_{+}$ be the program of \cref{cert:prop:leaf-compositions}(a), which
reads four-field packets and the header $[A,0]$, with four changes: the
default depth of the opener is $2$ instead of $0$; images are validated at the
depth $5$ instead of $3$, in the fallback of the opener and in the candidate
of the leaf test; the witness of an introduction is opened at the depth $4$
instead of $2$; and the initial context is $[0]$ instead of $[[\,]]$. It reads
$1$-shifted canonical certificates whose packets without substantive major have
the major field $0$.

\begin{proposition}[Shifted direct-leaf program]\label{cert:prop:offset}
Consider inputs of integers and arrays whose last element is $[A,0]$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If a call of $P_{+}$ with goal $a$, context $g$ and floor $k$ returns, and
$a$ and the members of $g$ are closed at floor $k$ in the $1$-shifted reading,
then the auxiliary reading of $a$ is derivable in $\Teq$ from the auxiliary
readings of the members of $g$. If $P_{+}$ accepts an input whose goal $A$ is the
$1$-shifted canonical encoding of a closed public formula, that formula is a
theorem of $\mathcal U$.
\item For every closed public theorem of $\mathcal U$ there is a certificate that
$P_{+}$ accepts with the $1$-shifted canonical encoding of that theorem as goal.
\end{enumerate}
Hence $P_{+}$ accepts exactly the $1$-shifted canonical encodings of the closed
public theorems of $\mathcal U$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) The soundness of the control is \cref{cert:prop:leaf}(a) for the
four-field program of $474$ bytes, whose proof rests on
\cref{cert:prop:header,cert:prop:scalar} and \cref{cert:lem:eager}, transferred
to the eager selector by \cref{cert:prop:leaf-compositions}(a). These proofs use
the encoding only through the lemmas listed in \cref{cert:lem:offset}, and the
neutral resource and the neutral major $[\,]$ only through four properties:
(1) it is closed at every floor and reads $0=0$; (2) it is false-valued, and
so is its threefold repetition, so that a packet with this major is an
introduction; (3) it is equal to no nonempty array, in particular to no
candidate $M^{\circ}$ of the replacement test, which is a nonempty array in
every case (\cref{cert:prop:scalar}); (4) a call with this goal returns by
membership of the initial resource, which is a member of every context, since
contexts are extended by concatenation. The scalar $0$ has (1) by
\cref{cert:lem:offset} with $e=1$, (2) since $0\cdot 3=0$, (3) since no scalar
equals an array, and (4) since $E_k(0;\bot,2)=0$ for $k\le 0$. The selector
$\mathrm{any}((m,3\bmod\lvert a\rvert))$ has the same value for $m=0$ and for
$m=[\,]$. So the induction of those proofs applies with the $1$-shifted lemmas.
For the second statement, a $1$-shifted canonical encoding contains no negative
scalar, so it is closed at the floor $0$ of the header and reads as the encoded
formula, and \cref{cert:rem:imported} concludes.

(b) Compile as in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:leaf}(b), with its
branch-aware treatment of early closures, in the $1$-shifted canonical encoding
and with the major field $0$ in place of $[\,]$ in every packet without
substantive major. By \cref{cert:lem:offset} the equalities of trees on which
that proof relies hold for the shifted encodings: the canonical opening lemma
for the replacement test, for the openings of a matrix, of a closure and of an
iterator body, and for the identity of the candidate $H_k$ with the canonical
computation instance; the instance of \rulename{IS} at the predecessor $0$ is
obtained from the leaf $Z=0$ and \rulename{SE} as there. The candidate of a
logical packet is defined, since a canonical template is opened in it at the
depth $5$ instead of $2$, which raises every depth by $3$, as for $e=0$. A
neutral major is closed by membership of $0$, which the program tests before
it reads a packet, so it consumes no packet.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the shifted program]\label{cert:rem:offset}
The proposition concerns the accepted canonical goals and makes no claim about
the raw acceptance of $P_{+}$ and of its control, which differ: the threefold
repetition of a binary major has arity $6$, and its children are traversed at
the depth $1$ instead of $-1$, so an annotation $0$ can be retained by one
program and replaced by the other; a scalar $1$ at the formula root is retained
by $P_{+}$. A canonical certificate of the control becomes one of $P_{+}$ by
adding $2$ to every slot code of an expression field and writing $0$ for the
neutral major $[\,]$; the package's translator does this for canonical
certificates and is not part of the proof. By the argument of
\cref{cert:prop:header}(b), which uses the scalar $0$ of a canonical goal at a
term position, now of depth at least $5$, a header $[A,k_0]$ with $k_0$ an
array or a positive integer is rejected; the package states its contract for the
header floor $0$ only. \pkg{offset472} also states the family of
\cref{cert:def:offset} for every $e\ge 0$ and observes that $e=1$ is the least
offset for which the scalar $0$ can serve as neutral formula. Its soundness
argument is carried out for the ordinary reading in the native core as well;
the converse of soundness is asserted only for public formulas, where the two
readings agree: the matrix $E(y)\equiv(y=\Suc 0)$ has a derivable ordinary
reading $E(0)\to\forall y\,E(y)$ of $\Q{y}E$, since $E(0)$ is false, while its
progressive reading $\forall y\,(E(y)\to E(\Suc y))$ fails at $y=\Suc 0$.
\end{remark}

The program (\texttt{offset472.py}) has $271/307/472$ against the program of
$473$ bytes of \pkg{vacuous\_specialization} ($272/308/473$,
\texttt{control473.py}): the initial context saves one token, one node and one
byte, and the depth constants have the same length. Its goals are $1$-shifted
encodings, so it is listed under its own code.

\subsubsection*{Uniform terminals in the shifted encoding}

\pkg{468\_presentation} applies to the four-field uniform terminal program of
\pkg{shared\_leaf} and \pkg{uniform\_terminals} ($272/306/469$,
\cref{cert:prop:terminal}) the four changes that turn the program of $473$ bytes
into $P_{+}$: the default depth $2$, the root term depth $5$ in the fallback of
the opener and in the candidate, the witness depth $4$, and the initial context
$[0]$. Call the result $T_{+}$. Its calls differ from those of $P_{+}$ only at
the terminal test: a call of $P_{+}$ returns if $E_k(a;\bot,2)$ is a member of
$g$, and otherwise removes a packet $[m,s,u,t]$ and returns if $a$ equals the
candidate $H_k(s,u,t)$ of \cref{cert:def:leaf}, formed with the image validated
at the depth $5$; a call of $T_{+}$ removes a packet first and returns if
$E_k(a;\bot,2)$ is a member of $g+[H_k(s,u,t)]$. Otherwise both programs perform
the same logical step. A returning call of $T_{+}$ returns a nonempty list, and
one of $P_{+}$ the value \texttt{True} or a nonempty list; the caller uses only
the truth value.

\begin{theorem}[Terminal packets in the shifted encoding]\label{cert:thm:shifted-terminal}
On inputs of integers and arrays whose last element is $[A,0]$, there is a
certificate that $T_{+}$ accepts with the goal $A$ if and only if there is one
that $P_{+}$ accepts with the goal $A$. Hence $T_{+}$ accepts exactly the
$1$-shifted canonical encodings of the closed public theorems of $\mathcal U$
(\cref{cert:prop:offset}).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By \cref{cert:lem:eager}, which holds in the $1$-shifted encoding by
\cref{cert:lem:offset}, a defined opening $E_k(a;\bot,2)$ is $a$ itself: an
out-of-range scalar would evaluate the missing image, the checker function, and
fail. Both programs evaluate this opening before any comparison of a call ($T_{+}$
after removing its packet), so both validate the same goal before their first
comparison, and the test
``$E_k(a;\bot,2)=H$'' of $T_{+}$ is the test ``$a=H$'' of $P_{+}$.

From $T_{+}$ to $P_{+}$, by induction on the tree of calls of an accepting run,
in the order of evaluation (major call before continuation). At a call whose
opened goal is a member of $g$, delete the packet that $T_{+}$ removed there;
$P_{+}$ returns at its membership test without reading a packet, and the
construction of $H$ by $T_{+}$, which succeeded, changes no context, floor or
input. At any other call keep the packet: if the opened goal equals $H$, both
programs return; otherwise both perform the same logical step on the same packet,
and the induction hypothesis applies to the major call and to the continuation.
Terminal calls allocate no parameter, so the floors of corresponding calls agree.
The kept packets, in the order of consumption, together with the unused prefix,
form a certificate that $P_{+}$ accepts with the same goal.

From $P_{+}$ to $T_{+}$, insert the packet $[0,0,0,0]$ at every call at which
$P_{+}$ returns by membership. Its candidate is $[[0,0,0],0]$, formed without an
opening, since the false-valued source selects the right side $0$; the opened
goal is a member of $g$, so $T_{+}$ returns. Every other packet is kept, and the
calls correspond as before. Inserted packets allocate no parameter, so every
later floor and every name is unchanged.

The second statement follows from the first by \cref{cert:prop:offset}, whose
soundness covers arbitrary integer and array trees inside the certificate.
\end{proof}

The theorem concerns the existence of certificates; the two programs do not
accept the same stacks, since one removes a packet at a hypothesis leaf and the
other does not. A canonical certificate of the uniform terminal program of
$469$ bytes becomes one of $T_{+}$ under the translation of
\cref{cert:rem:offset}; the package's translator is untrusted and the proof
does not use it. The program (\texttt{code/compact468.py}) has $271/305/468$,
against $272/306/469$ for its unshifted control (\texttt{code/control469.py},
the text of \pkg{shared\_leaf}) and against $271/307/472$ for $P_{+}$
(\texttt{code/reference\_offset472.py}, the text of \pkg{offset472}). The package
also delivers a readable form ($327/391/1572$, \texttt{code/checker\_readable.py}),
whose agreement with $T_{+}$ is supported by $264$ executions and is not proved,
and a validated companion, \texttt{code/checker\_validated.py}
($690/773/2064$), which runs the kernel of $T_{+}$ (the same assignment, compared
as syntax trees) on the parsed input after three tests: every value of the whole
input is an exact integer or an array, the last element is $[A,0]$ with the
integer $0$, and $A$ satisfies the following predicate $V$. For a tuple $\sigma$
of slots, innermost first, each marked genuine or dummy, a term satisfies
$V_{\mathrm t}(\sigma)$ if it is $0$; or a scalar $5+2j$ with slot $j$ of $\sigma$
genuine; or $[t]$ with $t$ in $V_{\mathrm t}$ of $\sigma$ extended by two dummy
slots; or $[n,b,h]$ with $n,b$ in $V_{\mathrm t}$ of $\sigma$ extended by one dummy
slot and $h$ in $V_{\mathrm t}$ of $\sigma$ extended by one genuine slot. A
formula satisfies $V(\sigma)$ if it is $[s,t]$ with $s,t$ in $V_{\mathrm t}(\sigma)$,
or $[B]$ with $B$ in $V$ of $\sigma$ extended by two genuine slots, where, if $B$
is an array of arity $2$, both of its entries are arrays $[7,0,h]$ of arity $3$.

\begin{proposition}[The validated companion]\label{cert:prop:shifted-validated}
A tree satisfies $V(\,)$ exactly when it is the $1$-shifted canonical encoding of
a closed public formula of $\mathcal U$. Hence the validated companion accepts an
input exactly when the input consists of integers and arrays, its last element
is $[A,0]$ with such an $A$, and $T_{+}$ accepts it; it accepts exactly the
encodings of closed public theorems of $\mathcal U$, on arbitrary JSON input.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By induction on the tree. Slot $j$ below $p$ slots is written $5+2j$ in the
$1$-shifted encoding (\cref{cert:def:offset}); the canonical encoding uses only
$0$ for zero, slot codes and negative names, and $V_{\mathrm t}$ admits $0$ and
the codes of genuine slots, so it excludes the other spellings of zero, negative
names (the goal is closed) and references to dummy slots, which the canonical
encoding never makes. The dummy slots added at a successor and at the counter
and initializer of an iterator, and the genuine state slot of the body, are
those of the reading, by the child depth $d+5-r$. Below $\D{x}{y}$ the variable
$y$ is slot $0$ and $x$ slot $1$; at the counter of an iterator in the matrix one
dummy slot is added, so $y$ is written $7$ there, and an equation matrix
$[[7,0,h],[7,0,j]]$ is the guarded matrix $\It{y}{0}{u}{h}=\It{y}{0}{v}{j}$ of
\cref{cert:def:U}, with $h$ and $j$ checked as terms below their state slots. A
compound matrix is a formula $[B']$, which is checked recursively; arrays of arity
$3$, which encode $Q$, are rejected. The test of the alphabet applies the
exact-type test of integers, which excludes Booleans, and the reading of the
consumed packets by the kernel is that of $T_{+}$ on the same parsed array.
Soundness is then \cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal}; for completeness, the
certificates given by that theorem consist of integers and arrays and end with
$[A,0]$.
\end{proof}

The companion is not compared with the compact programs and has its own code.

\begin{proposition}[Collision of generated hypotheses]\label{cert:prop:mode-collision}
In the stream and four-field programs, the hypothesis generated at an
elimination with the unary major $[B]$, before its opening, is the closure
$[B,B,B]$, which is opened at the fresh parameter; the hypothesis generated at an
introduction whose goal is $\D{x}{y}\Q{v}C$ is the opened matrix, an array
$[C',C',C']$ of arity $3$, which is opened at $0$. Hence a selector that reads
the image of the opening from the arity of the generated hypothesis alone gives
both the same image.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The closure of the elimination is the threefold repetition of the major. At an
introduction the goal $[[C,C,C]]$ is opened at its witness by an opening of its
only child, which keeps the arity $3$ and opens the three copies alike.
\end{proof}

A selector that gives the fresh image to every hypothesis of arity $3$ therefore
grants progress at matrices with $Q$ at their root, which by
\cref{cert:prop:closure-progress} yields induction for every native formula; one
that gives them the image $0$ opens the closure of an elimination of $D$ at $0$
in place of a fresh parameter, which is not an instance of \rulename{DE}. This is the
shortcut that \pkg{shared\_leaf} rejects (\cref{cert:sec:leaves}); restricting
the public goal to equality and $D$ does not exclude the auxiliary formulas of
the derivation of \cref{cert:prop:closure-progress}. \pkg{468\_presentation}
claims no certificate that a program with this selector accepts for a false or an
underivable goal, and $T_{+}$ keeps the selector of its control.

\subsubsection*{Further deliveries of $T_{+}$, and the checking function as a procedure}

This part follows \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf}, \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf}, the
checker part of \pkg{scalar\_constancy} and the compact part of
\pkg{procedure\_presentation}. The first three deliver $T_{+}$ again, each byte
for byte the text of \pkg{468\_presentation}: \texttt{code/checker468.py} of
\pkg{468\_shared\_leaf}, \texttt{code/checker.py} of \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf}
and \texttt{checkers/code/checker\_bytes468.py} of \pkg{scalar\_constancy}, with
the control $T$ of \pkg{shared\_leaf}, also byte for byte
(\texttt{code/control469.py}, \texttt{controls/shared\_leaf469.py},
\texttt{checkers/controls/bytes469.py}). Each package obtains it from $T$ by the
four changes stated above and presents it as the composition of the shared leaf
with the even offset. The compact part of \pkg{selfseed\_injectivity} delivers
$T_{+}$ once more (\texttt{compact/checker468.py}, $271/305/468$, against $T$), a
reconstruction that its package records as byte for byte the text
\texttt{code/compact468.py} of \pkg{468\_presentation}; its recorded hash is the
one that \pkg{468\_presentation} records for that file, and its checking
function is literally the kernel of the validated companion of
\pkg{468\_presentation}; it is not a new program.

\begin{remark}[The three proofs of the shifted terminal program]\label{cert:rem:shifted-proofs}
\pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf} and \pkg{scalar\_constancy} prove
\cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal} by the correspondence with $P_{+}$ used there:
a defined eager no-image opening returns its argument literally, the packet
$[0,0,0,0]$ is harmless at a hypothesis leaf, and the different values returned
by successful calls are used only through their truth value.
\pkg{468\_shared\_leaf} gives instead a direct induction on the successful call
in the $1$-shifted reading, with the shifted forms of the opening, exposure and
freshness lemmas and of \cref{cert:lem:leaves} (\cref{cert:lem:offset}), and
completeness by the branch-aware compiler of \cref{cert:prop:leaf}(b) with a
dummy packet at every hypothesis leaf and neutral major; this is
\cref{cert:prop:offset} together with the step of \cref{cert:prop:terminal},
and adds nothing to the proof given here. \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf} also
delivers a readable form of $T_{+}$ ($305/363/1933$,
\texttt{code/readable\_checker.py}), and \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf} one without a
reported size (\texttt{code/readable\_checker.py}); their agreement with $T_{+}$
is supported by fixtures and is not proved. Both packages give certificates of
false goals for weakened programs (the end of \cref{cert:sec:validated}, and
the paragraph after \cref{cert:prop:inclusive-floor} for \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf}).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[The checking function as a procedure]\label{cert:prop:procedure}
Let $P$ be $T_{+}$, or $N_2$ or $S_2$ of \cref{cert:sec:validated-terminal}. In $P$
the checking function is an anonymous function whose body is a comprehension over
the one-element list $[q()]$ that destructures the removed packet into $m,s,u,t$
and has the element $E$, and the major call occurs in the conjunction
``$c(g,m,k)$ and $3m$''. Let $P_{\mathrm p}$ be the program in which the checking
function is a named procedure with the same parameters and defaults, whose body
is the statement $m,s,u,t\defeq q()$ followed by $E$ as an expression statement,
with the conjunction replaced by the disjunction ``$c(g,m,k)$ or $3m$'', and in
which the import of the reader follows the definition. Then $P$ and
$P_{\mathrm p}$ accept the same inputs, with the same packets consumed and the
same goals, contexts, floors and, for $N_2$ and $S_2$, values of the flag $v$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
This is the converse of \cref{cert:prop:truthy-first}. Every returning call of
$P$ returns a one-element list, a true value; every returning call of
$P_{\mathrm p}$ returns the null value, since the procedure has no return
statement. After a returning major call the conjunction of $P$ and the
disjunction of $P_{\mathrm p}$ both have the value $3m$; if it is false-valued,
both continue with the same opening of the matrix; a failed major call fails in
both. The comprehension iterates exactly once; it removes one packet and
destructures it as the assignment statement does, failing on the same values,
and then evaluates $E$. The assignment expressions in $E$, to the opener $i$, to
$a$, to $k$ and in $N_2$ and $S_2$ to $v$, bind in the enclosing anonymous
function in $P$, which is the rule of the host language for assignment
expressions in comprehensions, and in the procedure in $P_{\mathrm p}$; so the
opener, which reads $k$ and $v$, sees the same values. The missing image is the
global checking function in both, evaluated when the opener is formed during a
call, after the definition; no operation inspects its name, code or defaults. The
value of $E$ is used in $P$ only as the element of the result; in $P_{\mathrm p}$
it is discarded. The module of the reader is bound before the first call in
both. Induction on the tree of calls, in both directions, relates the runs; an
operation fails in one exactly when it fails in the other.
\end{proof}

Hence $T_{+,\mathrm p}$, the procedure form of $T_{+}$, accepts exactly the
$1$-shifted canonical encodings of the closed public theorems of $\mathcal U$
(\cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal}). \pkg{procedure\_presentation} delivers it as
\texttt{code/checker\_compact462.py} ($267/298/462$), against $T_{+}$
(\texttt{controls/offset\_shared468.py}, byte for byte the text of
\pkg{468\_presentation}, $271/305/468$). Its own lemma for the step from $P_{+}$
to $T_{+}$ is \cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal}, proved in the same way; it notes
that an unused packet can make the candidate undefined, so equality of the
accepted stacks is not claimed for that step. Removing the comprehension
removes frames, so the physical recursion limits differ.

\subsection{Uniform terminals in the validated programs with uniform zero}\label{cert:sec:validated-terminal}

This subsection follows the checker parts of \pkg{scope\_refinement} and
\pkg{validated\_leaves}, the strict part of \pkg{checked\_presentations} and the
validated part of \pkg{procedure\_presentation}. The
first two combine the uniform terminals of
\cref{cert:def:terminal}, the direct leaves of \cref{cert:def:leaf} and the
uniform zero of \cref{cert:prop:uzero-native} in the validated programs $S_1$
and $N_1$, which answers a question of \cref{cert:sec:questions}. The no-image
opening $O^{+}_k$ is not idempotent on raw trees: at the depth $1$, which only
annotations reach, a scalar $0$ is retained and returned as $1$, which a
second opening at the depth $1$ rejects; and at the floor $0$ and the depth $2$
the scalar $2$ opens to $0$, which opens to $1$. So \cref{cert:lem:retraction},
on which the proof of \cref{cert:prop:terminal} relies, is not available for
$O^{+}$, as both packages state; the proofs below use the following lemma
instead.

\begin{lemma}[Closed computation candidates]\label{cert:lem:closed-leaf}
Let $s,u,t$ be raw trees of integers and arrays and $k$ a floor. Let $c$ be
$[s]$ if $s$ is true-valued, and $s$ or the scalar $1$ if $s$ is
false-valued. Let $H\defeq[[c,u,t],\,O_k(t;[s,u,t],3)]$ if $s$ is true-valued
and $H\defeq[[c,u,t],\,u]$ otherwise, where $O_k$ may be replaced by $O^{+}_k$
or $E_k$. If $H$ is closed at floor $k$, its reading is an instance of
\rulename{I0} or \rulename{IS}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The proof of \cref{cert:lem:leaves} uses the validation of the goal through two
facts only: the trees $n,b,h,r$ of the goal $[[n,b,h],r]$ have readings
without a hole at their positions, and, in the successor case, $r$ is the
result of the opening $O_k(h;[n',b,h],3)$ with the same raw $n',b,h$. The first
holds for a closed $H$. The second holds by construction of $H$. The proof of
\cref{cert:lem:dummy-erasure} uses of the validation of $a$ only that every
scalar with a reading lies in the range of its position at the shallower depth,
which is the closedness of $a$ there. If $s$ is false-valued, the counter $c$ is
the scalar $0$, the empty array or the scalar $1$, each of which reads $0$ at
the term position of depth $5$, so the case of a false-valued counter in the
proof of \cref{cert:lem:leaves} applies. For $O^{+}_k$ and $E_k$ the lemmas used
in that proof hold by \cref{cert:lem:plus-opening,cert:lem:eager}.
\end{proof}

Let $S_2$ be the program $S_1$ of \cref{cert:prop:uzero-native} with three
changes.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The computation branch of the hypothesis is deleted: a true-valued
initializer field selects nothing.
\item Every call removes a packet $[m,s,u,t]$ at its entry and returns if the
opened goal $O^{+}_k(a;\bot,0)$ is a member of the tuple consisting of $0$, the
members of the context, and the candidate $H$ of \cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf}
with $c=[s]$ or $c=1$ and the opener $O^{+}_k$. The member $0$ is written as the
assignment that ends the first phase; the left operand of the membership test,
the opening of the goal, is evaluated before it, and the candidate after it.
The initial context is empty.
\item The floor of the new hypothesis is the fresh parameter exactly when $m$
is true-valued, or the opened matrix has arity $2$ and $u=2$.
\end{enumerate}
Let $N_2$ be $N_1$, with the test ``$a$ is $+a$'', with the same three changes.
Let $S_3$ be $S_1$ with the change (i), with (ii) replaced by: every call
removes a packet at its entry and returns if $O^{+}_k(a;\bot,0)$ is a member of
the context extended by $O^{+}_k(H;\bot,0)$, where $H$ is the candidate of
\cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} with $c=[s]$ or $c=s$, the flag of the first phase
being reset before $H$ is formed and the initial context being $[0]$; and with
(iii) replaced by: the floor of the new hypothesis is the fresh parameter
exactly when $m$ is true-valued, or the opened matrix has arity $2$ and
$u\neq 2$. Finally, let $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ be the strict token
and node profiles of \cref{cert:prop:abstraction} ($328/377/634$ and
$335/368/643$) with the two changes that turn the program $340/382/616$ into
$S_1$: the leaf condition of the first opening $d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$, without a
test of exact integers, and $O^{+}$ in the proof phase.

\begin{proposition}[Uniform terminals with uniform zero]\label{cert:prop:validated-terminal}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $S_2$, $S_3$, $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ accept only inputs that
satisfy $J$ and whose goal satisfies $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}(a,0)$; $N_2$ accepts
only goals that satisfy $J$ and $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}(a,0)$.
\item If one of $S_2$, $S_3$, $S^{\mathrm T}_1$, $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ accepts, or $N_2$
accepts an input whose consumed packets satisfy $J$, then the reading of the
goal, in which the scalar $1$ reads as $0$, is a closed theorem of $\mathcal A$.
\item For every closed theorem $B$ of $\mathcal A$ in the language with equality
and $D$, each of the five programs accepts a certificate with goal $B^{\sharp}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) The first opening of each program is that of $S_1$ or $N_1$; in
$S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ the first phase is marked by the empty
context, as in their controls (\cref{cert:prop:empty-phase}). In $S_2$, $N_2$ and
$S_3$ the flag of the first phase is $1$ when the goal is opened at the root
call and is reset only afterwards, before the candidate is formed. The root call
removes a packet before this opening, which changes nothing in the argument of
\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(a), and that argument applies.

(b) For $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(b) applies with the kernels of their controls,
whose soundness is that of the program (g) of \cref{cert:prop:late-compositions}
with \cref{cert:prop:abstraction}, and with \cref{cert:lem:plus-opening}. For
$S_2$, $N_2$ and $S_3$ the proof is the induction of
\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(b) on the returning call, for contexts whose
members are closed at the floor, with the following cases at the entry. Let
$a'=O^{+}_k(a;\bot,0)$; it is closed and has the reading of $a$
(\cref{cert:lem:plus-opening}). If $a'$ is the scalar $0$, the goal reads $0=0$.
If $a'$ is a member of the context, \rulename{Hyp} applies. In $S_2$ and $N_2$,
if $a'=H$, then $H$ is closed, and \cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} shows that its
reading, which is that of $a$, is a computation instance. In $S_3$, if
$a'=O^{+}_k(H;\bot,0)$, then $H$ is validated, hence closed, and has the reading
of $a'$, and the same lemma applies. The candidate enters no context. A call
that proves the neutral major $0$ removes a packet and returns by membership of
$0$: the opening of $0$ at the formula root in the proof phase is $0$, and $0$ is
the first member of the tuple in $S_2$ and $N_2$ and the initial member of every
context in $S_3$. In the other calls the logical steps are those of $S_1$ or
$N_1$, without the computation branch, so a true-valued $u$ selects nothing, and
with the new selector. In an elimination the selector is the fresh parameter, as
before. In an introduction the hypothesis $B(\tau,z)$ is granted exactly when
the opened matrix has arity $2$ and the marker condition holds; such a matrix
reads as a literal equation, and the step is \rulename{DI}-progress. Every
other matrix receives $B(\tau,0)$, and the step is \rulename{DI}-case, which
$\mathcal A$ has for every matrix. The remaining steps (transport with the source
$1$, the empty goal at an introduction) are those of the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(b).

(c) For $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ this is the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(c) with the completeness of the program (g). For
$S_2$, $N_2$ and $S_3$ compile as in \cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(c), with the
changes of \cref{cert:prop:terminal} and the branch-aware compiler of
\cref{cert:prop:leaf}(b). A hypothesis leaf and a neutral major receive a dummy
packet, $[0,0,1,1]$ for $S_2$ and $N_2$ and $[0,0,0,0]$ for $S_3$, whose
candidate is formed, and in $S_3$ opened, without failure. \rulename{I0} and
\rulename{IS} receive $[0,0,R(a),R(h)]$ and $[0,R(n),R(a),R(h)]$. In $S_2$ and $N_2$
the candidate is $[[1,R(a),R(h)],R(a)]$ or
$[[[R(n)],R(a),R(h)],O^{+}_k(R(h);[R(n),R(a),R(h)],3)]$, which is $R$ of the
computation instance by \cref{cert:lem:recoding}. In $S_3$ the candidate of
\rulename{I0} has the counter $0$, which $O^{+}$ returns as $1$, and $O^{+}$ fixes
canonical encodings with uniform zero (\cref{cert:lem:recoding}), so the opened
candidate is again $R$ of the instance. The predecessor $0$ of \rulename{IS} is
the true-valued scalar $1$, so no repair is needed. An introduction by cases
receives the marker $u=0$ in $S_2$ and $N_2$ and $u=2$ in $S_3$, and an
introduction by progress the marker $u=2$ in $S_2$ and $N_2$ and $u=0$ in $S_3$;
a compound matrix receives cases with any marker. Transport, \rulename{QE} and
\rulename{DE} receive the packets of \cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(c). In the
candidate of a canonical packet a closed term field occurs at a depth not below
the root term depth, and a template or a matrix at a depth at least $3$ above
the formula root; so all their scalars remain in range, the image of an opening
in the candidate consists of closed canonical terms and a field so placed, and
every opening in the candidate, and in $S_3$ the opening of the candidate, is
defined. The run then follows the compiled derivation.
\end{proof}

The programs are $S_2$ (\pkg{validated\_leaves},
\texttt{code/checker\_strict606.py}, $334/379/606$ against $S_1$, $334/378/608$),
$N_2$ (\texttt{code/checker\_native579.py}, $328/373/579$ against $N_1$,
$328/372/581$), $S_3$ (\pkg{scope\_refinement}, \texttt{code/strict\_bytes.py},
$335/382/607$ against $S_1$), $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ (\texttt{code/strict\_tokens.py},
$322/373/626$ against $328/377/634$) and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$
(\texttt{code/strict\_ast.py}, $329/364/635$ against $335/368/643$). The controls
of $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ encode zero by the scalar $0$ and are
programs of T/ed; the comparison crosses the encodings, as for
\pkg{zero\_alias}. \pkg{scope\_refinement} also delivers a readable form of $S_3$
($421/503/2152$, \texttt{code/readable\_strict.py}), whose agreement with $S_3$ is
supported by a differential test of $740$ inputs and is not proved. The tuple of
$S_2$ supplies the neutral resource by the value of the assignment that ends the
first phase; $S_3$ opens its candidate so that a counter $0$ becomes $1$, while
$S_2$ writes the counter as $s\land[s]\lor 1$ and needs no opening.
\pkg{validated\_leaves} states \cref{cert:thm:shared-leaf} for $\mathcal A$ in
place of $\mathcal U$, with the same proof, which the remark after that theorem
covers, and \cref{cert:lem:neutral-resource} for the neutral formula $0=0$. The
converter of \pkg{scope\_refinement}
recodes canonical certificates and rejects a retained computation that is not a
leaf; completeness is the compilation above, from derivations.

\subsubsection*{Conditional selection and the second marker}

The strict part of \pkg{checked\_presentations} gives three further programs
of the contract T/ed$_1$. In $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ the new goal
after the replacement test is formed as the element, selected by the test, of
the pair $(a,O^{+}_k(t;s,0))$, so that the source instance is opened also when
the test fails. Let $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ be $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ with this pair
replaced by the conditional expression ``$O^{+}_k(t;s,0)$ if the test holds,
else $a$''. Let $S^{\mathrm N}_2$ be $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ with the same change and
with the factor $\mathrm{bool}(m\lor\lvert a\rvert\bmod 2=u)$ of the new floor
written ``$1$ if $m$ else $\lvert a\rvert\bmod 2=u$''. Let $S_4$ be $S_3$ with
its change (iii) replaced by the change (iii) of $S_2$ (progress exactly at a
matrix of arity $2$ with $u=2$), and with the right operand
$g+[(v\defeq 0)\lor O^{+}_k(H;\bot,0)]$ of the terminal test written
$g+[(v\defeq 0),O^{+}_k(H;\bot,0)]$.

\begin{proposition}[Three further strict programs]\label{cert:prop:checked-strict}
The statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for
$S^{\mathrm T}_2$, $S^{\mathrm N}_2$ and $S_4$. Moreover every input accepted by
$S^{\mathrm T}_1$ or $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ is accepted by $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ or
$S^{\mathrm N}_2$ respectively, with the same packets consumed and the same calls.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_2$: the first opening is that of their
controls, which gives (a). The comparison of the replacement test, including the
opening $O^{+}_k(t;u,0)$ of the target, is evaluated in both programs. If it
holds, both open the source instance and take it as the new goal. If it fails,
the new goal is $a$ in both, and the control additionally evaluates
$O^{+}_k(t;s,0)$ and discards it; the opener reads no packet, assigns nothing
and allocates no parameter, so this evaluation can only cause a rejection. The
two factors of the new floor are equal integers after the multiplication by the
new floor: for a true-valued $m$ they are $1$ and \texttt{True}, otherwise the
same Boolean comparison, evaluated in the same order. Hence a run of the control
is a run of the new program, which gives the last statement and, with the
completeness of the controls, (c). For (b), the induction of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(b) for the controls uses the source instance
only at a step whose replacement test holds, where it is the new goal; at a step
whose test fails, the step is justified by the major and the generated hypothesis
alone (\rulename{DE} for a unary major, a neutral resource for a true-valued
major of another shape, a computation hypothesis or an introduction for a
false-valued major), so the induction applies
to the runs of the new programs as well.

For $S_4$: the value $0$ of the assignment is a member of the extended context
in both forms, since the initial context $[0]$ is a member of every context;
the order of evaluation is the same (the opened goal, then the assignment that
ends the first phase, then the opened candidate), so the terminal test has the
same value. The runs of $S_4$ are therefore those of $S_3$ except at the selector
of an introduction. Statement (a) is that of $S_3$. In the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(b) the hypothesis $B(\tau,z)$ is granted only
when the opened matrix has arity $2$, whatever the marker condition, so (b)
holds. For (c) compile as for $S_3$, with the dummy packet $[0,0,0,0]$, with the
marker $u=2$ for an introduction by progress and $u=0$ (or $[\,]$) for one by
cases, as for $S_2$; the opened candidate of an introduction packet $[0,0,2,w]$ is
defined, since the scalar $2$ is in range at the depths $5$ and $3$ and reads $0$
there, and a compound matrix receives cases with every marker.
\end{proof}

The programs are $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ ($319/370/630$, \texttt{code/strict\_tokens.py},
again as \texttt{strict/code/strict\_tokens.py}), $S^{\mathrm N}_2$
($327/358/642$, \texttt{code/strict\_ast.py}) and $S_4$ ($335/380/605$,
\texttt{code/strict\_bytes.py}); their controls in the article are
$S^{\mathrm T}_1$ ($322/373/626$), $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ ($329/364/635$) and $S_3$
($335/382/607$), from which they differ by exactly the changes stated.
% ed.: the package does not name S^T_1, S^N_1 and S_3; it compares with the
% programs of zero_alias (331/373/625) and S_1 (334/378/608), and supplies the
% T/ed profiles 328/377/634 and 335/368/643 as implementation controls. The
% differences above were read off the texts of the four programs pairwise.
The package compares them with the program of \pkg{zero\_alias} and $S_1$, and
supplies the strict token and node profiles of \cref{cert:prop:abstraction} as
controls of construction. It states the selector $\lvert a\rvert=u=2$ of $S_4$
as a repair found by its review: with the computation branch deleted, the
selector $\lvert a\rvert\bmod 2=u$ grants progress at a compound matrix for
$u=1$ (\cref{cert:rem:marked-mode}); the unrepaired draft is not delivered. The
untrusted translator of the package turns canonical certificates with
computation supplies into terminal certificates; the proof does not use it.

\begin{remark}[The mode of an introduction]\label{cert:rem:marked-mode}
With the computation branch deleted, a true-valued field $u$ reaches the
introduction. The selector $\lvert a\rvert\bmod 2=u$ of $S_1$ would then grant
progress at a compound matrix of arity $1$ or $3$ for $u=1$, which adds
induction for compound formulas (\cref{cert:prop:closure-progress}); this is the
hazard of the native direct-leaf programs of \pkg{computation\_leaves}
(\cref{cert:sec:leaves}). \pkg{scope\_refinement} withdraws a prototype of $606$
bytes with this selector, which is not a result. The two packages choose
opposite markers: in $S_2$ the scalar $2$ marks progress, in $S_3$ it marks cases
and is not a public numerical code. \pkg{scope\_refinement} notes that selecting
progress at every equation would be a sound selector for the guarded calculus
$\mathcal U$ but loses \rulename{DI}-case at an equation in $\mathcal A$, unless a
separate recovery theorem applies.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Variants that were examined]\label{cert:rem:examined-variants}
The packages record the following variants as unsuccessful; the figures are
theirs. \pkg{validated\_leaves}: writing the test $\lvert a\rvert=2$ as the truth
value of the slice $a[1{:}]$ gives a compact program of $464$ bytes that grants
progress also at a matrix of arity $3$, an auxiliary closure, as in
\cref{cert:prop:closure-progress}; the package refutes one proposed repair, a
decoder that reads nested closures as truth, by the template $F(x)$ whose inner
matrix is $\sel(x,0,y)=0$, true at $x=0$ and false at $x=\Suc 0$, which the
repaired decoder would let \rulename{QE} transfer; the slice $s[{:}1]$ as counter
(a program of $468$ bytes) produces the counter $[\,]$ in a zero computation
while the guard of a public equation carries the scalar $0$, and no repair is
given. \pkg{scope\_refinement}: the local floor $k=-\lvert g\rvert$ gives
$250/267/573$ and $k=1-\lvert g\rvert$ gives $251/268/576$, both above their
controls and needing a renaming of eigenparameters by branches
(\cref{cert:rem:floor-shortcut}); writing \texttt{for $\dots$ in q()} in place of
\texttt{for $\dots$ in [q()]} lets an empty packet produce an empty comprehension
and bypasses the check of the goal; moving the decrement into one arm of the
selector allocates no fresh parameter at a compound introduction; giving the
opener the next fresh name before the major and the witness are validated
admits future witnesses; returning the proved formula in place of a truth value
requires keeping the original goal through its replacements; and a canonical
successor-seeded recursor would remove about $17$ bytes of computation dispatch
but needs a normalizing substitution that costs more.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{The procedure form and the byte-string extension}

The validated part of \pkg{procedure\_presentation} applies two devices to the
programs of this subsection, at a revision that does not contain
\pkg{checked\_presentations}. Let $S_{2,\mathrm p}$ and $N_{2,\mathrm p}$ be the
procedure forms of $S_2$ and $N_2$ (\cref{cert:prop:procedure}), and let
$S^{\mathrm T}_{1,\mathrm b}$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_{1,\mathrm b}$ be
$S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ with the in-place extension
$g\mathrel{+}=[0]$ of the context written $g\mathrel{+}=\texttt{b'\textbackslash x00'}$.

\begin{proposition}[Two devices on the validated programs]\label{cert:prop:validated-devices}
The statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for
$S_{2,\mathrm p}$, $N_{2,\mathrm p}$, $S^{\mathrm T}_{1,\mathrm b}$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{1,\mathrm b}$; each accepts exactly the inputs of its control,
with the same run.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For $S_{2,\mathrm p}$ and $N_{2,\mathrm p}$ this is \cref{cert:prop:procedure}; the
reader, the test of the printed form of $S_2$, the initial call with $v=1$ and
the verdict are unchanged. For the other two it is the change (v) of
\cref{cert:prop:abstraction}: the initial context is the empty list and every
context passed to a call is a list, either that of the caller or one formed by
concatenation, so every extension in place is the extension of a list, and the
extension by the byte string appends the integer $0$ to the same list object,
which is also the list of the caller. No operation inspects the byte string after
the extension.
\end{proof}

The programs are $S_{2,\mathrm p}$ (\texttt{code/checker\_strict\_procedure.py},

\subsubsection*{The returned floor as the image, and rotated packets}

This part follows the checker part of \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers}, the
third delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation} and \pkg{prefix\_conditional}, all
at one revision that contains \pkg{procedure\_presentation} and
\pkg{direct\_packets}. The first two rewrite the stream program $L_{\mathrm r}$
of \cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}, the third rewrites $L_{\mathrm r}$,
$T_{+,\mathrm p}$ and $S_{2,\mathrm p}$. Recall that the image of the new
hypothesis in the final call of $L_{\mathrm r}$ is
$c(g,s)\lor k\cdot\mathrm{bool}(s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2)$, the second argument of
an opening whose first argument $3s\lor(a\defeq O(a;t,2))$ opens the matrix at
the witness.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $L_{\mathrm f}$ is $L_{\mathrm r}$ with this image written
$(c(g,s)\lor s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2)\land k$, and $L_{\mathrm c}$ is
$L_{\mathrm r}$ with it written ``$k$ if $c(g,s)\lor s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2$ else
$0$''.
\item For $P\in\{L_{\mathrm f},L_{\mathrm c}\}$, $P_{\mathrm m}$ is $P$ with the
floor initialized in the default of the opener as in $L_{\mathrm d}$
(\cref{cert:prop:terminal-rewrites}(a)) and the destructuring of the next record
replaced by the structural binding ``match the next record: case $[s,u,t]$ as
$r$: pass'' as in $L_{\mathrm m}$ (\cref{cert:prop:match}); and
$L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ is $L_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ with the declaration of the floor as a
module name moved from the head of the body into the case, in place of its empty
statement.
\item $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm c}$ and $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ are
$L_{\mathrm c}$ and $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ with the reading of the next line
replaced by the removal of the last element of one array parsed from the whole
input; in $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ the reader is bound by an assignment
expression in the argument of the root call, as in $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm m}$.
\item For $P\in\{T_{+,\mathrm p},S_{2,\mathrm p}\}$, whose calls destructure the
removed packet as $m,s,u,t$, $P^{\rho}$ is $P$ with the destructuring
$s,u,t,m\defeq r\defeq q()$ and with the arrays $[s,u,t]$ (the image of the
computation candidate) and $[s,u]$ (the inferred major) replaced by the prefixes
$r[{:}3]$ and $r[{:}2]$. For an array $\sigma$ let $\rho(\sigma)$ be $\sigma$
with every element other than the last that is an array of length $4$, say
$[m,s,u,t]$, replaced by $[s,u,t,m]$; $\rho$ is a bijection on arrays, and its
inverse rotates the other way.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proposition}[The returned floor as the image, and rotated packets]\label{cert:prop:returned-floor}
On inputs whose values are integers and arrays:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $L_{\mathrm c}$ has exactly the runs of $L_{\mathrm r}$; $L_{\mathrm f}$
accepts the same inputs as $L_{\mathrm r}$ and reads the same lines, with the same
floors, and its trees are those of $L_{\mathrm r}$ up to the replacement of
\texttt{False} by $0$.
\item Executed by CPython 3.12.14, $L_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ and $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$
accept the same inputs as $L_{\mathrm f}$, and $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ the same inputs
as $L_{\mathrm c}$, with the same lines read and the same calls, goals,
contexts and floors.
\item $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm c}$ accepts the array $[p_r,\dots,p_1,A]$ exactly
when $L_{\mathrm c}$ accepts the lines $A,p_1,\dots,p_r$, with the same run, and
likewise $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ and $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$.
\item For $P\in\{T_{+,\mathrm p},S_{2,\mathrm p}\}$, $P^{\rho}$ accepts
$\rho(\sigma)$ exactly when $P$ accepts $\sigma$, with the same run.
\end{enumerate}
Hence the programs of (a), (b) and (c) accept a certificate with a given
canonical closed goal exactly when $L_{\mathrm r}$ does, on the stream or on the
reversed array, and \cref{cert:prop:return-reservation} applies to them;
$T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$ accepts exactly the $1$-shifted canonical encodings of
the closed public theorems of $\mathcal U$ (\cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal});
and the statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold
for $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$, with the certificates of (c) rotated by $\rho$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) No call of the three programs has a return statement with a value, so every
returning call returns the null value. In each of the three images the major call
$c(g,s)$ is evaluated first and once, after the first argument of the opening; if
it fails, all three programs reject. If it returns, let $k_1$ be the floor at its
return. If $s$ is true-valued, none of the three evaluates $\lvert a\rvert$; the
image of $L_{\mathrm r}$ is $k_1\cdot\mathtt{True}=k_1$, that of $L_{\mathrm c}$
is $k_1$, and in $L_{\mathrm f}$ the disjunction has the value $s$, so the
conjunction has the value $k_1$. If $s$ is false-valued, all three evaluate
$\lvert a\rvert=2$ on the same opened matrix and fail together where it is
undefined; if it holds, all three images are $k_1$; otherwise the images of
$L_{\mathrm r}$ and $L_{\mathrm c}$ are $0$ and that of $L_{\mathrm f}$ is
\texttt{False}. The test reads no record, assigns nothing and leaves the floor
unchanged, so it does not matter that $L_{\mathrm r}$ reads $k$ before it and the
two other programs after it. All other operations are literally the same, so
$L_{\mathrm c}$ has the runs of $L_{\mathrm r}$. For $L_{\mathrm f}$ relate trees
up to the replacement of \texttt{False} by $0$, as in the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:tuple-stream}: the opener succeeds or fails on related arguments
together and gives related results, \texttt{False} and $0$ agreeing in every
comparison, truth value, product and failed traversal, and an image
\texttt{False} being retained as an in-range scalar exactly where $0$ is; the
value \texttt{False} enters only through the image of a new hypothesis, hence
only members of contexts, which are used only in tests of membership, and these
compare with \texttt{==}, under which related trees are equal. Induction on the
consumed records relates the runs call by call; the final decrement is the same.

(b) The initialization is that of \cref{cert:prop:terminal-rewrites}(a): the
default is evaluated once, when the definition of the opener is executed, before
any statement reads the floor. The structural binding is that of
\cref{cert:prop:match}(a): on an array of length $3$ it binds $s,u,t,r$ as the
destructuring does; otherwise the names stay unbound in the new frame and the
first test of the call, whose tuple is formed from $s$, fails before a further
record is read. A declaration of a module name is not executed: it fixes for the
whole body of the function that $k$ denotes the module name, wherever in the body
it stands, and it is legal there because no occurrence of $k$ precedes it in the
text. The empty statement performs nothing. Hence $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ and
$L_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ perform the same operations; the case in which the pattern
fails is the same in both.

(c) The argument of \cref{cert:prop:stream}, read in the other direction, as in
\cref{cert:prop:terminal-rewrites}(c) and \cref{cert:prop:match}(b); in
$L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ the reader is bound when the argument of the root
call is evaluated, before the body of the root call removes the goal.

(d) At every consumed element of length $4$ the destructuring of $P^{\rho}$
binds to $m,s,u,t$ the same values as that of $P$ at the corresponding element of
$\sigma$, and then $r[{:}3]$ and $r[{:}2]$ are new arrays with the elements of
$[s,u,t]$ and $[s,u]$, evaluated at the places of these arrays. No operation of
either program tests the identity of an array or changes an array in place, and
the image is traversed by the opener into new arrays, so the copies are not
observable. A consumed element of another length, which $\rho$ leaves unchanged,
or a scalar, fails the destructuring in both programs, and unused elements do not
affect a run. In $S_{2,\mathrm p}$ the test of the printed form removes the
characters of a fixed set from both ends of the printed whole input and requires
that nothing remain, that is, that every character belong to the set; $\rho$
permutes elements of arrays and does not change the set of characters of the
printed form, so the test agrees. Induction on the tree of calls in both
directions gives the claim. The consequences follow from
\cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}, \cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal} and
\cref{cert:prop:validated-devices}, with $\rho$ applied to the certificates.
\end{proof}

The programs are $L_{\mathrm f}$ ($239/261/549$;
\texttt{checkers/code/checker\_tokens239.py} of \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers}
and, byte for byte the same text, \texttt{code/token239.py} of the third delivery
of \pkg{checked\_presentation}), $L_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ ($246/257/589$,
\texttt{code/checker.py} of the third delivery), $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$
($245/256/580$, \texttt{checkers/code/checker\_ast256.py} of
\pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers}), and, of \pkg{prefix\_conditional},
$L_{\mathrm c}$ ($239/261/553$, \texttt{code/checker\_tokens239.py}),
$L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ ($246/257/593$, \texttt{code/checker\_ast257.py}),
$L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm c}$ ($244/269/547$, \texttt{code/checker\_tokens244.py}),
$L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ ($252/261/593$, \texttt{code/checker\_ast261.py}),
$T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$ ($267/298/461$, \texttt{code/checker\_bytes461.py}) and
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$ ($330/372/599$, \texttt{code/checker\_strict599.py}),
against $L_{\mathrm r}$ ($240/266/551$), $T_{+,\mathrm p}$ ($267/298/462$) and
$S_{2,\mathrm p}$ ($330/372/600$), reproduced with identical text. The stream
programs read the goal on the first line and one record per line and carry the
double dagger; the whole-input programs read the reversed array. $L_{\mathrm f}$ is
below $L_{\mathrm r}$ in all three coordinates, and $L_{\mathrm c}$ has one token
and five nodes fewer and two bytes more. The initialization in a default costs one
token and one byte and saves one node, and the structural binding with its empty
statement costs six tokens and $39$ bytes and saves three nodes; moving the
declaration into the case saves one token, one node and nine bytes more. The
rotation costs four bytes for the alias $r$ and saves four and one bytes at the two
prefixes. The three packages compare their stream programs for nodes with
$L_{\mathrm m}$ ($252/259/595$), which uses the allocation of $L$ and not the
reservation at return; $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$, $L_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ and
$L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ are below it in all three coordinates, a comparison of sizes and
not of runs. \pkg{prefix\_conditional} uses as the control of runs of
$L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm c}$ the same reframing of $L_{\mathrm r}$
(\texttt{controls/matched\_return\_whole245.py}), whose size it does not report.
The readable forms (\texttt{checkers/code/checker\_readable.py} and
\texttt{code/readable.py}) have no reported size, and their agreement with the
programs is supported by tests and not proved.

\begin{remark}[A repeated delivery of $L_{\mathrm c}$ and $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$]\label{cert:rem:conditional-image}
\pkg{conditional\_image} delivers, as \texttt{code/checker\_tokens239.py} and
\texttt{code/checker\_ast257.py}, the programs $L_{\mathrm c}$ and
$L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ of \pkg{prefix\_conditional} with identical text, against the
control $L_{\mathrm r}$ reproduced with identical text
(\texttt{reference/control\_tokens240.py}). Its proof is that of
\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(a),(b): no call returns a value, so a returning
major call yields the null value and leaves the floor $k_1$ that it has
reserved last; the residual test $s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2$ reads no record and
assigns nothing, so it does not matter that $L_{\mathrm r}$ reads the floor
before it and $L_{\mathrm c}$ after it, and both images are $k_1$ or the integer
$0$; the opening of the witness in the first argument of the opening precedes the
major call in both programs, so a witness is opened before the major can reserve
the name that it would capture. For $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ the default is evaluated
once, when the opener is defined, before the first record is read, and on a record
that is not an array of length $3$ the names $s,u,t$ stay unbound and the
membership test of the call fails, since it forms the computation candidate from
$s$ before a further record is read. The package also delivers a readable form
(\texttt{code/checker\_readable.py}) without a reported size, whose agreement with
the programs is supported by tests and not proved. It compares at the revision
\texttt{95d3dd192e}, in which \pkg{prefix\_conditional} and
\pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers} are incoming archives, and its census covers
the tree of artifacts but not these archives; its statements that the two
programs lower the minima of tokens and nodes and occur nowhere in the corpus hold
for that tree only. Under its own contract (the goal on the first line, one
three-field record per line, the reservation at return) $L_{\mathrm c}$ has the
token minimum $239$ of $L_{\mathrm f}$ with four bytes more, and $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$
one node more than $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ ($245/256/580$); neither changes an
envelope (\cref{cert:sec:envelope}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[A rejected mode test]\label{cert:rem:rejected-mode}
\pkg{prefix\_conditional} records that the mode test of the image may not be
replaced by a comparison such as $m\neq 3\bmod\lvert a\rvert$ in place of the
truth value of $m$ or of $3\bmod\lvert a\rvert$. The neutral major $[\,]$ is
false-valued, so the program takes the branch of an introduction and opens the
matrix at the witness; but the comparison is true for every arity of the matrix,
so the progressive image $k$ is supplied also for a compound matrix, and the
generated hypothesis is the progressive reading where the conditional reading is
required (\cref{cert:def:U}). No such program is delivered; the consequence that
the package states is listed in \cref{cert:sec:questions}.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{Arity erasure, and the byte-string extension on the conditional selection}

This part follows \pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement}, at a revision that contains
\pkg{checked\_presentations} and, as an incoming archive, \pkg{prefix\_conditional}.
In $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$, as in $S_2$, the opener reads the flag $v$ of the first
phase (\cref{cert:thm:fused}), and its arity guard is written $v\le p$, as in
\cref{cert:prop:order}, where $p$ is the condition
$\lvert a\rvert\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$ and $\le$ compares the integer $v$ with the
truth value of $p$ read as $0$ or $1$. Let $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ be
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$ with this guard written
$\lvert a\rvert^{v}\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$, every other character being unchanged;
here $0^{0}=1$, as in integer exponentiation. Let $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ be $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_2$ with the
in-place extension $g\mathrel{+}=[0]$ written
$g\mathrel{+}=\texttt{b'\textbackslash x00'}$, the device of
$S^{\mathrm T}_{1,\mathrm b}$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_{1,\mathrm b}$; this is the
composition with the conditional selection of \cref{cert:prop:checked-strict} that
\pkg{procedure\_presentation} does not deliver.

\begin{lemma}[Arity erasure]\label{cert:lem:arity-erasure}
For integers $n\ge 0$ and $d$ and for $v\in\{0,1\}$,
\[
v\le\bigl[n\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}\bigr]
\iff
n^{v}\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\},
\]
where $[P]$ is $1$ if $P$ holds and $0$ otherwise.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $v=1$ the left side holds exactly if $[P]=1$, that is, if
