$n\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$, and $n^{1}=n$, so both sides state the same membership.
For $v=0$ the left side holds, and $n^{0}=1$, also for $n=0$, belongs to the set.
No condition on $d$ is used: $d\bmod 2\in\{0,1\}$ for every integer $d$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Arity erasure and the byte-string extension]\label{cert:prop:arity-erasure}
On every input, $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ has exactly the runs of
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$, and $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ have exactly the runs of $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_2$: they accept or reject together, after reading the same packets,
with the same calls, goals, contexts and floors. Hence the statements (a), (b) and
(c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for the three programs, for
$S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ with the certificates of (c) rotated by $\rho$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Arity erasure. The flag is a local name of each call. The root call receives
$v=1$, every recursive call receives the default $0$, and the only assignment is
$v\defeq 0$ in the membership test, after the opening of the goal and before the
candidate is formed; no value of the input is assigned to it. The opener reads the
flag of the call in which it is defined, so $v\in\{0,1\}$ wherever the guard is
evaluated. The guard is evaluated only after the array test $a\cdot 0\neq 0$ has
succeeded. Both programs have the same reader and the same test of the printed
form, so an input that reaches the kernel consists of exact integers and arrays;
the values that reach the opener are then integers (Booleans among them, if the
program forms any), arrays, and the default image, a function, for which the array
test fails with an exception in both programs. For an integer or a Boolean $a$ the product $a\cdot 0$
equals $0$, so the guard is evaluated only for arrays, and $n=\lvert a\rvert$ is a
nonnegative integer. By \cref{cert:lem:arity-erasure} the two guards have the same
truth value; neither reads a record, assigns a name or fails, since they consist
of a length, a power with exponent $0$ or $1$, and a membership in a pair of
integers. Hence the two openers take the same branch on the same arguments, and by
induction on the opened tree they return the same result or fail together, also on
empty or noncanonical arrays and at negative depths. Every other operation is
literally the same, so induction on the tree of calls gives the same runs.

Byte-string extension. This is the argument of \cref{cert:prop:validated-devices}:
in $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_2$ the initial context is the empty list,
and every context passed to a call is either the list of the caller or a list
formed by concatenation, so every extension in place is the extension of a list.
Iterating the byte string yields the one integer $0$, so the extension appends $0$
to the same list object, which is also the list of the caller, exactly as the
extension by $[0]$ does; no operation inspects the byte string afterwards. The
empty context that marks the first phase is extended at the same point in both
programs. Induction on the tree of calls gives the same runs, including the
changes of a context seen through the alias that a caller retains.

The consequences follow from \cref{cert:prop:checked-strict} for
$S^{\mathrm T}_2$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_2$ and from \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(d)
for $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$, since equal runs give equal accepted inputs.
\end{proof}

The programs are $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ ($317/368/634$,
\texttt{code/checker\_strict\_tokens317.py}), $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$
($325/356/646$, \texttt{code/checker\_strict\_ast356.py}) and
$S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ ($328/372/597$, \texttt{code/checker\_strict597.py}),
against $S^{\mathrm T}_2$ ($319/370/630$), $S^{\mathrm N}_2$ ($327/358/642$) and
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$ ($330/372/599$), reproduced with identical text
(\texttt{controls/strict\_tokens.py}, \texttt{controls/strict\_ast.py},
\texttt{controls/checker\_strict599.py}); each differs from its control in exactly
the one statement or expression stated. The byte-string extension saves two tokens
and two nodes and costs four bytes, as on $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_1$; arity erasure saves two tokens and two bytes and leaves the
number of nodes unchanged. The certificates of $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ have the packet order $[m,s,u,t]$ of their controls,
and those of $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ the rotated order of
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$. The package states that the three programs improve the
envelope of T/ed$_1$ from $319/358/599$ to $317/356/597$ with three different
programs (\cref{cert:sec:envelope}), that no rule of the calculus is removed, and
that the compact envelope $239/256/461$ is not reached.

\subsubsection*{Eager opening on the validated programs, a singleton image, and three fields}

This part follows \pkg{checked\_reduction} and \pkg{validated\_three\_field}, both
at the revision \texttt{798856e4ef}, which contains \pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement}
as an incoming archive; neither package contains the other. The contract T/ed$_1$
restricts the accepted goals and the validation of the whole input
(\cref{cert:def:contracts}), through the statements (a), (b) and (c) of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}; it does not fix the certificates, and $S_2$ and
$S_3$ already use opposite markers. A program that accepts other certificates than
its control is therefore listed under T/ed$_1$ when it satisfies these three
statements. Let $E^{+}_k$ be the eager opener $E_k$ of \cref{cert:lem:eager} with
the retention $a\lor 1$ of $O^{+}_k$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm E}$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm E}$ are
$S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ with the fallback
``($d\bmod 2$ or the context is empty) and $O(t;\bot,3)$'' of the opener written as
the product $(d\bmod 2)\cdot O(t;\bot,3)$, and with the extension in place by the
byte string, which appends the integer $0$, replaced by the extension by $[[\,]]$,
which appends the empty array.
\item $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ is $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ without the flag
$v$: the arity guard $\lvert a\rvert^{v}\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$ is written ``the
context is nonempty or $\lvert a\rvert\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$'', the leaf guard
$v\le d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$ is written ``the context is nonempty or
$d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$'', the fallback ``$(d\bmod 2)\mathbin{|}v$ and
$O(t;\bot,3)$'' is written $(d\bmod 2)\cdot O(t;\bot,3)$, the first member
$(v\defeq 0)$ of the tuple of the terminal test is replaced by the appending of
$[\,]$ to the context, whose value is the null value, and the root call receives
no flag.
\item For a certificate $\sigma$ in the format of one of the controls,
$\nu(\sigma)$ is $\sigma$ with the major field of every consumed packet in which
it is the integer $0$ replaced by $[\,]$; no other field and not the goal is
changed.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proposition}[Eager opening with the empty neutral, validated]\label{cert:prop:eager-validated}
Let $P$ be $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm E}$, $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm E}$ or
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$, and $P_0$ its control $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$,
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ or $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The first opening of the goal in the root call of $P$ gives the result of
that of $P_0$ or fails with it. Hence $P$ accepts only inputs that satisfy $J$ and
whose goal satisfies $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}(a,0)$.
\item If $P$ accepts, the reading of the goal, in which the scalar $1$ reads as
$0$, is a closed theorem of $\mathcal A$.
\item Let $\sigma$ be a certificate that the compilation in the proof of (c) for
$P_0$ produces from a derivation in $\mathcal A$ (the packets of
\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(c) for $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$; those of $S_2$ in
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(c), rotated by $\rho$, for
$S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$). Then $P$ accepts $\nu(\sigma)$. Hence
statement (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} holds for $P$, with the
certificates translated by $\nu$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) In $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm E}$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm E}$ the root call
receives the empty list and extends it in place after its entry test; a major call
receives the list of its caller after that extension, and a final call a list
formed by concatenation with one hypothesis. In $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ the opening
of the goal is the left operand of the membership test and is evaluated before the
tuple, whose first member appends $[\,]$ to the list of the call; the candidate, the
images, the hypothesis and the new goal of the root call are opened afterwards, and
every other call receives that list or one formed from it by concatenation. So in
all three programs the context is empty exactly during the first opening of the
root call, and exactly there the context of $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ is empty and the flag of
$S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ is $1$
(\cref{cert:prop:empty-phase,cert:prop:arity-erasure}). During that opening the
guards of $P$ are those of the first phase of $P_0$; for $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ by
\cref{cert:lem:arity-erasure} with $v=1$, and because $1\le x$ holds exactly for
$x=1$ when $x=d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)\in\{0,1\}$. At the fallback both $P$ and $P_0$
evaluate the opening of the missing image, since the left factor of $P_0$ is
true-valued in the first phase; this opening fails, because the missing image is
the checking function, on which the array test $a\cdot 0\neq 0$ fails. So the two
openings take the same branches and give the same tree or fail together. The test
of the printed form is that of $P_0$, and (a) follows from (a) for $P_0$
(\cref{cert:prop:arity-erasure}).

(b) After the first opening of the root call the context of every opening is
nonempty, so the guards hold and the opener is $E^{+}_k$. The proof of
\cref{cert:lem:eager} applies verbatim to $E^{+}_k$ and $O^{+}_k$, with the retained
scalar $a\lor 1$ on both sides (\cref{cert:lem:plus-opening}): at an even depth the
eager product is $0$ or $[\,]$ and the lazy fallback $0$ or \texttt{False}, all of
which read $0=0$. Hence \cref{cert:lem:opening,cert:lem:exposure,cert:lem:fresh},
\cref{cert:lem:leaves,cert:lem:closed-leaf} and \cref{cert:lem:zeroinstance} hold
for $E^{+}_k$. The soundness proofs for $P_0$ (\cref{cert:prop:checked-strict}(b)
and \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(b), through \cref{cert:prop:arity-erasure})
are inductions on the returning call that use of the opener only these lemmas, of a
neutral resource only that it is closed and reads $0=0$, and of a major field
only its truth value and its comparison with the arrays $[s,u]$ and $[t,t,t]$. In
$P$ the neutral resource is $[\,]$, closed and reading $0=0$ at a formula position
(\cref{cert:def:reading}), discharged by \cref{cert:lem:neutral-resource}; copies
appended through a list shared with a major call add only this resource. The empty
array is false-valued, $3\cdot[\,]=[\,]$, the selectors of the floor treat it as
$0$, and it is unequal to every array of length $2$ or $3$, so a major field $[\,]$
takes the branches of the major field $0$. In $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ the null
value in the tuple is never equal to the opened goal, since an opening returns an
integer or an array. The induction therefore applies to the runs of $P$, with
$E^{+}$ in place of $O^{+}$.

(c) Relate a call of $P$ on $\nu(\sigma)$ and a call of $P_0$ on $\sigma$ if their
goals agree except that a neutral major $0$ of $P_0$ is $[\,]$ in $P$, their floors
agree, their remaining stacks correspond under $\nu$, and their contexts agree up to
copies of the neutral resource. In a compiled certificate a scalar of a canonical
formula occurs only at a term position, that is at an odd depth below the formula
root, and the openings of templates in the candidate of $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ keep
all scalars in range (last paragraph of the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(c)). An eager and a lazy opening take different
branches only at a scalar out of range at an even depth, and on canonical formulas
there is none. The exceptions in a compiled run are the openings $O(t;u,0)$, and in
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ also $O(t;s,0)$, of the comparison with the major at a
packet whose field $t$ is a canonical term (the witness of an introduction, the
update of a computation, the scalar $0$ of a \rulename{DE} packet), opened at the
formula root. There $P$ opens the image $u$ or $s$ at the depth $3$; in the compiled
packets these fields are $0$, $[\,]$, $2$ or closed canonical terms, whose openings
are $1$, $[\,]$, $2$ and the term itself (\cref{cert:lem:recoding}), so they are
defined. The results differ from those of $P_0$ only at even depths and enter only
the comparison with the major, which fails in both programs, since the major field
of such a packet is the neutral major or a unary array, or, in
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$, the discarded member of the pair. A call whose goal is the
neutral major returns by membership in both programs: in $P_0$ by the opening $0$ or
\texttt{False} of the scalar $0$, which equals the integer $0$ of its context or
tuple; in $P$ by the opening $[\,]$ of the empty array, which has been appended to
the context before the test. It consumes no packet in the first two programs and the
same dummy packet in the third. Opened goals of a compiled run are nonempty arrays
and differ from $0$, \texttt{False}, $[\,]$ and the null value, so no other membership
test changes. Related calls therefore consume the same packets and make related
major and final calls, and induction on the compiled derivation gives the claim.
\end{proof}

\Cref{cert:prop:eager-validated} is the composition of the eager opener and the empty
neutral of the program of $477$ bytes of \pkg{head\_conversion} with the validated
programs of \cref{cert:prop:arity-erasure}, and in $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ with the
phase by an empty context of \cref{cert:prop:empty-phase}. The accepted certificates
change in both directions: an image that a lazy opening skips is validated, and a
neutral major $0$ is rejected; the accepted goals do not change.

\begin{remark}[The phase cannot simply be dropped]\label{cert:rem:lazy-phase}
\pkg{checked\_reduction} records that deleting the condition on the phase from the
lazy fallback, leaving ``$d\bmod 2$ and $O(t;\bot,3)$'', does not validate the
grammar. In the goal $[[0]]$ the scalar lies at the depth $8$; the lazy fallback
returns $0$ there without consulting the missing image, so the first opening
succeeds on a goal outside $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$, and an introduction by cases
retains the inner array as a hypothesis and closes the final call by
\rulename{Hyp}. The reading $\D{x}{y}\D{x'}{y'}(0=0)$ of that goal is a theorem;
what fails is statement (a). With the product the missing image is opened at every
depth and the goal is rejected.
\end{remark}

Let $L''_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ be $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ with the image $[k]$ of the new goal,
in the final call, written as the set $\{k\}$ with the one element $k$.

\begin{proposition}[A singleton image]\label{cert:prop:singleton-image}
On inputs whose records are integers and arrays, $L''_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ has exactly
the runs of $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$: the same lines read, calls, goals, contexts and
floors.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The opener of both programs uses its image only by passing it on to the openings
of the children and, at a scalar out of range at an odd depth, by opening it with
the default image at the depth $3$. The image of the new goal is formed in the
second argument of the final call, after the first argument, which contains the
major call, and the opening of the new goal follows at once, with the current floor
$k_0\le 0$, which the opener does not change. The attempted traversal of the set
$\{k_0\}$ succeeds; its length is $1$, and its one element $k_0$ is opened at the
depth $3+5-1=7$, where the attempted traversal of the integer fails and the handler
retains it, since $k_0\le k_0<7$. The result is the array $[k_0]$, which the opening
of the array $[k_0]$ also gives, by the same steps. So the opening of the new goal
with either image takes the same branches and gives the same tree or fails at the
same point; the set is never returned, stored in a context or compared. Every other
operation is literally the same, and induction on the consumed records gives the
claim.
\end{proof}

The validated part of \pkg{validated\_three\_field} defines the following programs.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),start=4]
\item $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$ is $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ with three
changes: the missing image, the default of the opener, is the complex number $0j$
instead of the checking function; the extension by the byte string followed by the
destructuring $m,s,u,t\defeq q()$ is replaced by ``match the next record: case
$[m,s,u,t]$: extend the context by the byte string''; and the array
$s\land[s,u]\lor[t,t,t]$ of the comparison with the major is written ``$[s,u]$ if
$s$ else $[t,t,t]$''.
\item $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm o}$ is $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ with the last
member $H=[[s\land[s]\lor 1,u,t],\,O(t;r[{:}3],3)\text{ if }s\text{ else }u]$ of the
tuple of the terminal test replaced by $O^{+}_k(H';\bot,0)$, the opening of
$H'=[[s\land[s],u,t],\,O(t;r[{:}3],3)\text{ if }s\text{ else }u]$ at the depth $0$
without an image, as in $S_3$.
\item $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ is $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ with the missing
image $0j$ and, after the entry test and the extension of the context by the byte
string, the steps of the three-field protocol (\cref{cert:def:three-field}) on its
validated kernel: the call removes a record $[s,u,t]$; ($3'$) if
$O^{+}_k(t;u,0)=a$, it replaces $a$ by $O^{+}_k(t;s,0)$, then $s$ by $[s,u]$ if $s$
is true-valued and by $[t,t,t]$ otherwise, then $u$ by $0$; ($4'$) if $u$ is
true-valued, $h$ is the computation candidate
$[[s\land[s],u,t],\,O^{+}_k(t;[s,u,t],3)\text{ if }s\text{ else }u]$; otherwise the
call runs $\mathrm{Check}(s,g,k)$ and $h$ is $3\cdot s$ if $s$ is true-valued, and if
not, the goal $a=[B]$ is replaced by $O^{+}_k(B;t,2)$ and $h=a$; ($5'$) the floor is
decreased to $k-1$, and the final call is
$\mathrm{Check}(O^{+}_{k-1}(a;[k-1],0),\,g+[O^{+}_{k-1}(h;\iota,0)],\,k-1)$ with
$\iota=k-1$ if $s$ is true-valued or $\lvert a\rvert\bmod 2=u$, and $\iota=0$
otherwise. $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f}$ is the same program with the opener bound
by an assignment expression in the entry test, the body placed under the negated
test, and the reader of $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proposition}[Validated programs of \pkg{validated\_three\_field}]\label{cert:prop:validated-three}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Executed by CPython 3.12.14, $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$ has on every input
exactly the runs of $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$, up to the kind of a failure.
\item $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ and $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f}$ have the same
runs, and the statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}
hold for them, with the certificates of (c) consisting of the records
$[R(s),R(u),R(F)]$ for \rulename{Tr}, $[0,R(u),R(B')]$ for \rulename{QE} with matrix
$B'$, $[R(\D{x}{y}B'),0,0]$ for \rulename{DE}, $[0,R(a),R(h)]$ and $[R(n),R(a),R(h)]$
for \rulename{I0} and \rulename{IS}, and $[0,[\,],R(w)]$ and $[0,0,R(w)]$ for
\rulename{DI}-case and \rulename{DI}-progress.
\item The statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm o}$, with the certificates of (c) compiled as for
$S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ by the branch-aware compiler applied to its own
terminal test.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The missing image is consulted only at a fallback, in the first phase or at an odd
depth. There $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ open the
checking function and fail at the array test; with $0j$ the product $0j\cdot 0$ is
$0j$, which equals $0$, so the array test is false, and the range test compares an
integer with a complex number and fails. A defined opening never consults the missing
image, so defined openings agree, and no program of this subsection catches a failure.

(a) On an array of length $4$ the pattern binds $m,s,u,t$ as the destructuring does,
and the context is extended. The extension and the removal of the record act on
different lists, since the context is formed from the empty list by extensions and
concatenations and the record is removed from the parsed input, so their order is
not observable. On any other record the destructuring of the control fails; in the
measured runtime the pattern fails before it binds a capture name, as in
\cref{cert:prop:match}(a), and the next step evaluates the major call, whose
argument $m$ is unbound, so the program rejects before it reads a further record.
For a true-valued $s$ the conjunction $s\land[s,u]$ is the nonempty array $[s,u]$,
which the disjunction returns; for a false-valued $s$ both expressions give
$[t,t,t]$; neither has another effect. Every other operation is literally the same,
and induction on the tree of calls gives the claim.

(b) The two programs differ in layout only: the opener is defined before its first
use in both, the body of the negated test is the code that follows the early return,
and the reader parses the input, tests its printed form and binds the remover before
the root call in both. The first opening is that of $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ by
the remark on $0j$, which gives statement (a).

For statement (b), induct on the returning call as in \cref{cert:thm:three-sound}, for contexts
whose members are closed at the floor, with $O^{+}$ in place of $O$
(\cref{cert:lem:plus-opening}; \cref{cert:lem:zeroinstance} holds for $O^{+}$, as in
the proof of \cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(b)) and with the rules of $\mathcal A$. A
return at the entry is \rulename{Hyp}; a member of the context is a hypothesis or a
copy of the integer $0$, which reads $0=0$. If the test of ($3'$) succeeds, the
major $M^{\circ}$ is proved in the unchanged context at the floor $k$; for a
true-valued $s$ it reads $\sigma=\upsilon$, the new goal reads $F(\sigma)$ and $a$
reads $F(\upsilon)$ (\cref{cert:lem:opening}), and the step is \rulename{Tr}; for a
false-valued $s$ it reads $\Q{y}B(y)$, the new goal, opened at the image $0$, reads
$B(0)$, and the step is \rulename{QE}; the hypothesis $3\cdot M^{\circ}$ has arity
$6$ or $9$, reads $0=0$, and is discharged by \cref{cert:lem:neutral-resource}. If
the test fails and $u$ is true-valued, the opened hypothesis is a computation
candidate, whose zero instance is an instance of \rulename{I0} or \rulename{IS} for
both values of $\iota$ (\cref{cert:lem:zeroinstance}); the parameters named $k-1$ are
fresh for the context and the goal (\cref{cert:lem:fresh}), and
\cref{cert:lem:supply} discharges the hypothesis. If the test fails and $u$ is
false-valued, the major call proves $s$; for a unary $s=[B]$ the hypothesis
$3\cdot s$, opened at the fresh parameter, reads $\Q{y}B(b,y)$ and the step is
\rulename{DE}; at another arity or a scalar it reads $0=0$. If also $s$ is
false-valued, the matrix is opened at the witness at the floor $k$, before the
decrease, so the witness does not contain the new parameter; by $J$ the field $u$ is
$0$ or $[\,]$, so $\iota=k-1$ exactly when $u=0$ and the opened matrix has even
arity: at arity $2$ the matrix reads as a literal equation and the step is
\rulename{DI}-progress, at another even arity the matrix reads $0=0$ and the goal
$\D{x}{y}(0=0)$ is derivable; otherwise $\iota=0$ and the step is \rulename{DI}-case,
which $\mathcal A$ has for every matrix. The remaining raw cases of an introduction
(a goal $[\,]$, an even arity) are those of the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}(b). The goal of the root call satisfies
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$, and its reading is that of $B^{\sharp}$
(\cref{cert:lem:recoding}).

For statement (c), compile a derivation in $\mathcal A$ with the records stated, emitting the
records of the major before those of the continuation, as in
\cref{cert:thm:three-complete}. At \rulename{Tr} and \rulename{QE} the test succeeds:
$O^{+}_k(R(F);R(u),0)$ is $R(F(u))$ (\cref{cert:lem:recoding}), the goal; the new goal
is $R(F(s))$, or $R(B'(0))$, since the image $0$ opens to $1$; and $M^{\circ}$ is the
encoded major, proved by the records that follow. At \rulename{DE} the test compares
the opening \texttt{False} of the scalar $0$ at the depth $0$ with an array and
fails; $s$ is proved and exported with $\iota=k-1$. At an introduction and at a
computation the field $t$ is a canonical term, and the test is defined and fails by
\cref{cert:lem:separation}, whose proof holds for $O^{+}$: from the depth $0$ the
term is traversed at even depths, where a scalar is retained, as itself or as $1$, or
replaced by \texttt{False}, and the image is not consulted. At an introduction the
major call on $0$ returns at once, since the opening of $0$ is \texttt{False} and the
context contains $0$; the matrix is opened at the witness, and $\iota=k-1$ exactly for
the marker $0$ at an equation. At a computation the initializer $R(a)$ is
true-valued (\cref{cert:lem:truthy}), and the opening of the candidate at $k-1$ and at
$0$ is the same tree, $R$ of the instance: the counter $0$ becomes $1$, the other
scalars are canonical and retained, and the image is not consulted
(\cref{cert:lem:recoding,cert:lem:stable}). The run follows the compiled derivation;
canonical certificates satisfy $J$.

(c) The first opening is that of the control, which gives statement (a). For
statement (b), the induction of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(b) for $S_2$, carried over by
\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(d) and \cref{cert:prop:arity-erasure}, applies with the
terminal case of $S_3$: if the opened goal equals $O^{+}_k(H';\bot,0)$, this opening is
defined, so $H'$ is closed and has its reading (\cref{cert:lem:plus-opening}), and
\cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} with the counter $c=s\land[s]$ makes that reading an
instance of \rulename{I0} or \rulename{IS}. For statement (c), the last paragraph of
the proof of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(c) shows that the opening of $H'$ is defined on
every compiled packet, as for $S_3$; the dummy packet gives
$O^{+}_k([[0,1,1],1];\bot,0)=[[1,1,1],1]$. At a computation leaf the opening is $R$ of
the instance, the candidate of the control: the counter $0$ becomes $1$ and the
canonical fields are retained. The branch-aware compiler emits only the packet of a
call that the terminal test closes, and every other step is that of the control.
\end{proof}

The programs are, of \pkg{checked\_reduction}, $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm E}$
($315/365/618$, \texttt{native/code/checker\_tokens315.py}), $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm E}$
($323/353/630$, \texttt{native/code/checker\_ast353.py}), $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$
($324/367/589$, \texttt{native/code/checker\_bytes589.py}) and $L''_{\mathrm{f,m}}$
($245/255/580$, \texttt{compact/checker\_ast255.py}), against
$S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$, $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$,
$S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ and $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$, reproduced with identical
text; and, of \pkg{validated\_three\_field}, $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$
($306/362/668$, \texttt{code/checker\_tokens306.py}), $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f}$
($313/353/701$, \texttt{code/checker\_joint.py}), $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$
($330/348/681$, \texttt{code/checker\_ast348.py}) and $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm o}$
($329/372/595$, \texttt{code/checker\_bytes595.py}), against the three programs of
\pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement}, reproduced with identical text. Each eager program
is below its control in all three coordinates. In the syntax tree an array display
has a context child and a set display has none, so $L''_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ saves exactly
one node; it reads one record per line and carries the double dagger. The three
changes of $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$ save eight nodes together. The packages also
deliver readable forms (\texttt{native/code/readable\_checker.py}, $368/442/2075$, of
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$; \texttt{code/readable\_three\_field\_strict.py},
$363/422/1953$, of the three-field protocol), whose agreement with the programs is
supported by tests and not proved, and untrusted translators of certificates
(\texttt{native/translate.py} for $\nu$, \texttt{tools/compile\_three\_field.py}),
which the proofs do not use. $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ tests the endpoint before the
floor is decreased, whereas $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ compares after the decrease;
\pkg{validated\_three\_field} claims the equality of the accepted goals and not a
simulation of the four-field program on arbitrary stacks. Both packages state that no
rule of the calculus is removed, that no program attains two of the new minima, and
that the compact envelope ($239/256/461$, or $239/255/461$ with $L''_{\mathrm{f,m}}$)
is not reached.

\subsubsection*{A loop for the continuation, eager three-field programs, a parity dispatcher, and the floor as the phase}

This part follows \pkg{304\_token\_refinement}, \pkg{neutral\_modes},
\pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots} and \pkg{scope\_phase}, all four at the revision
\texttt{7a7633980d}, which contains \pkg{checked\_reduction} and
\pkg{validated\_three\_field} as incoming archives; none of the four contains
another, and \pkg{neutral\_modes} and \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots} deliver
three programs with identical text. The following programs are defined.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),start=7]
\item $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$ is $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ in which a
call is a loop: as long as the opening of the goal is not a member of the context,
the body of the call is executed, and its last step, the final call, is replaced by
the rebinding of the local name of the context to the new list
$g+[O^{+}_{k-1}(h;\iota,0)]$, followed by the rebinding of the goal to
$O^{+}_{k-1}(a;[k-1],0)$; when the opening of the goal is a member of the context,
the call returns.
\item $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$, $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$
and $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm{m,E}}$ are $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$,
$S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f}$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$ with the two changes
of (i): the fallback ``($d\bmod 2$ or the context is empty) and $O(t;\bot,3)$''
written as the product $(d\bmod 2)\cdot O(t;\bot,3)$, and the extension of the
context by the byte string replaced by the extension by $[[\,]]$.
\item $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$ is $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ with the change of (v):
the last member $H$ of the tuple of the terminal test replaced by
$E^{+}_k(H';\bot,0)$, with $H'$ as in (v).
\item $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ is $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$ with the factor
$\mathrm{any}((m,3\bmod\lvert a\rvert))$ of the new floor written
$\lvert m\lor a[1{:}]\rvert\bmod 2$, where $a[1{:}]$ is the array $a$ without its
first element.
\end{enumerate}
For a stack $\sigma$ of three-field records let $\nu_3(\sigma)$ be $\sigma$ with the
field $s$ of every consumed record in which $s$ is the integer $0$ and $u$ is
false-valued replaced by $[\,]$; in the certificates of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-three}(b) these are exactly the records $[0,[\,],R(w)]$ and
$[0,0,R(w)]$ of the introductions.

\begin{proposition}[The continuation as a loop]\label{cert:prop:loop-continuation}
On every input, $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$ and $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$
read the same records in the same order and accept or reject together; an iteration
of a call of $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$ and the corresponding call of
$S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ begin with the same goal, the same floor and equal
contexts. Hence the statements (a), (b) and (c) of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$, with
the certificates of $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ in (c).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
In both programs the opener is an anonymous function formed at the entry of a call;
it reads the context of the call only through its truth value, in the guards of the
first phase and in the fallback, and the floor through its value, and in
$S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$ it reads both names of its call at the time of
each evaluation. A final call of $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ is a new call with the
context $g'=g+[O^{+}_{k-1}(h;\iota,0)]$, the floor $k-1$ and the goal
$O^{+}_{k-1}(a;[k-1],0)$, the goal being opened in the caller, whose context is still
$g$; the next iteration of $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$ has the same three
values, its goal being opened after the rebinding to $g'$. Both $g$ and $g'$ are
nonempty, since $g$ has been extended in place before the record was read, so the
two openings take the same branches and give the same tree or fail together. The
rebinding creates a new list, as the concatenation of the control does; the
extension in place at the next iteration acts on that list, as the extension at the
entry of the final call does; and the major call receives the list of the current
iteration, as that of the control receives the list of its call, so its extensions
in place act on corresponding objects. Every returning call of either program
returns the null value, which the expression of the hypothesis uses only through its
truth value. Induction on the consumed records, applied to the major calls and to
the iterations, relates the runs in both directions: a return of the control at its
entry test corresponds to the end of the loop, and a failure to a failure at the
same operation.
\end{proof}

The rebinding of the context must create a new list: an extension of $g$ in place
by the hypothesis would make it visible to the caller of a major call, and the proof
does not cover it. Removing the final calls removes frames, so the physical
recursion limits differ.

\begin{proposition}[Eager opening and the opened candidate on further validated programs]\label{cert:prop:eager-three}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$ has exactly the runs of
$S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$. Executed by CPython 3.12.14,
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm{m,E}}$ has on every input exactly the runs of
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm E}$, up to the kind of a failure.
\item The statements (a) and (b) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for
$S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$, and $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$
accepts $\nu_3(\sigma)$ for every certificate $\sigma$ of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-three}(b); hence statement (c) holds with these
certificates.
\item The statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold
for $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$, with the certificates of $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ in
(c).
\end{enumerate}
Hence the three statements hold for the four programs, for
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm{m,E}}$ with the certificates of
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm E}$ (\cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) The first statement is the argument of the first paragraph of the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-three}(b): the two programs differ in layout only.
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm{m,E}}$ is $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm E}$ with the three
changes of (iv). The proof of \cref{cert:prop:validated-three}(a) uses of the
opener only that the missing image is consulted at a fallback and nowhere else, and
that there the sentinel $0j$ fails as the checking function does; with the eager
fallback the missing image is opened at every fallback, and its opening fails, since
$0j\cdot 0$ equals $0$, so the array test is false, and the range test compares an
integer with a complex number. The structural binding and the conditional major do
not involve the opener or the neutral resource, so that proof applies.

(b) As in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}(a), the context is empty
exactly during the first opening of the root call, which precedes the extension of
the context, and there both fallbacks evaluate the opening of the missing image $0j$,
which fails; so the first opening is that of $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$, which gives
statement (a) (\cref{cert:prop:validated-three}(b)). For statement (b), the
induction of \cref{cert:prop:validated-three}(b) applies with $E^{+}$ in place of
$O^{+}$, whose lemmas hold by the proof of \cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}(b). The
members of a context are hypotheses and copies of $[\,]$, which is closed and reads
$0=0$. A false-valued field $s$ is $0$ or $[\,]$ on inputs that satisfy $J$, and both
values are treated alike: the replacement of $s$ by $[t,t,t]$, the false-valued
threefold repetition, which selects the introduction, the selector of the floor, and
the counter $s$ of the computation candidate, which reads $0$
(\cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf}); a major call on $[\,]$ has a goal that reads $0=0$.

For $\nu_3(\sigma)$, relate a call of $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$ on
$\nu_3(\sigma)$ and a call of $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ on $\sigma$ if their goals
agree, except that a goal $0$ of the second is $[\,]$ in the first, their floors agree,
their remaining stacks correspond under $\nu_3$, and their contexts agree up to the
copies of $0$ and $[\,]$. The goals of a compiled run are canonical encodings with
uniform zero, on which $E^{+}$ and $O^{+}$ agree (they contain no scalar at a position
of formula sort, and every scalar at a term position is in range), or the neutral
major of an introduction, which returns at the entry test in both programs: the
opening of $0$ is \texttt{False}, equal to the member $0$ of the context of the control,
and the opening of $[\,]$ is $[\,]$, appended to the context of the new program before
the major call. Neither consumes a record. At the records of \rulename{Tr} and
\rulename{QE} the replacement test opens canonical templates and succeeds in both
programs. At a record of \rulename{DE} it compares $E^{+}_k(0;0,0)=0$, respectively
$O^{+}_k(0;0,0)=\texttt{False}$, with an array and fails. At the records of an
introduction and of a computation the field $t$ is a canonical term traversed from
the depth $0$ at even depths; an out-of-range scalar there now evaluates the image
$u$, which is $0$, $[\,]$ or a closed canonical term, so the opening is defined, and
its result, like that of the control, contains no binary array and differs from the
canonical goal (\cref{cert:lem:separation}). After the test the dispatch is the same;
at an introduction the false-valued source $[\,]$ takes the branches of $0$. The
computation candidate is opened at the floor $k-1$ with either image; its scalars lie
at term positions, all in range, so the two openers give the same tree
(\cref{cert:lem:stable}). The openings of the witness and of the new goal concern
canonical trees. Induction on the compiled derivation gives the claim.

(c) The first opening is that of $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$, which gives statement (a)
(\cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}). For statement (b), the induction of
\cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}(b) applies with the terminal case of the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-three}(c): if the opened goal equals $E^{+}_k(H';\bot,0)$,
this opening is defined, so $H'$ is closed and has its reading
(\cref{cert:lem:eager,cert:lem:plus-opening}), and \cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} with
the counter $s\land[s]$ makes that reading an instance of \rulename{I0} or
\rulename{IS}. For statement (c), on every record of a certificate of
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ the fields of a canonical record occur in $H'$ at a depth not
below the root term depth, or, for a template or a matrix, at least $3$ above the
formula root; so all scalars of $H'$ are in range, the eager opener does not consult
its missing image, and the opening is defined (last paragraph of the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(c)). The dummy record $[0,1,1,[\,]]$ gives
$E^{+}_k([[0,1,1],1];\bot,0)=[[1,1,1],1]$, and at a computation leaf the opening is
$R$ of the instance, the candidate of $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$: the counter $0$
becomes $1$ and the canonical fields are retained. Every other step is that of
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the packages state the compositions with a summary proof; the reduction of
% S^N_{2,m,E} to S^N_{2,E}, the relation of runs under nu_3 and the eager form of the
% proof for the opened candidate are supplied here from the proofs cited.

\begin{proposition}[A parity dispatcher for the neutral resources]\label{cert:prop:parity-dispatch}
Consider inputs of integers and arrays whose last element is $[A,0]$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If a call of $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ with goal $a$, context $g$ and floor $k$
returns, and the members of $g$ are closed at floor $k$ in the $1$-shifted reading,
then the auxiliary reading of $a$ is derivable in $\Teq$ from the auxiliary readings
of the members of $g$. If $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ accepts an input whose goal $A$ is
the $1$-shifted canonical encoding of a closed public formula, that formula is a
theorem of $\mathcal U$.
\item On every certificate obtained from those of \cref{cert:prop:offset}(b) by the
insertion of dummy records in the proof of \cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal} and by the
rotation $\rho$, the programs $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ and $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$
consume the same records, make the same calls with the same goals and floors, and
accept; their contexts differ at most in members of arity $6$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ accepts exactly the $1$-shifted canonical encodings of
the closed public theorems of $\mathcal U$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
A call of $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ removes a record $[s,u,t,m]$, returns if
$E_k(a;\bot,2)$ is a member of $g$ or equals the candidate $H$ of
\cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal}, and otherwise makes the major call on $m$ with the
context $g$ at the floor $k$, forms $h=3m$ if $m$ is true-valued and otherwise
replaces the goal $[B]$ by $E_k(B;t,4)$ and lets $h$ be this matrix, decreases the
floor to $b=k-1$, opens $h$ at the depth $2$ with the image $b$ if the factor
$\lvert m\lor a[1{:}]\rvert\bmod 2$ is $1$ and $0$ otherwise, and makes the final call
on the opening at $[b]$ of $E_b(t;s,2)$ if $(m,a)=(M^{\circ},E_b(t;u,2))$, with
$M^{\circ}=[s,u]$ for a true-valued $s$ and $[t,t,t]$ otherwise, and of $a$ otherwise.

(a) By induction on the returning call, with the $1$-shifted forms of the lemmas
(\cref{cert:lem:offset}). The opening $E_k(a;\bot,2)$ is defined, so it is $a$, and
$a$ is closed at $k$ (\cref{cert:lem:eager}). If $a$ is a member of $g$, the step is
\rulename{Hyp}; if $a=H$, \cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf}, whose proof uses only
\cref{cert:lem:leaves,cert:lem:dummy-erasure}, makes its reading an instance of
\rulename{I0} or \rulename{IS}. Otherwise the major call returns; its goal $m$ is
closed at $k$, and by the induction hypothesis its auxiliary reading is derivable
from $g$. The name $b$ is fresh for the readings of the members of $g$, of $a$ and of
$m$, which are closed at $k$ (\cref{cert:lem:fresh}); the hypothesis and the new goal
are openings at the floor $b$, hence closed there, so the induction hypothesis
applies to the final call, and it remains to derive the reading of $a$.
If $m$ is true-valued and $(m,a)=(M^{\circ},E_b(t;u,2))$, then $m$ has arity $2$ or
$3$, its threefold repetition has arity $6$ or $9$ and reads $0=0$ whatever the
image, and is discharged by \cref{cert:lem:neutral-resource}; $m$ reads $\sigma=\upsilon$,
$a$ reads $F(\upsilon)$ and the new goal $F(\sigma)$, with $F$ the reading of $t$ at the
depth $2$, or $m$ reads $\Q{y}B(y)$, $a$ reads $B(\upsilon)$ and the new goal $B(0)$
(\cref{cert:lem:exposure}), and transport in $\Teq$ or \rulename{QE} concludes. If
$m$ is true-valued and the test fails, the new goal has the reading of $a$. For a
unary $m=[B]$ the factor is $1$, the hypothesis $[B,B,B]$ opened at $b$ reads
$\Q{y}B(b,y)$, and the elimination of $\D{x}{y}B$ through the auxiliary reading
(proof of \cref{cert:prop:scalar}) concludes; for an array $m$ of arity at least $2$
the hypothesis has arity at least $6$ and reads $0=0$, whatever its image; for a
scalar $m$ the length is undefined and the call does not return. If $m$ is
false-valued, it is $0$ or $[\,]$, the test fails since $M^{\circ}$ is a nonempty
array, and the factor is $(\lvert B'\rvert-1)\bmod 2$ for the opened matrix $B'$. The
goal is $[\,]$, which reads $0=0$, or $[B]$; for a scalar goal or one of arity at least
$2$ the unpacking fails, and for a scalar $B$ the slice. The opened matrix $B'$ has
the arity of $B$ and reads $B(\tau,y)$ with the witness $\tau$ validated at $k$. At
arity $2$ it is a literal equation, the hypothesis reads $B(\tau,b)$ and the new goal
$B(\tau,\Suc b)$, and with $b$ fresh for $g$, $\tau$ and $B$ the progressive reading
of $\D{x}{y}B$ follows; at arity $1$ or $3$ the hypothesis reads $B(\tau,0)$, and the
ordinary reading follows by coverage, as for the introduction of $\mathcal U$ at a
compound matrix; at arity $0$ or at least $4$, $B$ reads $0=0$ and the goal
$\D{x}{y}(0=0)$ is derivable under either reading. These are all cases. The second
statement follows as in \cref{cert:prop:offset}(a).

(b) Every major field of such a certificate is the scalar $0$ or a canonical
encoding of arity $1$, $2$ or $3$, and every goal of the run is $0$ or a canonical
encoding of arity $1$, $2$ or $3$ (\cref{cert:prop:offset}(b)). The two factors agree
except at a major of arity $2$: at arity $1$ and $3$ both are $1$; at the major $0$
the opened matrix $B'$ of a canonical goal has arity $1$, $2$ or $3$, and
$3\bmod\lvert B'\rvert$ is $0$, $1$, $0$, as is $(\lvert B'\rvert-1)\bmod 2$. At a
major of arity $2$ the hypothesis is the threefold repetition of the major, of arity
$6$, opened at the image $0$ in place of $b$. Both openings are defined, since an
eager opening whose image is a scalar retained at the depth $5$ is defined on every
tree of integers and arrays, and both results have arity $6$. No goal of the run has
arity $6$, so these members decide no membership test, and the candidate does not
depend on the context. The comparison and the new goal are the same in both
programs. Induction on the consumed records gives the claim, and the last statement
follows from (a), (b) and \cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal}.
\end{proof}

The accepted stacks differ in both directions. A true-valued scalar major, which the
control can pass, makes $\lvert m\rvert$ undefined. The stack
\[
\bigl[\,[0,[0],0,0],\ Z,\ Z,\ Z,\ [0,0,0,[[\,]]],\ [[[0,[0],0],[0]],0]\,\bigr],
\qquad Z=[0,0,0,0],
\]
whose goal is the canonical encoding of $\It{0}{\Suc 0}{u}{0}=\Suc 0$, is accepted
by $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ and rejected by $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$: the major
$[[\,]]$, which reads $\D{x}{y}(0=0)$, is introduced with the matrix $[\,]$, at which the
control evaluates $3\bmod 0$ and fails, while the new factor is $0$; the root goal is
then closed by its computation record $[0,[0],0,0]$. The control accepts a shorter
certificate of the same goal, by the computation record alone. \pkg{neutral\_modes}
states the case analysis of (a) as a principle: a dispatcher that gives the fresh
image to a unary major and to an equation matrix, and the image $0$ to a matrix of
arity $1$ or $3$, is sound whatever it gives elsewhere, since every other generated
hypothesis reads $0=0$ or the goal reads $\D{x}{y}(0=0)$. The length of the slice
$a[1{:}]$ is used here modulo $2$; its truth value, the test of
\cref{cert:rem:examined-variants}, would grant progress at a matrix of arity $3$.

\begin{definition}[Validated programs with the floor as the phase]\label{cert:def:floor-phase}
The programs $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$ and $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ of \pkg{scope\_phase} read
three-field records, close computations as leaves with the candidate of
\cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf}, test the endpoint before the major call, and keep a
global floor $k$, initially $0$, read by one opener $O^{+}_k$ with the missing image
$0j$. Their input is read and tested as in $S^{\mathrm T}_2$, and a call
$\mathrm{Check}(a,g)$ performs the following steps.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(F\arabic*)]
\item Remove a record $r=[s,u,t]$; the destructuring fails unless it has three
entries.
\item Open the goal, $a'\defeq O^{+}_k(a;\bot,0)$, with the guards of the first phase
exactly when the phase is marked: in $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$ by a global flag $v$,
initially $1$, with the guards of $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ (the arity guard
$\lvert a\rvert^{v}\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$, the leaf guard $v\le d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$
and the fallback ``$(d\bmod 2)\mathbin{|}v$ and $O(t;\bot,3)$''); in
$S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ by the condition $k=0$, with the guards of
$S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$ in which ``the context is empty'' is replaced by
``$k=0$''.
\item Evaluate the marker $\mu$: in $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$ the assignment $v\defeq 0$,
with the value $0$; in $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ the assignment $k\defeq k-1$, the
\emph{reservation at entry}, with the new floor as value.
\item Return if $a'$ is a member of the tuple of $\mu$, the members of $g$, and the
candidate $H\defeq[[[s],u,t],\,O^{+}_k(t;r,3)]$ if $s$ is true-valued and
$H\defeq[[1,u,t],u]$ otherwise, formed at the current floor.
\item Otherwise, if $O^{+}_k(t;u,0)=a$, replace $a$ by $O^{+}_k(t;s,0)$, and then $s$
by $[s,u]$ if $s$ is true-valued and by $[t,t,t]$ otherwise.
\item Let $h\defeq 3s$ if $s$ is true-valued; otherwise replace the goal $a=[B]$ by
$O^{+}_k(B;t,2)$ and let $h\defeq a$.
\item Make the major call $\mathrm{Check}(s,g)$, and let $\beta$ be the floor at its
return.
\item Let $\iota\defeq\beta$ if $s$ is true-valued or ($\lvert a\rvert=2$ and $u=2$),
and $\iota\defeq\texttt{False}$ otherwise, and make the final call
$\mathrm{Check}\bigl(g+[O^{+}_{\beta}(h;\iota,0)],\,O^{+}_{\beta}(a;[\beta],0)\bigr)$.
\item In $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$ only, decrease $k$ by $1$ when the call returns, at (F4)
or after (F8): the reservation at return of \cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}.
\end{enumerate}
The initial context of $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$ is empty. That of $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ is a
byte string whose only element is the integer $0$; every later context is a list
$[{*}g,x]$, which contains that $0$. $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ is $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$
with the destructuring of (F1) written as the structural binding of a record of
three entries, whose case contains the declaration of the global name $k$, with $k$
initialized in a default of the opener, and with the reader bound by an assignment
expression in the argument of the root call. $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ is the four-field
program of the same kind: it removes a record $r=[s,u,t,m]$, opens with $E^{+}_k$
whose missing image is the checking function, has the initial context $[[\,]]$,
reserves at entry as $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$, forms the candidate with $r[{:}3]$ in place
of $r$, has no step (F5), lets $h\defeq 3m$ if $m$ is true-valued and otherwise opens
the matrix as in (F6), makes the major call on $m$, takes $\iota\defeq\beta$ if $m$
is true-valued or ($\lvert a\rvert=2$ and $u=2$), and makes the final call on the new
goal $E^{+}_{\beta}(a^{*};[\beta],0)$, where $a^{*}\defeq E^{+}_{\beta}(t;s,0)$ if
$(m,a)=(M^{\circ},E^{+}_{\beta}(t;u,0))$, with $M^{\circ}$ as in (F5), and
$a^{*}\defeq a$ otherwise; its endpoint test is made after the major call, at the
floor $\beta$, as in $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$.
\end{definition}

The hypothesis to be opened, in an introduction the matrix opened at the witness, is
formed before the major call in all four programs, as in
\cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}; the floor is $0$ only before the first
reservation, so in $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ it marks the first
phase, and no flag and no empty context is needed.

\begin{lemma}[Floors of the global schedule]\label{cert:lem:floor-phase}
In the four programs of \cref{cert:def:floor-phase} the floor never increases. If a
call entered at the floor $K$ returns, the floor at its return is at most $K-1$. In
$S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ the major call
of a call entered at $K$ is entered at $K-1$ and returns at most at $K-2$, and the
marker $\mu=K-1$ is not equal to a defined opening $a'$ of the goal at the floor $K$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The floor changes only by the decrements of (F3) or (F9). In the programs with
reservation at entry every call decreases it once before it makes a further call, and
in $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$ every returning call decreases it once at its return. A defined
opening $a'$ is an array, a scalar retained by the range test, hence at least $K$ or
the scalar $1$, or a fallback at an even depth, \texttt{False}, $0$ or $[\,]$; none of
these equals the negative integer $K-1<K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Validated programs with the floor as the phase]\label{cert:thm:floor-phase}
The statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for
$S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ and
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$, with the certificates of (c) consisting of the records
$[R(s),R(u),R(F)]$ for \rulename{Tr}, $[0,R(u),R(B')]$ for \rulename{QE},
$[R(\D{x}{y}B'),0,0]$ for \rulename{DE}, $[0,R(a),R(h)]$ and $[R(n),R(a),R(h)]$ for the
leaves of \rulename{I0} and \rulename{IS}, $[0,2,R(w)]$ and $[0,0,R(w)]$ for
\rulename{DI}-progress and \rulename{DI}-case, and $[0,0,0]$ at a hypothesis leaf and
at a neutral major, with every negative name renamed as in the proof; for
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ the records are $[R(s),R(u),R(F),[R(s),R(u)]]$,
$[0,R(u),R(B'),[R(B'),R(B'),R(B')]]$, $[0,0,0,R(\D{x}{y}B')]$,
$[0,R(a),R(h),[\,]]$, $[R(n),R(a),R(h),[\,]]$, $[0,2,R(w),[\,]]$, $[0,0,R(w),[\,]]$
and $[0,0,0,[\,]]$, in the same order of the rules.
Moreover, executed by CPython 3.12.14, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ has exactly the runs of
$S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ on inputs that pass the test of the printed form.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
(a) A record is removed in (F1) without an opening, and the floor, or the flag, marks
the first phase exactly during the opening (F2) of the root call
(\cref{cert:lem:floor-phase}). During that opening the guards are those of the first
phase of $S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$, $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ or
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$, and the fallback evaluates the opening of the missing image,
which fails for $0j$ as in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:validated-three} and for the
checking function at the array test. So the first opening is that of those programs,
and statement (a) follows from \cref{cert:prop:arity-erasure,cert:prop:eager-validated}.

(b) We prove by induction on the returning call: if a call entered at the floor $K$
returns, and the members of $g$ are closed at $K$, then the reading of $a$ is
derivable in $\mathcal A$ from the readings of the members of $g$. Let $\alpha$ be the
floor after (F3): $K-1$ with reservation at entry, $K$ in $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$. The
opening $a'$ is closed at $K$ and has the reading of $a$
(\cref{cert:lem:plus-opening,cert:lem:eager}).

If the call returns at (F4): $a'=\mu$ is excluded by \cref{cert:lem:floor-phase} or
reads $0=0$ in $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$; if $a'$ is a member of $g$, the step is
\rulename{Hyp} (a member $0$ or $[\,]$ reads $0=0$); if $a'=H$, then $H$ is closed at
$K$, hence at $\alpha$, and was formed with the opener at $\alpha$, so
\cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} makes its reading an instance of \rulename{I0} or
\rulename{IS}.

Otherwise the major call is entered at $\alpha$ with the context $g$, whose members
are closed at $\alpha$ (\cref{cert:lem:fresh}); by the induction hypothesis the
reading of its goal is derivable from $g$, and its goal is closed at $\alpha$, since
its opening succeeded. It returns at $\beta<\alpha$
(\cref{cert:lem:floor-phase}). The hypothesis and the new goal are openings at
$\beta$, closed there, so the induction hypothesis applies to the final call, and it
remains to justify the step.
\begin{itemize}
\item Three fields, test (F5) succeeded: the major is $M^{\circ}$; for a true-valued
$s$ it reads $\sigma=\upsilon$, the old goal, closed at $K$, reads $F(\upsilon)$ and
the source instance, opened at $\alpha$ and closed there, reads $F(\sigma)$, where $F$
is the reading of $t$ at the depth $0$ and floor $\alpha$; for a false-valued $s$ it
reads $\Q{y}B(y)$, the old goal $B(\upsilon)$ and the new goal $B(0)$, since the image
$s$ opens to $1$ or $[\,]$ (\cref{cert:lem:exposure}). The hypothesis $3M^{\circ}$ has
arity $6$ or $9$ and reads $0=0$ (\cref{cert:lem:neutral-resource}). The new goal has
no hole, so its opening at $[\beta]$ keeps its reading. The step is \rulename{Tr} or
\rulename{QE}; the template $F$ may contain the parameter $\alpha$, on which
\rulename{Tr} places no condition.
\item Four fields: the hypothesis is formed at $\alpha$ before the major call and the
endpoint test is made at $\beta$. If $(m,a)=(M^{\circ},E^{+}_{\beta}(t;u,0))$, then $m$
has arity $2$ or $3$ and was proved, $3m$ reads $0=0$, and the previous case applies
with the template $F$ read at the floor $\beta$, which may contain parameters in
$[\beta,\alpha)$; the major $m$, closed at $\alpha$, fixes the readings of $s$ and
$u$, or of $t$ below its slot, and the old goal, closed at $K$, is $F(\upsilon)$ or
$B(\upsilon)$. A unary major and a false-valued major fail the test, since
$M^{\circ}$ has arity $2$ or $3$.
\item The test fails or was not taken, and the source $s$ (the major $m$) is
true-valued: the new goal has the reading of $a$. For a unary major $[B]$ the
hypothesis $[B,B,B]$, opened at $\beta$, reads $\Q{y}B(\beta,y)$; $\beta$ is fresh for
$g$ and $a$, closed at $K$, and for the major, closed at $\alpha$; the step is
\rulename{DE}. For an array of arity at least $2$ the hypothesis has arity at least $6$
and reads $0=0$, and for a scalar it is a scalar at a position of formula sort, which
reads $0=0$.
\item The source or the major is false-valued: the goal is $[\,]$, which reads $0=0$,
or $[B]$ (for another goal the unpacking of (F6) fails), and the matrix $B'$ was opened
at the witness $\tau$ at the floor $\alpha$, before the major call, so $\tau$ is closed
at $\alpha$ and $\beta<\alpha$ is fresh for $\tau$, for $g$ and for $B$. If $B'$ has
arity $2$ and $u=2$, it reads a literal equation, the hypothesis $B(\tau,\beta)$ and
the new goal $B(\tau,\Suc\beta)$, and the step is \rulename{DI}-progress. Otherwise the
image \texttt{False} is retained as the scalar $1$ at the depth $3$ and reads $0$, the
hypothesis is $B(\tau,0)$, and the step is \rulename{DI}-case, which $\mathcal A$ has
for every matrix; a matrix of arity $0$ or at least $4$ reads $0=0$, and
$\D{x}{y}(0=0)$ is derivable.
\end{itemize}
With $E^{+}$ the lemmas hold by \cref{cert:lem:eager}, as in the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}(b), and the neutral resource $[\,]$ is closed and reads
$0=0$. At the root call the context is $[0]$, $[\,]$ or $[[\,]]$, whose members read
$0=0$, and the goal is closed at the floor $0$, so its reading is a closed theorem of
$\mathcal A$.

(c) Compile a derivation in $\mathcal A$ with the records stated, emitting the records
of the major before those of the continuation, with the branch-aware treatment of
leaves of \cref{cert:prop:leaf}(b): a call that the terminal test closes receives only
its record. Keep, as in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}(b), an
injective map from the eigenparameters of the derivation to negative names: where the
derivation introduces an eigenparameter after the major of an elimination or an
introduction, the program uses the floor $\beta$ left by the major call, and the map
is extended by that name; the names in its range before the extension are at least
the floor at the entry of the current call, which exceeds $\beta$. The names reserved
at entry or at return that are not in the range of the map occur in no record. Every
record mentions only names in the range of the map, which are at least the current
floor, so every opening of a canonical field is defined, and an injective renaming
commutes with the openings and preserves equality and membership. The test (F5)
succeeds at \rulename{Tr} and \rulename{QE}, where the opening of the canonical template
at the encoded target is the goal (\cref{cert:lem:recoding}), and the source instance
is the new goal (the image $0$ opens to $1$); it fails at \rulename{DE}, where the
opening of the scalar $0$ at the formula root is \texttt{False} or $0$, and at an
introduction, by \cref{cert:lem:separation} (the lazy opener does not consult the
image $2$ or $0$; the eager opener of $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ consults it at even depths,
where it is retained). A computation call returns at (F4), since $H$ is $R$ of the
instance: the counter is $1$ for \rulename{I0} and $[R(n)]$ for \rulename{IS}, and the
opening of $R(h)$ at the image $r$ or $r[{:}3]$ is the right side
(\cref{cert:lem:recoding}). A hypothesis leaf returns by membership after removing
$[0,0,0]$, whose candidate $[[1,0,0],0]$ is formed without an opening; a neutral major
$0$ returns by membership of $0$, its opening being $0$ or \texttt{False}, and in
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ the neutral major $[\,]$ returns by membership of $[\,]$. The marker
$2$ selects progress exactly at an equation matrix, and $0$ selects cases. In
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ the endpoint test at $\beta$ compares canonical trees, which the
lower floor does not change. The candidate of every canonical record is formed
without failure (last paragraph of the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}(c)). The run follows the compiled derivation.

The last statement: on a record of three entries the structural binding binds as the
destructuring does, and on another record the names stay unbound and the next step,
which forms the candidate from $s$ after the opening and the reservation, fails before
a further record is read (\cref{cert:prop:match}(a)); the declaration of a global name
in a case is not executed and fixes the meaning of $k$ for the whole body
(\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(b)); the default is evaluated once, when the opener
is defined, before the first record is read (\cref{cert:prop:terminal-rewrites}(a));
and the reader is bound before the body of the root call removes a record, as in
$L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ (\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(c)).
\end{proof}
% ed.: the package proves the theorem by a short induction and a reference to the
% inherited compiler; the case analysis of (b), the four-field case with the endpoint
% test at the lower floor, and the renaming of (c) are supplied here from its proof
% notes and from the proofs cited.

\begin{remark}[The order of the witness and the major]\label{cert:rem:floor-order}
The witness must be opened before the major call, which reserves the eigenparameter.
\pkg{scope\_phase} delivers the two programs that differ from $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$
and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ only in that the major call precedes the opening of the
witness (\texttt{diagnostics/UNSOUND\_three\_field.py},
\texttt{UNSOUND\_four\_field.py}), and stacks with the goal
$\D{x}{y}\bigl(\sel(y,0,x)=\sel(y,0,y)\bigr)$, encoded
\texttt{[[[5,1,7],[5,1,5]]]}, which is false since its instances at $y=\Suc 0$ and
$y=\Suc\Suc 0$ force different values of $x$. At the root the floor becomes $-1$; the
neutral major reserves $-2$, and the witness $\Suc(-2)$, opened afterwards, is admitted
and equals the successor of the eigenparameter $-2$, so the premise of progress becomes
the reflexive equation $\sel(\Suc(-2),0,\Suc(-2))=\sel(\Suc(-2),0,\Suc(-2))$, which the
remaining records prove. According to the package's replay, both variants accept and
the delivered programs reject: opened at the floor $-1$, the witness consults the
missing image. The test $\lvert a\rvert=2=u$ is needed for the reason of
\cref{cert:rem:marked-mode}: with direct leaves a true-valued $u$ reaches the
introduction. No sizes of the variants are reported.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Exact image of the canonical encoder]\label{cert:prop:exact-image}
For a tuple $\varsigma$ of slots, innermost first, each marked genuine or dummy, let a
raw tree satisfy $T(\varsigma)$ if it is the exact integer $1$; or an exact odd integer
$3+2j$ with $j$ less than the length of $\varsigma$ and the slot $j$ genuine; or $[b]$
with $b$ in $T$ of $\varsigma$ extended by two dummy slots; or $[n,b,h]$ with $n,b$ in
$T$ of $\varsigma$ extended by one dummy slot and $h$ in $T$ of $\varsigma$ extended by
one genuine slot. Let it satisfy $F(\varsigma)$ if it is $[B]$ with $B$ in $F$ of
$\varsigma$ extended by two genuine slots, or $[s,t]$ with $s,t$ in $T(\varsigma)$. Then
a raw tree satisfies $F(\,)$ exactly when it is $B^{\sharp}$ for a closed formula $B$
of the language with equality and $D$. Let $S^{\mathrm x}$ be
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$ preceded, after the test of the printed form, by the test
that the input is a nonempty array whose last element satisfies $F(\,)$. Then
$S^{\mathrm x}$ accepts an input exactly when it satisfies $J$, its goal is $B^{\sharp}$
for a closed formula $B$, and $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$ accepts it; its accepted goals
are exactly the encodings $B^{\sharp}$ of the closed theorems of $\mathcal A$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By induction on the tree, as in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:shifted-validated}. In
$B^{\sharp}$ the term $0$ is the scalar $1$, a closed formula has no parameter, and a
bound variable is written $3+2j$ with its slot counted through the dummy slots of
\cref{cert:def:reading}: two below a successor, one below the counter and the
initializer of an iterator; the state of an iterator and the two variables of $D$ are
genuine slots. The encoding never refers to a dummy slot, uses the arities $1$ and $3$
at term positions and $1$ and $2$ at formula positions, and every tree of these shapes
decodes to a formula whose encoding it is. The tests of exact integers exclude the
Booleans. The test precedes the kernel and fails on every other goal; on $B^{\sharp}$,
which satisfies $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$, the run is that of $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$,
whose statements (a), (b) and (c) are \cref{cert:prop:eager-three}(c).
\end{proof}

$S^{\mathrm x}$ rejects the goals \texttt{[[3],[1]]} and \texttt{[[5],[1]]}, which
satisfy $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$ and read $\Suc 0=\Suc 0$ through a dummy slot;
\cref{cert:cor:second-dummy} shows that the programs of T/ed$_1$ accept no certificate
for the second. Under its contract $S^{\mathrm x}$ is complete for every goal that it
admits.

The programs are, of \pkg{304\_token\_refinement}, $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$
($304/361/705$, \texttt{code/checker304.py}) against $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$
reproduced with identical text (\texttt{benchmarks/strict\_tokens306.py}); of
\pkg{neutral\_modes}, and with identical text of \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots},
$S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$ ($304/359/652$,
\texttt{code/checker\_tokens304.py}), $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$
($311/350/685$, \texttt{code/checker\_joint311.py}) and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm{m,E}}$ ($328/345/665$, \texttt{code/checker\_ast345.py}),
against $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$, $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f}$ and
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$; of \pkg{neutral\_modes}, $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$
($266/299/460$, \texttt{code/checker\_bytes460.py}) against $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$
(\texttt{controls/checker\_bytes461.py}); of \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots},
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$ ($325/367/587$, \texttt{code/checker\_bytes587.py}) against
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ and $S^{\mathrm x}$ ($581/641/1424$,
\texttt{code/checker\_exact.py}); and, of \pkg{scope\_phase}, $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$
($302/352/646$, \texttt{code/checker\_tokens302.py}), $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$
($304/349/646$, \texttt{code/checker\_entry\_tokens304.py}), $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$
($314/340/681$, \texttt{code/checker\_ast340.py}) and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$
($311/362/584$, \texttt{code/checker\_bytes584.py}), which the package compares, as
programs of other protocols, with $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$, $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$
and $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$. The controls are reproduced with identical text. The loop
saves two tokens and one node and costs $37$ bytes; each eager composition saves two
tokens, three nodes and $16$ bytes, as in (i); the opened candidate saves two bytes and
costs one token; the parity dispatcher saves one token and one byte and costs one
node. $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ is below $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ in all three
coordinates. The readable form of $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$
(\texttt{code/readable\_compact.py}) has no reported size; its agreement with the
program is supported by tests and not proved. Each package states that no rule is
removed, that no single program is below all coordinates of an envelope, and that the
compact envelope is not reached in all coordinates.

\subsubsection*{Phase tests by order, exact validators, actual binding and other input interfaces}

This part follows \pkg{validated\_phase}, the checker refinement of
\pkg{shared\_witness\_bundles}, \pkg{exact\_bindings}, \pkg{exact\_masks},
\pkg{intrinsic\_validation}, the checker part of \pkg{names\_and\_scopes} and
\pkg{nontraditional\_inputs}. All but \pkg{intrinsic\_validation} and
\pkg{names\_and\_scopes} start from the revision \texttt{e88ebf229b}, which contains the
four packages of the previous part as incoming archives; those two start from
\texttt{fc05b10f0e}, which also contains
\pkg{exact\_bindings}, \pkg{exact\_masks} and \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs}. The mathematical
result of \pkg{shared\_witness\_bundles}, a common witness for two closures that recovers
one-term transport from variable-source substitution and computation at the root, is
\cref{disch:sec:bundle}; the naming calculus of \pkg{names\_and\_scopes} is
\cref{wit:sec:ns}. The following programs are defined.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),start=11]
\item The \emph{phase tests by order} $\pi$ rewrite the two guards of the first phase of
a program with a global floor $k$: the leaf guard ``$k$ or $d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$''
becomes $k<d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$, and the arity guard ``$k$ or
$\lvert a\rvert\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$'' becomes $k\mathbin{|}(\lvert a\rvert\in
\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\})$, the Boolean being read as $0$ or $1$; $\pi_1$ rewrites the leaf
guard only.
\item $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ and $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$ are $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ and
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ with the fallback ``($d\bmod 2$ or $k=0$) and $O(t;\bot,3)$''
written as the product $(d\bmod 2)\cdot O(t;\bot,3)$, with the initial context, the byte
string whose element is $0$, replaced by $[[\,]]$, and with $\pi$.
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi}$ and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi_1}$ are $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$
with $\pi$ and with $\pi_1$.
\end{enumerate}
For a certificate $\sigma$ of $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ compiled as in
\cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}(c), let $\nu_{\mathrm e}(\sigma)$ be $\sigma$ with the field
$s=0$ of the records $[0,2,R(w)]$ and $[0,0,R(w)]$ of the introductions replaced by
$[\,]$; no other field is changed.

\begin{lemma}[Phase tests by order]\label{cert:lem:phase-guards}
Let $k\le 0$ and $d$ be integers.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item For every integer or Boolean $a$, the value $x\defeq d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$ is
$0$ or $1$, and ``$k$ or $x$'' is true-valued exactly when $k<x$.
\item For $b\in\{0,1\}$, ``$k$ or $b$'' is true-valued exactly when
$k\mathbin{|}b\neq 0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The remainder modulo $2$ is $0$ or $1$ also for negative operands, and the bitwise
conjunction of an integer with $0$ or $1$ is $0$ or $1$, also for negative $d$. If $k=0$,
both sides of (a) hold exactly when $x=1$ and both sides of (b) exactly when $b=1$. If
$k<0$, both sides of (a) hold, and $k\mathbin{|}b$ is negative, hence nonzero.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Phase tests by order on the validated programs]\label{cert:prop:phase-guards}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item On every input, $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi}$ and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi_1}$ have
exactly the runs of $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$.
\item The statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for
$S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$, with the certificates $\nu_{\mathrm e}(\sigma)$ in (c). Executed by
CPython 3.12.14, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$ has exactly the runs of $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ on
inputs that pass the test of the printed form; so the three statements hold for it with
the same certificates.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) The floor is initially $0$ and changes only by decrements, so it is a nonpositive
integer at every evaluation of a guard. An input with a value other than an exact
integer or array is rejected by the test of the printed form before any guard is
evaluated (\cref{cert:thm:repr}). Depths are formed from $0$, $2$ and $3$ by integer
arithmetic, so $d$ is an integer. The leaf guard is evaluated only after $k\le a<d$ has
succeeded, so $a$ is an integer or the internal \texttt{False}; the arity guard only
after the array test $a\cdot 0\neq 0$ has succeeded, so $a$ is an array and its length
is defined; the missing image, the checking function, fails at the array test, before
either guard. Hence the operands of \cref{cert:lem:phase-guards} are defined, each
rewritten guard selects the branch of the original, and the evaluations that the
original short-circuits are defined and have no effect. Every other operation is
literally the same, and induction on the run gives the claim.

(b) The same argument shows that $\pi$ does not change the runs of $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ or
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ with the eager fallback, whose floors obey
\cref{cert:lem:floor-phase} and whose missing image $0j$ fails at the range test (proof of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-three}); so it suffices to treat the eager fallback and the
neutral resource $[\,]$. By \cref{cert:lem:floor-phase} the floor is $0$ exactly during
the first opening of the root call. There the lazy fallback of $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$
consults the missing image, since $k=0$, and so does the product; the opening of $0j$
fails. So the first openings coincide, and statement (a) is that of
\cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}.

For statement (b), the induction of the proof of \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}(b) applies
with $E^{+}$ in place of $O^{+}$, whose lemmas hold by the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}(b). The members of a context are hypotheses and copies
of $[\,]$, which is closed and reads $0=0$ (\cref{cert:lem:neutral-resource}). A
false-valued field $s$ is $0$ or $[\,]$ on inputs that satisfy $J$, and both values are
treated alike, as in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:eager-three}(b).

For statement (c), relate a call of $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ on $\nu_{\mathrm e}(\sigma)$ and a
call of $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ on $\sigma$ if their goals agree, except that a neutral major
$0$ of the second is $[\,]$ in the first, their floors agree, their remaining stacks
correspond under $\nu_{\mathrm e}$, and their contexts agree up to the initial member,
$0$ in the second and $[\,]$ in the first. A neutral major consumes the dummy record
$[0,0,0]$ in both programs, whose candidate $[[1,0,0],0]$ is formed without an opening,
and returns at the entry test: the lazy opening of $0$ at the depth $0$ and a negative
floor is \texttt{False}, equal to the member $0$, and the eager opening of $[\,]$ is
$[\,]$, a member of every context, since contexts are formed from $[[\,]]$ by
concatenation. At the record of an introduction the field $s=[\,]$ is false-valued and
$3\cdot[\,]=[\,]$, so the matrix is opened at the witness, and the image of the
hypothesis is the returned floor under the same condition $\lvert a\rvert=2=u$. The
replacement test opens the canonical witness at the formula root with the image $u\in
\{0,2\}$; the eager opening consults $u$, whose opening at the depth $3$ is $1$ or $2$,
so it is defined, and the test fails in both programs by \cref{cert:lem:separation}.
At every other record the fields are those of $\sigma$; canonical trees have no scalar
at a position of formula sort and all their scalars at term positions are in range, so
the eager and the lazy opener give the same trees (\cref{cert:lem:stable}). Related
calls therefore consume the same records and make related calls, and induction on the
compiled derivation gives the claim.

The last statement is the last statement of \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}: the two
programs differ in the structural binding of the record, the declaration of the global
name in a case, the initialization of the floor in a default of the opener and the
binding of the reader in the root call, and in nothing that $\pi$ or the eager fallback
touches.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the package proves (a) as stated and proves (b) by a reference to the inherited
% lemmas and an untrusted translator; the induction of (b) and the relation of runs under
% nu_e are supplied here from the proofs cited.

The translation $\nu_{\mathrm e}$ must not replace every field $s=0$: at a record of
\rulename{QE} the scalar $0$ is the numerical image of the new goal. \pkg{validated\_phase}
records that a translator replacing every such field fails on five positive fixtures.
It also records, without a delivered program, that $\pi$ applied to $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$
alone gives $312/340/677$. \pkg{shared\_witness\_bundles} delivers
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi_1}$ with the proof of (a) for $\pi_1$.

\paragraph{Actual binding.} \pkg{exact\_bindings} removes the dummy slots from the
encoding instead of testing them.

\begin{definition}[Actual-binding reading and encoding]\label{cert:def:actual}
The \emph{actual-binding reading} is the reading of \cref{cert:def:reading} in which the
child of a unary term array is read below the same slots as the array, the children $n$
and $a$ of a ternary term array below the same slots, and the child $h$ below one
additional slot, the state; positions of formula sort are read as before. A child depth
function $\delta$ is \emph{admissible} if $\delta(d,r,j)=d+5-r$ for even $d$, and for
odd $d$, $\delta(d,1,0)=\delta(d,3,0)=\delta(d,3,1)=d$, $\delta(d,3,2)=d+2$, and
$\delta(d,r,j)-d$ depends only on $r$ and $j$ otherwise. Examples are the increments
$2[j=2]$ and $j\mathbin{\&}2$ at odd depths. The opener $O^{\natural}$ is that of
\cref{cert:def:opener}, with the child $j$ of an array of arity $r$ at the depth $d$
opened at $\delta(d,r,j)$; $O^{\natural+}$ and $E^{\natural}$ are obtained in the same way
from $O^{+}$ and the eager opener. The \emph{actual-binding encoding} $B^{\natural}$ of a
closed formula of the language with equality and $D$ is that of \cref{cert:def:enc},
with $0$ written as the scalar $1$ and the slots counted through genuine binders only.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[The actual-binding codec]\label{cert:lem:actual}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item \Cref{cert:lem:opening,cert:lem:exposure,cert:lem:canonical,cert:lem:fresh,cert:lem:plus-opening,cert:lem:eager,cert:lem:recoding,cert:lem:separation,cert:lem:closed-leaf}
hold for the actual-binding reading, the openers of \cref{cert:def:actual} with any
admissible $\delta$, and the encoding $B^{\natural}$.
\item At the floor $0$ with the guards of the first phase of $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$,
$S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ or $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ and a missing image
whose opening fails, the opening at the depth $0$ of a tree that satisfies $J$ is defined
exactly when the tree is $B^{\natural}$ for a closed formula $B$ of the language with
equality and $D$, and then returns the tree.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
