(a) The proofs of these lemmas are inductions on the tree that use the child depths in
two ways only: every child that has a reading is opened at the depth of its position,
and the depth of a child increases by $2$ when that of its parent does, which yields the
exposure of the outermost slot. The first holds by admissibility and the definition of
the reading, the second since $\delta(d+2,r,j)=\delta(d,r,j)+2$; annotations have no
reading, so their depths do not matter. The proof of \cref{cert:lem:separation}
traverses canonical terms from the depth $0$ at even depths, where $\delta$ is unchanged.
In \cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} the dummy slots disappear: in
$H=[[c,u,t],O(t;[s,u,t],3)]$, read at the formula root, the trees $c$ and $u$ lie at the
term depth $3$, the child $s$ of $c=[s]$ also at the depth $3$, and $t$ at the depth $5$
below the state; in the image $[s,u,t]$, opened at the depth $3$, the trees $s,u,t$ lie
at the same depths. So the image reads as $\It{\sigma}{\upsilon}{u}{\eta}$ with the
readings of the same raw trees, and the two cases of the proof of \cref{cert:lem:leaves}
apply without \cref{cert:lem:dummy-erasure}.

(b) By induction on the tree, as in \cref{cert:lem:scope}: the arity guard admits the
arities $1$ and $2$ at even depths and $1$ and $3$ at odd depths; the leaf guard at the
term depth $3+2p$ admits the odd scalars in $[0,3+2p)$, which are $1$ and the codes
$3+2j$ of the $p$ genuine slots; every other scalar consults the missing image and fails;
and the children are opened at the depths of their positions in $B^{\natural}$. Every tree
of this form decodes to a closed formula whose encoding it is, since no slot is a dummy
slot; the scalars admitted are retained, so the opening returns the tree.
\end{proof}

For $S\in\{S^{3}_{\mathrm r},S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}},S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}\}$ let $S^{\natural}$
be $S$ with the child depths of the increment $2[j=2]$ at odd depths
(\pkg{exact\_bindings}), and $S^{\natural\prime}$ with the increment $j\mathbin{\&}2$
(\pkg{names\_and\_scopes}); let $S^{\rho\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm e}$ be
$S^{\rho\natural\prime}_{\mathrm e}$ with the choice of the increment written as the pair
$(5-\lvert a\rvert,\,j\mathbin{\&}2)$ indexed by $d\bmod 2$.

\begin{proposition}[Actual binding]\label{cert:prop:actual}
Each of the seven programs $S^{\natural}$, $S^{\natural\prime}$ and
$S^{\rho\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm e}$ accepts only inputs that satisfy $J$ and whose
goal is $B^{\natural}$ for a closed formula $B$ of the language with equality and $D$; if
it accepts, $B$ is a theorem of $\mathcal A$; and for every closed theorem $B$ of
$\mathcal A$ it accepts a certificate with goal $B^{\natural}$, compiled as in
\cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}(c) with the encoding $B^{\natural}$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The test of the printed form gives $J$ (\cref{cert:thm:repr}) and
\cref{cert:lem:actual}(b) the goal. The proof of \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}(b) and (c)
uses the encoding only through the lemmas of \cref{cert:lem:actual}(a), and the
recoding $R$ is now the encoding $B^{\natural}$ with $0$ written as $1$; so it applies.
In $S^{\rho\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm e}$ both entries of the pair are defined
integers wherever the pair is formed (on an array, with an integer index), and
$d\bmod 2\in\{0,1\}$, so the pair has the value of the conditional, and the program has
exactly the runs of $S^{\rho\natural\prime}_{\mathrm e}$.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the packages give the reading and the transfer in outline; the reduction of each
% lemma to the two properties of the child depths, and the treatment of the computation
% candidate without dummy slots, are written out here.

The actual-binding encoding is a new encoding of the public goals: certificates of the
earlier programs must be recompiled, and the indices of their variables change. The
accepted goals are exactly the encodings of the closed theorems, as for
$S^{\mathrm x}$ of \cref{cert:prop:exact-image}, but in another encoding.

\begin{proposition}[Intrinsic validation]\label{cert:prop:intrinsic}
Let $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ be $S^{\rho\natural\prime}_{\mathrm e}$ without the test of
the printed form, with the reader removing records from the parsed input directly, the
array test $a\cdot 0=[\,]$, the range test $k\le(a\mathbin{|}0)<d$, the counter
$[s]$ if $s$ is true-valued and $1$ otherwise, and the candidate $r[{:}2]$ if $s$ is
true-valued and $[t,t,t]$ otherwise.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ accepts a JSON document, its last element is a tree of
integers, Booleans and arrays that becomes $B^{\natural}$ for a closed formula $B$ when
every \texttt{true} is replaced by $1$, and $B$ is a theorem of $\mathcal A$.
\item For every closed theorem $B$ of $\mathcal A$, $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ accepts the
certificates of $S^{\rho\natural\prime}_{\mathrm e}$ of \cref{cert:prop:actual} with goal
$B^{\natural}$, and also these certificates with any leaf $1$ of the goal written
\texttt{true}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Among the values of the JSON reader, the product with $0$ is the empty array exactly for
arrays (a string gives a string, a number or Boolean a number, and the other values
fail), and $a\mathbin{|}0$ is defined exactly for integers and Booleans. Every leaf of a
tree whose opening is defined reaches the range test, so such a tree consists of integers,
Booleans and arrays; images are opened before they are inserted; hence every opening
result is such a tree. On these trees every operation of the program treats a Boolean
as its integer (comparison, arithmetic, bitwise operations, truth value, equality,
membership), except that the retained scalar $a\lor 1$ is \texttt{true} itself, which
equals $1$; readings are taken with Booleans as $0$ and $1$ (\cref{cert:def:raw}).

(a) At the floor $0$, \texttt{true} passes the leaf guard as $1$ and \texttt{false} fails
it, since $0$ is even; so by \cref{cert:lem:actual}(b) the first opening succeeds exactly on
the stated goals. For soundness, run the induction of \cref{cert:prop:actual}, with one
addition: every raw value on which an inference depends is opened, or is compared for
equality with an opened tree, or is used only as a truth value or as the mode. The major
$m$ is the goal of the major call, opened there before that call returns. The field $t$
is opened in the replacement test, which every nonterminal call evaluates. The fields
$s$ and $u$ are images of openings of $t$; the eager opener consults an image at every
out-of-range scalar, so they are opened whenever the hole occurs in $t$. If the hole does
not occur, the openings of $t$ at $s$ and at $u$ are the same tree, so the new goal equals
the old goal, the generated hypothesis is the threefold repetition of the major, which
reads $0=0$, and the step is derivable whatever $s$ and $u$ are. In the candidate $H$ the
raw $s$ and $u$ occur unopened; $H$ decides the entry test only by equality with the
opened goal, which forces every unopened leaf of $H$ to be numerically equal to an integer
leaf of that goal (a string, an object or the null value equals no integer or array, a
floating-point number may equal an integer), and the reading of a numeric tree depends
only on the values of its scalars, so \cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} applies to $H$; a
false-valued $s$ of any type gives the counter $1$. The mode enters only through
$\lvert a\rvert=2=u$, and progress is taken only at a binary matrix, a literal
equation. A record that is not an array is either rejected at its first use or, if it
is a four-key object whose first key is the empty string, used only to form a candidate
of strings, which matches no opened goal, at a call that returns by membership. With
these facts every case of the induction is as before.

(b) On inputs of integers and arrays the program and $S^{\rho\natural\prime}_{\mathrm e}$
differ only in the spelling of operations that have the same values there (the test of
the printed form passes, the two array tests agree, $a\mathbin{|}0=a$, and $[s]$ and
$r[{:}2]$ are nonempty when $s$ is true-valued). So the runs on these certificates are
the same. A goal leaf \texttt{true} in place of $1$ behaves as $1$ in every operation and
remains \texttt{true} in the opened trees, which compare equal to the trees with $1$; so
every branch is taken as before, and the run accepts.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the package states the argument for unused and equality-matched values in prose;
% the case of a hole-free template, the unopened fields of the candidate and the
% non-array records are worked out here.

\pkg{intrinsic\_validation} first compared the increment $j\mathbin{\&}2$ with $2[j=2]$ on
the compiled certificates, where every array traversed at an odd depth has arity at most
$3$; with \cref{cert:def:actual} the comparison is not needed. The accepted inputs are
not those of a typed contract: unused records may hold arbitrary JSON values, and the
goal may spell zero as \texttt{true}. The accepted closed theorems are the same.

\paragraph{Exact validation in the padded encoding.} \pkg{exact\_bindings} and
\pkg{exact\_masks} keep the encoding of \cref{cert:def:enc} and test the dummy slots
during the first opening. For a tuple $\varsigma$ of slots, innermost first, each genuine
or dummy, let
\[
F(\varsigma)\defeq\sum_{\varsigma_j\ \text{dummy}}2^{j+1},\qquad
M(\varsigma)\defeq 1+\sum_{\varsigma_j\ \text{genuine}}2^{j+1},\qquad
F_{\infty}(\varsigma)\defeq F(\varsigma)-2^{\lvert\varsigma\rvert+1}.
\]
The \emph{signed state} is $-M$ at a position of formula sort and $M$ at a term position.

\begin{lemma}[Masks of slots]\label{cert:lem:masks}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Extending $\varsigma$ by two genuine slots, two dummy slots, one dummy slot and one
genuine slot maps $F$ to $4F$, $4F+6$, $2F+2$, $2F$, the same for $F_{\infty}$, and $M$ to
$4M+3$, $4M-3$, $2M-1$, $2M+1$. Hence the signed state of the child of an array of arity
$r$ with index $j$ is $4m-3$ for $r=1$, $-m$ for $r=2$ at a formula, and $2m-1+2[j=2]$ for
$r=3$ at a term; and $F$ of that child is
$\bigl(F\ll((r+1)\bmod 3)\bigr)+(d\bmod 2)\bigl(\lfloor 6/r\rfloor-2\lfloor j/2\rfloor\bigr)$
for the arities and parities admitted by the arity guard.
\item Let $d=3+2\lvert\varsigma\rvert$ and $0\le a<d$ an integer. Each of
$d\mathbin{\&}a\mathbin{\&}(\mathord{\sim}F\gg\lfloor a/2\rfloor)\mathbin{\&}1$ and
$d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)\mathbin{\&}(M\gg\lfloor a/2\rfloor)$ is nonzero exactly when
$a=1$ or $a=3+2j$ with $\varsigma_j$ genuine. At an even $d$ both are $0$.
\item For every integer $a\ge 0$ and odd $d$,
$d\mathbin{\&}a\mathbin{\&}(\mathord{\sim}F_{\infty}\gg\lfloor a/2\rfloor)\mathbin{\&}1$ is
nonzero exactly when $a=1$ or $a=3+2j$ with $j<\lvert\varsigma\rvert$ and $\varsigma_j$
genuine; for $a<0$ the shift fails.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(a) Prepending $q$ slots multiplies the old part by $2^{q}$; two dummy slots add $2+4$,
one adds $2$, genuine slots add nothing to $F$ and the same amounts to $M-1$; and
$F_{\infty}$ gains the factor $2^{q}$ in its last term as well. At $r=1$ the shift is $2$
and the increment $6$ exactly at a term; at $r=2$ (a formula) neither; at $r=3$ (a term)
the shift is $1$ and the increment $2,2,0$ for $j=0,1,2$. The signed state changes sign
exactly at equality, the change of sort. (b) Both expressions require $d$ and $a$ odd,
$a=1+2r'$; bit $r'$ of $\mathord{\sim}F$ and of $M$ is set exactly when $r'=0$ or slot
$r'-1$ is genuine, and $a<d$ gives $r'\le\lvert\varsigma\rvert$. (c) The bits of
$F_{\infty}$ up to $\lvert\varsigma\rvert$ are those of $F$, and all higher bits are set,
so the bound $a<d$ is supplied by the mask; a negative shift count fails.
\end{proof}

The programs are the following. $X^{\mathrm s}_{3\mathrm r}$, $X^{\mathrm s}_{3\mathrm m}$
and $X^{\mathrm s}_{\rho}$ (\pkg{exact\_bindings}) are $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$,
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ with a keyword-only argument $m$ of the
opener, initially $-1$, passed to the children as in \cref{cert:lem:masks}(a), and with the
leaf guard of the first phase extended by the second test of (b) (in
$X^{\mathrm s}_{3\mathrm r}$ the shift count is multiplied by the flag $v$).
$X^{\mathrm f}_{\rho}$ (\pkg{exact\_masks}) is $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ with a keyword-only
argument $f$, initially $0$, passed to the children as $k$ if $k\neq 0$ and otherwise by
the expression of (a), and with the leaf guard of the first phase extended by the first
test of (b); $X^{\mathrm f}_{\rho,\mathrm x}$ and $X^{\mathrm f}_{\rho,\ell}$ are
$X^{\mathrm f}_{\rho}$ with the reader of \cref{cert:lem:reader} below, on the whole input
and on its first line; $X^{\mathrm f}_{3\mathrm r,\ell}$ is the same change of
$S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$ (the argument being \texttt{True} after the first phase), with the
first-line reader; $X^{\mathrm f}_{3\mathrm m,\ell}$ is that of $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$ with
the initial argument $-2=F_{\infty}(\,)$ and the leaf test of the first phase replaced by
the test of (c), with the first-line reader; and $X^{\mathrm f}_{\sharp}$ is
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$, the kernel of $S^{\mathrm x}$, with the phase by an empty
context, the mask passed as the context if it is nonempty and otherwise by the cases of
(a), and the first test of (b) in the leaf guard of the first phase.

\begin{proposition}[Masks fused into the first opening]\label{cert:prop:masks}
Let $X$ be one of these programs and $K$ its kernel ($S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$,
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$, $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$, or for $X^{\mathrm f}_{\sharp}$ the
program $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm{E,o}}$). On inputs of integers and arrays, $X$
accepts exactly when $K$ accepts and the goal satisfies $F(\,)$ of
\cref{cert:prop:exact-image}. Hence the accepted goals of $X$ are exactly the encodings
$B^{\sharp}$ of the closed theorems of $\mathcal A$, with the certificates of $K$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
In the first phase (floor $0$, flag $1$ or empty context) the guards of $X$ are those of
$K$ conjoined with the mask test; the mask of a child is computed only for an array that
passed the arity guard, so (a) applies, and by induction on the tree with (b) or (c) the
first opening is defined exactly on the trees satisfying $F(\,)$, which satisfy
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$, and then returns the tree, as the opening of $K$ does. After the
first phase the mask test is short-circuited: the leaf guard has the form ``phase over
or $\ldots$'', and the argument passed to the children is the floor, \texttt{True} or the
context, except in $X^{\mathrm s}$, where the state is still computed by $4m-3$, $-m$ and
$2m\pm 1$, total on integers and never read. The positional arguments of every call of the
opener are those of $K$; since the new argument is keyword-only, the call $O(\ast a,t,2)$
on a binary goal still fails with four positional arguments. Every other operation is
literally that of $K$, so the runs agree from the end of the first opening on, and the
last statement follows as in \cref{cert:prop:exact-image}, by \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}
and \cref{cert:prop:eager-three}(c).
\end{proof}

\pkg{exact\_bindings} records that a fourth \emph{positional} argument would remove the
failure of $O(\ast a,t,2)$ at a binary goal and that, according to its replay, a variant
with it accepts the three-field certificate
\texttt{[[0,0,1],[0,0,1],[0,[],1],[1,[1]]]} of the false equation $0=\Suc 0$; the variant
is not delivered as a checker.

\paragraph{Readers of restricted expressions.} \pkg{exact\_masks},
\pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} and \pkg{names\_and\_scopes} read the certificate as a Python
expression after a test of its characters.

\begin{lemma}[Restricted data expressions]\label{cert:lem:reader}
Let $\Sigma$ consist of the ten digits, the minus sign, the square brackets, the comma, the
space and a set of whitespace characters (the line feed, or the line feed, the carriage
return and the tab). For a text $p$, the removal of characters of $\Sigma$ from both ends
of $p$ leaves the empty text exactly when every character of $p$ lies in $\Sigma$. If the
evaluation of such a text as an expression yields an array, the array satisfies $J$, and
the evaluation has no other effect. Every array satisfying $J$ is the value of its JSON
text.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A character outside $\Sigma$ stops the removal from both sides and remains. The reader
evaluates $p$ only when the remainder is empty; otherwise the remainder, a nonempty text,
is used as the remover, and its method lookup or call fails. Over $\Sigma$ the tokens are
decimal integer literals, the minus sign, brackets and commas; so the expressions are
integer literals, list displays, negations, subtractions, subscriptions and, at the top
level only, tuples, since there are no parentheses. No name, call, attribute, string,
other literal, comprehension, slice or assignment can be formed, so nothing is looked
up. By induction on a successfully evaluated expression other than a top-level tuple, its
value is an exact integer or a finite tree of exact lists and integers: negation and
subtraction succeed only on integers, a subscription only on a list with an integer
index, returning an element, and a display builds a list of such values. A tuple has no
method for removal. The last statement holds since a JSON text of integers and arrays is
such an expression.
\end{proof}

Consequently a program with this reader, applied to a text $p$, has after reading the run
of the program with the JSON reader and the test of the printed form on the JSON text of
the value of $p$, and conversely; the first-line variants read the first line only. The
accepted texts include spellings such as \texttt{[3-2,1]} or \texttt{[C][0]} of the same
value; the accepted values are those of the JSON program. The correspondence is one of
ideal executions: CPython's parser limits the nesting of list displays in an expression
(\pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} records the rejection of $201$ nested displays that the JSON
reader parses), in addition to the limits shared with the JSON reader. These programs do
not read their input as one JSON value and carry the double dagger.

\paragraph{Sequent input.} \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} changes the header of
$T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ from $[A,0]$ to $[\Gamma,A,0]$. Let $T^{\rho}_{+,\vdash}$ be
$T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ whose root call takes its context, goal and floor from the header
and whose entry test is membership in $[0,{*}g,H]$ in place of $g+[H]$.

\begin{proposition}[Sequent input]\label{cert:prop:sequent}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item For every list $g$, goal $a$, floor $k$ and stack, the call of $T^{\rho}_{+,\vdash}$
on $(g,a,k)$ and the call of $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ on $([0]+g,a,k)$ consume the same
records, make calls with the same goals and floors and contexts related in the same way,
and return or fail together.
\item If $T^{\rho}_{+,\vdash}$ accepts an input of integers and arrays with header
$[\Gamma,A,0]$, where $A$ and the members of $\Gamma$ are $1$-shifted canonical encodings
of closed public formulas, then the reading of $A$ is derivable in $\Teq$ from the readings
of the members of $\Gamma$.
\item If $\mathcal U$ derives the public sequent $\Gamma\proves A$ with closed $\Gamma$ and
$A$, then $T^{\rho}_{+,\vdash}$ accepts a certificate with header $[\Gamma,A,0]$. A theorem
certificate of $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ with header $[A,0]$ is accepted with header
$[[\,],A,0]$, and conversely.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) The opener does not consult the context, and the lists of the entry tests are equal,
$([0]+g)+[H]=[0,{*}g,H]$. The major call keeps the relation, and the continuation context
satisfies $[0]+(g+[h])=([0]+g)+[h]$. Every other expression is the same; induction on the
run. (b) By (a) and \cref{cert:prop:parity-dispatch}(a) with the context $[0]+\Gamma$, whose
members are closed at the floor $0$ and of which $0$ reads $0=0$, the auxiliary reading of
$A$ is derivable from those of $\Gamma$; on public formulas the auxiliary and the ordinary
readings are equivalent in $\Teq$ (\cref{cert:rem:imported}). (c) The compilation of the
proof of \cref{cert:prop:parity-dispatch}(b) is an induction on derivations whose
hypotheses are members of the context of the call; started with the context
$[0]+\Gamma$ in place of $[0]$, a hypothesis leaf whose formula lies in $\Gamma$ closes by
membership, and the names reserved below the floor $0$ are fresh for the closed members of
$\Gamma$ (\cref{cert:lem:fresh}). By (a) the certificate is accepted by
$T^{\rho}_{+,\vdash}$. The last statement is (a) with $g=[\,]$.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the package states the completeness for sequents through "the parameterized
% canonical compiler"; the use of the context of the compilation and the freshness of
% the reserved names for the closed assumptions are made explicit here.

Acceptance of $[\Gamma,A,0]$ means $\Gamma\proves A$: the input
$[[0=\Suc 0],\,0=\Suc 0,\,0]$ is accepted with a hypothesis leaf, and it does not certify
$0=\Suc 0$. \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} also delivers two \emph{seeded} variants that keep
the body of their control and take the initial context from the header: of
$T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm n}$ ($455$ bytes; tokens and nodes are not reported) and of the stream
program $L_{\mathrm c}$; they are complete only for contexts that contain the neutral
seed $0$ (respectively the byte string), which the certificate must then supply.

\begin{remark}[Input contracts of these programs]\label{cert:rem:new-contracts}
The programs of this part fall under six contracts, which the table keeps apart.
\begin{itemize}
\item $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$, $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi}$ and
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi_1}$ satisfy T/ed$_1$, the sorted grammar $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$
with the typed whole input; completeness holds for canonical goals, not for the aliases
of \cref{cert:cor:second-dummy}.
\item The mask programs satisfy T/ed$_1^{\sharp}$, the exact image of the padded encoding.
\item The programs of \cref{cert:prop:actual} satisfy the exact contract for the encoding
$B^{\natural}$ (T/ed$_1^{\natural}$). Like the shifted encoding of \cref{cert:def:offset}
against that of \cref{cert:def:enc}, it is another encoding of the same goals, and its
programs are not compared with those of T/ed$_1^{\sharp}$; comparisons across the two
encodings are stated as such.
\item $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ satisfies a goal contract for the encoding $B^{\natural}$
with the spelling \texttt{true} of $1$ and no condition on unused data
(G$^{\natural}_{\top}$). The programs of T/ed$_1^{\natural}$ satisfy this weaker contract,
so its envelope pools them, as the line of T/g bounds G/$\mathcal U$.
\item The readers of \cref{cert:lem:reader} keep the contracts of their JSON programs for
the parsed values but change the accepted texts; they carry the double dagger.
\item $T^{\rho}_{+,\vdash}$ and the seeded variants accept sequents
(R$^{+2}_{\vdash}$/$\mathcal U$, and R$_{\vdash}$/$\mathcal U$ for the unshifted stream
program). They are not compared with programs that accept theorems: a program of a theorem
contract may accept a goal only if it is a theorem, and $T^{\rho}_{+,\vdash}$ accepts
$[[A],A,0]$ for every $A$; read as a theorem checker it needs the further promise that the
context in the header is empty, which no program of R$^{+2}$/$\mathcal U$ requires, since
those programs form their initial context themselves. The saving of two bytes moves the
neutral seed from the initial call into the entry test, and that of the seeded variants
moves it into the certificate. \pkg{intrinsic\_validation} and \pkg{names\_and\_scopes}
include the sequent and seeded programs in a compact frontier ($239/255/458$, or
$238/255/455$ with the seeded header); the article does not.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

The programs are, of \pkg{validated\_phase}, $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ ($300/346/626$,

\subsubsection*{Local validation, lazy source instances, a threshold mode, names from finite trees and shifted strict programs}

This part follows \pkg{exact\_threshold}, \pkg{local\_validation},
\pkg{retained\_validation}, the checker part of \pkg{reconstructed\_introduction} and
\pkg{structural\_parameters}. The second, third and fourth start from the revision
\texttt{e0d702b905}, which contains the seven packages of the previous part as incoming
archives (\pkg{retained\_validation} adds a review at \texttt{f25af53c68});
\pkg{exact\_threshold} starts from \texttt{f25af53c68}, which also contains
\pkg{structural\_parameters}; and \pkg{structural\_parameters} starts from
\texttt{0198424b74}, with its compact control taken from \texttt{fc05b10f0e}. The calculus
of \pkg{reconstructed\_introduction}, whose introduction computes its matrix from the
proved premise, belongs to the chapter on witnesses; its readable reference checker has
$1896$ tokens, $2182$ nodes and $7639$ bytes and checks a calculus of that chapter. The
alternative basis of \pkg{local\_validation} (\texttt{tail\_induction.tex}: forward
iteration with induction on equations that lets one parameter change) concerns the base
theories and is not treated in this chapter (\cref{cert:sec:questions}).

The following devices are applied to programs of the earlier parts.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),start=13]
\item The \emph{intrinsic form} of a validated program omits the test of the printed form,
removes records from the parsed input directly and recognizes arrays by $a\cdot 0=[\,]$ in
place of $a\cdot 0\neq 0$, as $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ of \cref{cert:prop:intrinsic} does.
\item The \emph{leaf validation} $\beta$ of a four-field program replaces the guards of
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ by the guards $\pi$ of (xi) without a coercion: the range test is
$k\le a<d$, followed, whenever it succeeds, by the leaf test $k<d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$, and
the arity guard is $k\mathbin{|}(\lvert a\rvert\in\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\})$. The three-field
programs $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ and $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$ already have these guards.
\item The \emph{lazy source instance} $\lambda$ of a four-field program writes the new goal
as the opening at $[k]$ of ``$O(t;s,0)$ if $(m,a)=(M^{\circ},O(t;u,0))$, else $a$'', where
the earlier programs open at $[k]$ the element of the pair $(a,O(t;s,0))$ selected by the
same test, and so evaluate $O(t;s,0)$ also when the test fails.
\item The \emph{threshold mode} $\tau$ writes the selection of progress as
$\lvert a\rvert=2>u$ in place of $\lvert a\rvert=2=u$: progress is taken at a binary matrix
when the mode is a number below $2$, and cases otherwise.
\item The \emph{fused control} $\phi$ of a three-field program writes ``unless the
terminal test $L$ holds: if $O(t;u,0)=a$, then $a\defeq O(t;s,0)$ and $s\defeq M^{\circ}$;
then the final call $C$'' as the one expression $L\lor(M\lor(A_1,A_2),\,C)$, with $M$ the
test $O(t;u,0)\neq a$ and $A_1,A_2$ the two assignments written as assignment expressions.
\end{enumerate}
Let $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm E}$ and $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$ be $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ and
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$ with the increment $j\mathbin{\&}2$ at odd depths of
\cref{cert:def:actual}; they are not delivered. The programs are: $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},
\lambda\beta}$ and $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\tau}$, obtained from
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$; the intrinsic forms $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$ and
$S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m,i}}$ of $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm E}$ and $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$,
the first in two spellings (with the counter ``$s$ and $[s]$, or $1$'' and the arities
$(1,2+(d\bmod 2))$ as a tuple, or with ``$[s]$ if $s$ is true-valued, else $1$'' and the
arities as a set), and in $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m,i}}$ the initial context is $[[\,]]$ and
the reader is bound in the argument of the root call;
$S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$, which is $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$ with $\phi$, the
counter of the second spelling and the decrement written $k-1$; and
$S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi|}$, which is $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ with the range
test $k\le(a\mathbin{|}0)<d$; and, in the padded encoding, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$,
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ and $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi|}$, obtained from $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$
in the same way, the last two with the counter ``$s$ and $[s]$, or $1$'' and ``$[s]$ if $s$
is true-valued, else $1$''.

\begin{lemma}[Retained scalars are validated locally]\label{cert:lem:local-validation}
Let $O'$ be the opener of one of these programs and $E'$ the eager opener with the retention
$a\lor 1$ of the same reading ($E^{+}_k$, or its actual-binding form of
\cref{cert:def:actual}), on the raw trees of \cref{cert:def:raw} in which a value that is not
numeric and not an array makes every comparison undefined. Let $x$ be a value of the JSON
reader, $t$ an image or $\bot$, and let either $k<0$, or $k=0$ and $t=\bot$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If $O'_k(x;t,d)$ is defined, then $E'_k(x;t,d)$ is defined and equal to it; every
scalar that $O'$ retains is an integer or a Boolean, and the result is a tree of integers,
Booleans and arrays.
\item If $O'_k(x;\bot,d)$ is defined, then $x$ is a tree of integers, Booleans and arrays.
\item Every image that a defined opening inserts is opened with the missing image at the
root term depth, so it is a tree of integers, Booleans and arrays by (b).
\item With the range test $k\le(a\mathbin{|}0)<d$, every scalar that a defined opening
reaches is an integer or a Boolean.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Among the values of the JSON reader the product with $0$ is the empty array exactly for
arrays: a string gives a string, a number or a Boolean a number, and \texttt{null} and an
object make the product fail. At another value the range test compares it with $k$ and $d$,
which fails for a string. In the programs with the guards of $\beta$, if the range test
succeeds, the leaf test forms $d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)$; for a floating-point $a$ the remainder
is a floating-point number and the bitwise conjunction fails. In the programs with the coercion
$a\mathbin{|}0$ (those obtained from $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ without $\beta$, and those with the
subscript $|$) the coercion fails for a floating-point $a$ before the comparison. So a retained
scalar is an integer or a Boolean, and it is returned as $a\lor 1$. For such a scalar and $k<0$
the guards hold: $d\mathbin{\&}(a\bmod 2)\in\{0,1\}$ is above $k$, $k\mathbin{|}b$ is negative,
and a disjunction with $k$ is true-valued; at $k=0$ the guards are those of the first phase
(\cref{cert:lem:phase-guards}), which only add failures. A scalar that is not retained is a hole of $E'$ at its position, and
the fallback $(d\bmod 2)\cdot O'(t;\bot,3)$ evaluates the opening of the image, as $E'$ does;
for $t=\bot$ it fails, since the missing image ($0j$ or the checking function) fails every
comparison or the product with $0$. Arrays are traversed at the child depths of the reading.
Induction on $x$ gives (a), with the result a tree of retained scalars, inserted images and
products of an image with $0$, which are $0$ or $[\,]$. For (b), with the missing image no
scalar is a hole, so every scalar is retained. (c) is the form of the fallback. For (d),
$a\mathbin{|}0$ is defined exactly for integers and Booleans.
\end{proof}

Floating-point values may therefore occur in a successful run, but only as holes of templates,
in fields that are never opened, or in a computation candidate that is compared with an opened
goal. A floating-point scalar is numeric (\cref{cert:def:raw}); outside the range of the opener
it reads as the hole $w$ (\cref{cert:def:reading}), so \cref{cert:lem:opening,cert:lem:exposure}
apply to it as they stand. At the floor $-1$ and the depth $3$, for instance,
$O'(100.5;1,3)$ and $O'(\mathrm{NaN};[1],3)$ insert their images, while $O'(1.0;1,3)$ fails.
% ed.: retained_validation and local_validation replace each floating hole by an integer above
% every visited depth, separately for the two openings of one inference; by cert:def:raw this
% reification is not needed, since floating scalars already have the reading of a hole.

\begin{proposition}[Intrinsic forms]\label{cert:prop:intrinsic-forms}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The statements of \cref{cert:prop:actual} hold for $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm E}$ and
$S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$, with the certificates $\nu_{\mathrm e}(\sigma)$ of
\cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b) compiled with the encoding $B^{\natural}$.
\item Let $P$ be one of $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$,
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\tau}$, $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$ (in either spelling),
$S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m,i}}$, $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ and
$S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi|}$. If $P$ accepts a JSON document, its last element is a tree
of integers, Booleans and arrays that becomes $B^{\natural}$ for a closed formula $B$ of the
language with equality and $D$ when every \texttt{true} is replaced by $1$, and $B$ is a theorem
of $\mathcal A$. The same holds for $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ and
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi|}$, with ``satisfies $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}(\cdot,0)$'' in place of ``is
$B^{\natural}$'' and the sorted reading, in which $1$ reads as $0$.
\item For every closed theorem $B$ of $\mathcal A$ in the language with equality and $D$:
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ accepts the certificates of \cref{cert:prop:intrinsic}(b)
with goal $B^{\natural}$; $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\tau}$ accepts them after the mode $u$
of every introduction whose opened matrix is a binary array is exchanged, $2$ for $0$ and $0$
for $2$; the three-field programs of (b) in the actual-binding encoding accept the certificates
of (a); and $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ and $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi|}$
accept the certificates $\nu_{\mathrm e}(\sigma)$ of \cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b) with goal
$B^{\sharp}$. In each case the certificates are also accepted with any leaf $1$ of the goal
written \texttt{true}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) \Cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b) proves the statements of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal}
for $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ by the induction of \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}, which uses the encoding
only through the lemmas of \cref{cert:lem:actual}(a) and the first opening only through the
guards of the first phase; by \cref{cert:lem:phase-guards} the guards $\pi$ select the branches
of the guards of $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$, so \cref{cert:lem:actual}(b) applies. The proof of
\cref{cert:prop:actual} therefore applies to $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm E}$, and the last statement of
\cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b) transfers it to $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m}}$ on inputs that pass
the test of the printed form.

(b) The first opening is made at the floor $0$ with the missing image. By
\cref{cert:lem:local-validation}(b) the goal is then a tree of integers, Booleans and arrays, on
which the opener agrees with that of the program with the test of the printed form; a
\texttt{true} passes the leaf test as $1$, and \texttt{false} fails it, since $0$ is even, and
then consults the missing image. So \cref{cert:lem:actual}(b), or statement (a) of
\cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} for $S^{3}_{\mathrm E}$ (\cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b)) in
the padded encoding, gives the shape of the goal. For soundness run the induction of the proof
of \cref{cert:prop:intrinsic}(a) for the four-field programs, and that of
\cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}(b) with the eager fallback of \cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b) for the
three-field programs, on raw trees in the sense of \cref{cert:def:raw}. By
\cref{cert:lem:local-validation}(a) every opening of $P$ is an opening of $E'$, so the lemmas of
the codec hold for it. The following facts replace the test of the printed form.
\begin{itemize}
\item Every goal, major and context member is a tree of integers, Booleans and arrays: a goal
and a major are opened with the missing image at the entry of their calls
(\cref{cert:lem:local-validation}(b)), and a member of a context is the result of an opening.
Every image that is inserted is such a tree (\cref{cert:lem:local-validation}(c)). A template
$t$ may contain floating-point values only as holes, which both openings $O(t;u,0)$ and
$O(t;s,0)$, made at the same floor and depth, replace by their images; the reading of $t$ is
that of a numeric raw tree.
\item If the computation candidate $H$ equals the opened goal, each leaf of $H$ equals an
integer or Boolean leaf of the goal; so $H$ contains no string, \texttt{null}, object or NaN
(the goal contains none, so the shortcut of list equality by identity of the one NaN object of
the reader cannot apply), and its floating-point leaves are integral. Replacing each of them by
the equal integer gives a tree of integers and arrays with the same reading that is equal to
the goal. The opened component $O(t;[s,u,t],3)$ is unchanged by the replacement: if the image
is inserted it contains no floating-point value, and a floating-point leaf of $t$ is in the
range of the opener exactly where the equal integer is, where it fails the leaf test or the
coercion, and is a hole otherwise, as the integer is. So \cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf} applies.
\item A field is otherwise used only as a truth value, as the mode, which is compared with $2$
(by $=$, or by $>$ in $\tau$, where an undefined comparison rejects), or as a major, which is
proved. A record that is an object or a string of the right length instead of an array is
unpacked into its keys or characters; the candidate then contains strings and equals no opened
goal, and every other use of the fields fails; such a record is consumed only by a call that
returns by membership, which does not use it. In $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,m,i}}$ a record that is not an
array of three entries leaves the names unbound and is rejected (\cref{cert:prop:match}(a)).
\item With $\lambda$, when the endpoint test fails the opening $O(t;s,0)$ is not made. An
opening reads no record and changes no floor, and its result is used only when the test
succeeds, where it is made at the same floor as in $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$; so the extracted
inference is that of the case of the failed test, \rulename{DE} or an introduction, whose
conclusion and retained hypothesis do not involve $O(t;s,0)$.
\item With $\tau$, progress is taken only at an opened matrix of arity $2$, which reads a literal
equation, and otherwise the image of the hypothesis is \texttt{False}, read as $0$; the image is
the floor or \texttt{False} whatever $u$ is. \rulename{DI}-case is valid for every matrix and
\rulename{DI}-progress for literal equations, so either selection is an inference of
$\mathcal A$.
\end{itemize}
With these facts every case of the inductions is as before, and at the root call the context
$[[\,]]$ has the member $[\,]$, which reads $0=0$. The fused control $\phi$ performs the same
operations in the same order: if $L$ fails, the tuple is evaluated from the left, the two
assignments are made exactly when $M$ is false, the second reads the old $s$, and then $C$ is
evaluated; no value of the tuple is used. The coercion of $S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi|}$
only adds failures.

(c) On certificates of integers and arrays every program of (b) has the runs of its typed
counterpart, after the test of the printed form, which these certificates pass: the two array
tests agree on integers and arrays, $a\mathbin{|}0=a$ on integers, ``$[s]$ if $s$ else $1$'' and
``$s$ and $[s]$, or $1$'' have the same value since $[s]$ is true-valued, the set and the tuple
of arities have the same members, and the fused control performs the same operations by (b).
For $\beta$, on integers the leaf test and the guards $\pi$ agree with the guards of
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ (\cref{cert:lem:phase-guards}). For $\lambda$, the accepting run of
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ evaluates $O(t;s,0)$ successfully, and omitting a defined opening
changes no record, floor or value that is used. For $\tau$, the record of an introduction is
$[0,u,R(w),[\,]]$: its endpoint test fails for every $u$, since the major $[\,]$ is not an array
of arity $2$ or $3$; the opening $O(t;u,0)$ of the canonical witness is defined for $u\in\{0,2\}$,
since the eager opener consults $u$ at even depths and opens it at the depth $3$ to $1$ or $2$;
and its candidate is binary while its goal is unary; so $u$ enters only the selection of the
mode, and the exchanged values select the same mode as before. A leaf \texttt{true} of the goal
behaves as $1$ in every comparison, range test, parity, truth value and equality, and remains
\texttt{true} in opened trees, which compare equal to those with $1$, as in the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:intrinsic}(b). The completeness of the typed counterparts is (a),
\cref{cert:prop:intrinsic}(b) and \cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b).
\end{proof}
% ed.: the packages argue soundness for arbitrary JSON by a local reification of foreign
% values; the reduction to the inductions already proved, the candidate with integral
% floating-point leaves, the non-array records and the identity of the NaN object are written
% out here, and (a) is supplied from the proofs cited.

The accepted documents are not those of the earlier programs: $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},
\lambda\beta}$ accepts \texttt{[[0,1,1,[]],[0,1,1,[]],[null,0,1,[]],[[[1,1,1],1]]]}, which
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ rejects because it opens the template at the source
\texttt{null}, and an introduction whose witness $100.5$ does not occur in its matrix; and
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\tau}$ needs the exchanged modes. The accepted canonical goals are
the same. \pkg{exact\_threshold} notes that only the modes of introductions may be exchanged: the
integers $0$ and $2$ occur in other fields with other roles.

\begin{remark}[Input contracts of these programs]\label{cert:rem:local-contracts}
The programs of (b) with the actual-binding encoding satisfy G$^{\natural}_{\top}$, as
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ does, whether they read four-field packets or three-field records.
The padded programs $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ and
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi|}$ admit \texttt{true} as a spelling of $1$ and arbitrary unused data, so
they satisfy neither T/ed$_1$ nor G$_1$/$\mathcal A^{-}$, whose goals consist of exact integers;
their code is G$_{1,\top}$/$\mathcal A^{-}$, the goal contract for $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$ with
\texttt{true} admitted as a spelling of $1$ and no condition on unused data. They keep the dummy
slots of the padded encoding, so their completeness concerns the canonical goals $B^{\sharp}$ and
not the aliases of \cref{cert:cor:second-dummy}. The programs of T/ed$_1$ and of
G$_1$/$\mathcal A^{-}$ satisfy this weaker contract, and its envelope pools them, as that of
G$^{\natural}_{\top}$ pools T/ed$_1^{\natural}$.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Fresh names from finite trees.} \pkg{structural\_parameters} removes the floor from
the stream program $L_{\mathrm f}$ of \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}: a fresh parameter is
named by a tree that contains the data for which it must be fresh.

\begin{definition}[Opaque reading]\label{cert:def:opaque}
The \emph{opaque reading} is the reading of \cref{cert:def:reading} with the following changes.
There is no floor. At a term position an array of arity at least $4$ reads as a free parameter;
two such arrays name the same parameter exactly when they are equal after every \texttt{False}
is replaced by $0$ and every \texttt{True} by $1$, that is, under \texttt{==}. At a term position
below $p$ slots an integer $r$ reads as $0$ if $r<3$, as the slot $j$ if $3+2j\le r<5+2j$ and
$j<p$, and as the hole otherwise; negative integers are aliases of $0$. The opener
$O^{\mathrm o}(a;t,d)$ returns an array of arity at least $4$ unchanged, opens the children of an
array of arity $r\le 3$ at the depth $d+5-r$, retains a scalar $a<d$, and replaces another
scalar by $0$ at an even depth and by $O^{\mathrm o}(t;\bot,3)$ at an odd one. For a call with
context $g$, goal $a$ and record $R=[s,u,t]$ let $v_R\defeq[s,u,t,g,a]$. The \emph{size} of a
tree is the number of its nodes.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Opaque opening and structural freshness]\label{cert:lem:opaque}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item \Cref{cert:lem:opening,cert:lem:exposure,cert:lem:canonical,cert:lem:leaves} hold for the
opaque reading and $O^{\mathrm o}$, with ``closed at the floor $k$'' read as ``closed''. Every
array of arity at least $4$ is a fixed point of every opening, and every parameter of the
reading of a defined opening $O^{\mathrm o}(a;t,d)$ is a parameter of the reading of $a$ or of
$t$.
\item Let $x$ be a subtree of $s$, $u$, $t$, $a$ or of a member of $g$. The parameter named by
$v_R$ does not occur in the reading of $x$, of an opening of $x$ at an image that is a subtree of
$s$, $u$ or $t$, or $0$ or \texttt{False}, or of a repetition $[x,x,x]$ or $3x$ of such a tree.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(a) By induction on the tree, as for \cref{cert:lem:opening}, with one new case: an array of
arity at least $4$ reads as one parameter at a term position and as $0=0$ at a formula position,
and is returned unchanged. A scalar is retained exactly when it does not read as the hole, and an
image opened at the root term depth has, below $q$ of its own binders, only scalars below $3+2q$
and opaque parameters, so copying it below further slots captures nothing. The exposure lemma
holds since lowering the depth by $2$ turns exactly the interval of the outermost slot into the
hole, the interval of $0$ being all integers below $3$; the canonical opening lemma and
\cref{cert:lem:leaves} use the codec only through these facts (proof of
\cref{cert:lem:closed-leaf}). The statement on parameters follows in the same induction.

(b) A parameter of the reading of $x$ is a subtree of $x$, and $x$ is a proper subtree of
$v_R$, so it has fewer nodes than $v_R$; the replacement of Booleans preserves sizes, so it is not
equal to $v_R$ under \texttt{==}. By (a) the openings of $x$ at these images have only parameters
of $x$ and of the image, and a repetition has the parameters of $x$.
\end{proof}

Let $L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ be $L_{\mathrm f}$ with the opener $O^{\mathrm o}$, without the floor
and its decrement, with the record $r$ extended in place by $g$ and $a$ to $v_R$ after the
terminal test, with the image of the hypothesis $(c(g,s)\lor s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2)\land v_R$ in
place of $(c(g,s)\lor s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2)\land k$ and the new goal opened at $[v_R]$, and with
the initial context $[0]$ in place of the byte string whose element is $0$.

\begin{proposition}[Fresh names from finite trees]\label{cert:prop:opaque}
Consider streams of integers and arrays whose first line is the canonical encoding of a closed
public formula.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If a call of $L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ with goal $a$ and context $g$ returns, and $a$ and the
members of $g$ are closed in the opaque reading, then the auxiliary reading of $a$ is derivable in
$\Teq$ from the auxiliary readings of the members of $g$. If $L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ accepts, its
goal is a theorem of $\mathcal U$.
\item Every closed public theorem of $\mathcal U$ has a stream certificate that
$L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ accepts.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) As in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}(a), with the image of
\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(a). The opening of the goal, the terminal test with its candidate,
the replacement test with the opening of the source instance, and in an introduction the opening
of the matrix at the witness, are evaluated before the record is extended; the record is a fresh
list of the reader, which no earlier tree contains, and $g$ and $a$ are never changed in place,
so $v_R$ is a finite tree. Then the major call is made, and the hypothesis and the new goal are
opened at $v_R$. That proof uses of its eigenparameter only its freshness for the readings of
$a$, of the members of $g$, of the major and of the opened matrix, and the closedness of the
hypothesis and of the new goal. The major is $s$ when an eigenparameter is used, and the matrix is
the opening of the child of $a$ at $t$, so \cref{cert:lem:opaque}(b) gives the freshness; the
openings are closed by \cref{cert:lem:opaque}(a). Every other use of the codec is a lemma of
\cref{cert:lem:opaque}(a). The image \texttt{False} of a case hypothesis is retained at the depth
$3$ and reads $0$; the initial member $0$ reads $0=0$. At the root the goal is closed and
\cref{cert:rem:imported} concludes.

(b) Take a derivation of the theorem in $\mathcal U$ and replace, from the root upwards, every
free variable of a subderivation that is neither free in its end sequent nor an eigenparameter of
a rule in it by $0$ (\cref{base:sec:structural}). Afterwards every parameter free at a node is the
eigenparameter of a rule below it whose minor premise or premise contains the node, and every term
that a rule displays has only such parameters. Compile as in the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}(b), choosing the names online: where $L_{\mathrm r}$ uses the
floor left by a major call at a step $C$, $L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ uses $v_C$, formed from the
record of $C$, which does not mention the new name, and from the context and goal of $C$, which are
fixed when the record is emitted; later records refer to $v_C$ explicitly, so the certificate is
finite. The map $\nu$ from eigenparameters to these names need not be injective, but it is
injective on the parameters free at each node: two of them are the eigenparameters of steps $C_1$
and $C_2$ with $C_2$ in the continuation of $C_1$, and the context of $C_2$ extends that of $C_1$ by
at least the hypothesis of $C_1$, so $v_{C_2}\neq v_{C_1}$. Each call compares and opens only
trees whose parameters are free at its node, and a renaming that is injective on these commutes
with the openings and preserves equality, membership and the tests of arity, \texttt{False}
comparing equal to $0$. So the run of $L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ follows call by call that of
$L_{\mathrm f}$, which accepts the certificates of $L_{\mathrm r}$ with the same records and calls
(\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(a)); the arrays of arity at least $4$ that
$L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ does not traverse are tripled majors at positions of formula sort, which
equal no canonical goal.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the package states completeness by renaming "unrelated free source parameters" and by the
% remark that a name "may be reused in a different branch"; the normalization of the derivation
% and the injectivity of the renaming on the parameters free at a node are supplied here.

The order matters as for $L_{\mathrm r}$: the witness is opened before the major call and is part of
$v_R$, so a witness cannot name the parameter of its own step, and a name forged in a witness for a
later step $D$ cannot occur in the context or goal of $D$, since it would then be a proper subtree
of itself. \pkg{structural\_parameters} records that the variant with the fixed name
\texttt{[0,0,0,0]} (\texttt{structural/negative/deliberately\_unsound\_fixed\_name.py}) accepts the
false goal $\D{x}{y}\bigl(\It{y}{0}{u}{x}=\It{y}{0}{u}{y}\bigr)$ with the witness $\Suc F$ for the fixed
name $F$, and that $L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ rejects it. The names enlarge certificates: the
certificate of an induction with a used hypothesis grows from $12726$ to $635588$ bytes. The
readable checker of the package (\texttt{structural/reference\_checker.py}) validates the stream and
the guarded goal; it has no reported size.

\paragraph{Strict programs in the shifted encoding.} Let $S^{3}_{\mathrm e,+}$,
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m},+}$ and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,+}$ be $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$
and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ with the default depth of the opener $2$, images validated at the depth
$5$ in the fallback and in the candidate, and the witness opened at the depth $4$; in the first two
the fallback $(d\bmod 2\lor\neg k)\land O(t;\bot,3)$ is the product $(d\bmod 2)\cdot O(t;\bot,5)$
and the initial context is the byte string whose element is $1$, in the third the initial context
is $[1]$; in $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m},+}$ the floor is initialized by a statement and not in a default.
Let $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1,+}$ be $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1}$ read from the formula depth $2$, and let
$B^{\sharp}_{+}$ be $B^{\sharp}$ with $2$ added to every scalar of value at least $3$.

\begin{proposition}[Shifted strict programs]\label{cert:prop:shifted-strict}
The statements (a), (b) and (c) of \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} hold for
$S^{3}_{\mathrm e,+}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m},+}$ and $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,+}$ in the $1$-shifted
encoding: they accept only inputs that satisfy $J$ and whose goal satisfies
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1,+}(\cdot,2)$; the $1$-shifted sorted reading of an accepted goal, in which
every scalar below $5$ at the root term depth reads as $0$, is a closed theorem of $\mathcal A$;
and for every closed theorem $B$ they accept a certificate with goal $B^{\sharp}_{+}$, namely
the certificate of \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}(c) with $2$ added to every scalar of value at
least $3$, negative names and the scalars $0$, $1$ and $2$ unchanged, and, for
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,+}$, the neutral major $[\,]$ replaced by $0$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The proof of \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase} uses the encoding through the lemmas that
\cref{cert:lem:offset} transfers to the $1$-shifted reading, and the eager fallback through
\cref{cert:lem:eager}, as in \cref{cert:prop:eager-validated}(b) and
\cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}(b). It uses the neutral resource and the neutral major through the
four properties listed in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:offset}. In the shifted programs the
initial member $1$ and the neutral major $0$ lie in $[k,2)$ for every $k<0$, so both are retained
at the formula depth $2$, the major opening to $1$ by $a\lor 1$; both read $0=0$
(\cref{cert:lem:offset}); $1$ is a member of every context, which is formed from the initial one
by concatenation, the byte string contributing its element $1$; the major $0$ is false-valued and
so is $3\cdot 0$; and no scalar equals an array. So the transformation $\nu_{\mathrm e}$ is not
needed: the neutral major is retained by the eager opener, as in \cref{cert:prop:offset}. At the
floor $0$ the guards are those of the first phase with every depth raised by $2$, and they admit
exactly the trees of $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1,+}$; a scalar at a position of formula sort is a hole,
whose missing image fails. Canonical certificates have no other scalar at a position of formula
sort, so the eager fallback leaves their runs unchanged, and the induction of
\cref{cert:thm:floor-phase} applies with the shifted lemmas. The structural binding of
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m},+}$ is treated as in the last statement of \cref{cert:thm:floor-phase}.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the package (strict/PROOF.md) argues by the shifted opening lemma and the entry-freshness
% invariant; the reduction to cert:lem:offset and to the four properties of the neutral
% resources is written here.

The grammar $P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1,+}$ admits the scalar $3$ as a further spelling of $0$ and
references to dummy slots, among them \texttt{[[7],[1]]}, the shifted spelling of the goal of
\cref{cert:cor:second-dummy}; completeness is stated for the canonical goals $B^{\sharp}_{+}$ only.
The three programs satisfy a contract of their own, T/ed$_1^{+2}$: the typed contract for
$P^{\mathrm{ed}}_{1,+}$, another encoding of the goals of T/ed$_1$, as R$^{+2}$/$\mathcal U$ is of
those of R/$\mathcal U$.

(\texttt{controls/intrinsic\_checker.py}); of \pkg{local\_validation},
$S^{3\natural}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ ($312/353/588$, \texttt{code/checker\_exact.py}) and

and $S^{3\natural\prime}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$; of \pkg{reconstructed\_introduction},
