\subsubsection*{The pair-indexed increment, a first-line sorted program, a derived floor, list contexts and names from the printed length}

This part follows \pkg{checker\_research}, \pkg{scope\_free\_audit}, \pkg{stack\_clock} and the
compact refinement of \pkg{composition}. The first three start from the revision
\texttt{65c32fbe47}, which contains the five packages of the previous part as incoming archives
(\pkg{stack\_clock} adds a check at \texttt{6ce5acc401}); the compact refinement of
\pkg{composition} starts from \texttt{e0d702b905}. The presentation by programs and environments
without variable binders of \pkg{composition} concerns the base theories and is not treated in
this chapter; its reference checker for that presentation (\texttt{code/pointfree\_checker.py})
has $1687$ tokens, $2085$ nodes and $5184$ bytes.

The following devices are applied to programs of the earlier parts.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),start=18]
\item The \emph{pair-indexed increment} writes the child depth
$d+(5-\lvert a\rvert$ if $d$ is even, else $j\mathbin{\&}2)$ of the actual-binding reading as
$d+(5-\lvert a\rvert,\,j\mathbin{\&}2)[d\bmod 2]$, as $S^{\rho\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm e}$ of
\cref{cert:prop:actual} does.
\item The \emph{derived floor} $\kappa$ stores no floor: with $P$ the array of unread elements of
the input and $N$ its length after the removal of the goal, every read of the floor is replaced
by $\lvert P\rvert-N$; the opening of the goal at the entry of a call is made before the removal
of its packet; and the decrement at the entry is omitted, together with the decremented floor as
a member of the entry test.
\end{enumerate}
Let $S^{\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ and $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta,\kappa}$
be $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ with (xviii) and with (xix). Let
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi,\ell}$ be $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ with the arities
$\{1,2+(d\bmod 2)\}$ as a set, the counter ``$[s]$ if $s$ is true-valued, else $1$'', the decrement
written $\mathord{\sim}(-k)$, the entry test written as membership in $g+[k',H]$ in place of
$(k',{*}g,H)$, where $k'$ is the decremented floor and $H$ the computation candidate, the import of
all public names of the JSON module, and the reader of the first line of the input
(\cref{cert:sec:representation}). Let $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm c}$ be the seeded stream variant of
$L_{\mathrm c}$ of \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} (after \cref{cert:prop:sequent}), whose root call takes
its context and goal from the first line $[G,A]$; $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+}$ the program
$L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm c}$ with the entry test written as membership in $g+[H]$ in place of $({*}g,H)$
and the context of the continuation written $g+[h]$ in place of $[{*}g,h]$;
$L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+\sigma}$ the program $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+}$ with the image $[k]$ of the
new goal written as the set $\{k\}$; and $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+\lambda}$ the program
$L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+}$ with the opener written as an anonymous function that recognizes arrays by
$a\cdot 0\neq 0$ in place of an attempted traversal.

\begin{proposition}[Rewritings with the same runs]\label{cert:prop:same-runs}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item On every JSON document, $S^{\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ has exactly the
runs of $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$.
\item For every JSON value $v$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi,\ell}$ on an input whose first line is a
JSON text of $v$ has the run of $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ on a one-line JSON text of $v$, and every
JSON value has a one-line JSON text.
\item $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta,\kappa}$ accepts exactly the JSON documents that
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ accepts, with the same calls, goals, contexts and consumed
packets, and at corresponding points $\lvert P\rvert-N$ is the floor of
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$.
\item On inputs of integers and arrays whose first line is $[G,A]$ with an array $G$,
$L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+}$, $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+\sigma}$ and $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+\lambda}$
have exactly the runs of $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm c}$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence \cref{cert:prop:intrinsic-forms}(b) and (c) hold for $S^{\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$
and $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta,\kappa}$ as for $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$, and
for $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi,\ell}$ as for $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ with certificates written on
one line; and the programs of (d) accept what $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm c}$ accepts.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) The pair is formed only in the array branch of the opener, which is entered only for an array
$a$ (among the values of the JSON reader $a\cdot 0=[\,]$ holds exactly for arrays, proof of
\cref{cert:lem:local-validation}), with an integer index $j$ from the enumeration. Every depth is
an integer: the depths passed explicitly are the literals $2$ and $3$ or the default $0$, also in
the call $O({*}a,t,2)$, whose unpacked elements fill the first two parameters or make the call
fail, and a child depth adds an integer. So both entries of the pair are integers computed
without failure and without effect, and $d\bmod 2\in\{0,1\}$ selects the value of the
conditional. Every other operation is literally the same; induction on the run.

(b) The first-line reader parses the same value $v$ as the whole-input reader on a one-line text
of $v$; a string of a JSON text contains no unescaped line break, so every value has such a text,
and later lines are not read. The public names of the JSON module (\texttt{dump}, \texttt{dumps},
\texttt{load}, \texttt{loads}, \texttt{JSONDecoder}, \texttt{JSONDecodeError},
\texttt{JSONEncoder}) are not names of the program. As in (a) every depth is an integer, so
membership of the arity in the set and in the tuple of the same two integers agree. Since $[s]$ is
true-valued, ``$[s]$ if $s$ else $1$'' and ``$s$ and $[s]$, or $1$'' have the same value, and the
truth value of a value of the reader is computed without effect. For an integer $k$,
$\mathord{\sim}(-k)=k-1$. In both entry tests the goal is opened first, then the floor is
decremented, then $H$ is formed at the decremented floor; the context is an array (the initial
context $[[\,]]$ and every continuation context are arrays, and no operation of the program changes
an array in place), so $g+[k',H]$ and $(k',{*}g,H)$ have the same members in another order.
Membership is the existence of an equal member, and equality between values of the reader and
trees of the program is computed without failure and without effect, so the order does not change
the result. Every other operation is literally the same; induction on the run.

(c) Both programs fail together if the input is not an array or has no last element. The
invariant is: at the entry of a call, before its packet is removed, $\lvert P\rvert-N$ is the floor
$k$ of $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$; initially both are $0$. The first program removes
the packet and then opens the goal at $k$; the second opens the goal at $\lvert P\rvert-N=k$ and then
removes the packet. An opening reads neither the packet nor the input, so the two orders give the
same opened goal or both reject. Then the first program decrements its floor to $k-1$, and
$\lvert P\rvert-N=k-1$. From here on every opening is made at the same floor in both programs, since
no opening removes a packet and every later removal is the removal at the entry of a nested call,
which the first program accompanies by its decrement; by induction the major call and the
continuation keep the invariant. The member $k-1$ of the entry test of the first program never
equals the opened goal: an opened array is not an integer, and a scalar goal at the depth $0$ is
returned only if the leaf test $k\le a<0$ with $k<0$ holds, which makes it an integer of $[k,0)$
(a Boolean fails the range test, a floating-point number the bitwise conjunction), and otherwise
the fallback opens the missing image and fails. So omitting this member changes no entry test, and
the runs correspond.

(d) The context of the root call is the array $G$, and every later context is a new array; no
operation changes an array in place. The tuple $({*}g,H)$ and the array $g+[H]$ have the same members
in the same order. The display $[{*}g,h]$ unpacks $g$ and then evaluates $h$; the sum $g+[h]$
evaluates $h$ and then concatenates. The evaluation of $h$ contains the major call, reads lines and
changes the floor, but changes neither $g$ nor a member of $g$, so both give the same array after
the same evaluation of $h$. The image of the new goal is used only by the opener, which at a hole at
an odd depth opens it at the depth $3$ with the missing image; $\{k\}$ and $[k]$ have length one and
yield the integer $k$ once, so both openings return $[k]$, and the set never enters a tree. On
integers, arrays and the missing image $0j$, the test $a\cdot 0\neq 0$ holds exactly for arrays
($a\cdot 0$ is $0$ for an integer and $0j=0$ for the missing image); where the traversal profile
catches the failure of a child, it compares the array with the floor, which fails as well; so the two
openers return equal values on equal arguments or both fail, and neither reads the input or changes
the floor. Induction on the run in each case.
\end{proof}
% ed.: the packages argue (a), (b) and (d) for the changed expressions and (c) by a commuting
% argument; the integrality of every depth, the order of the members of the entry test and the
% omitted member of (c) at a scalar goal are worked out here.

\pkg{checker\_research} delivers $S^{\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ ($310/358/576$,
\texttt{code/checker.py}) against $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$
(\texttt{controls/retained\_bytes.py}): six bytes fewer, one token and three nodes more.
\pkg{scope\_free\_audit} delivers $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi,\ell}$ ($295/332/553$,
\texttt{code/checker\_sorted\_line.py}) against $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$
(\texttt{reference/checker\_sorted\_556.py}), below it in all three coordinates; it reads the first
line only and carries the double dagger. The package states the program to be below the envelope
$297/335/556$ of the sorted programs; that envelope takes its nodes from $S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m},+}$ of
T/ed$_1^{+2}$, another encoding, and omits the unfused control $S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i}}$
($288/331/586$) of G$_{1,\top}$/$\mathcal A^{-}$. \pkg{stack\_clock} delivers
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta,\kappa}$ ($327/378/611$, \texttt{code/checker.py}) against
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$, larger in all three coordinates, as the package states. By
(c), the length of the unread certificate serves as the floor once the goal is opened before its
packet is removed; this is the second question of \cref{cert:sec:questions} on the scope carried by
the certificate, answered for the reservation at entry of these programs. The design rejected in
\cref{cert:sec:notes} allocated at the consumption of the packet the name that the step itself uses,
before the witness is validated; here the eigenparameter of a step is still the floor after the
return of its major call, and the derived floor only reproduces it. The compact refinement of
\pkg{composition} delivers $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+}$ ($236/262/545$,
\texttt{compact\_refinement/code/checker\_tokens236.py}), $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+\sigma}$
($236/261/545$, \texttt{checker\_tokens236\_set.py}) and $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm{c},+\lambda}$
($236/270/516$, \texttt{checker\_tokens236\_lambda.py}) against $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm c}$
($238/262/545$, \texttt{control\_tokens238.py}); all read one record per line, and the first two
recognize arrays by attempted traversal. The package states the compact envelope $236/255/455$
in place of $238/255/455$, admitting the seeded sequent programs, the stream programs and the shifted
encoding together; none of the three programs attains $255$ nodes or $455$ bytes.

\begin{remark}[Decoded and generated goals]\label{cert:rem:decoded}
\pkg{checker\_research} records the following interface principle. Let $V$ be a checker for the
encodings $E(A)$ of closed native formulas, with a certificate $p$ such that $V(E(A),p)$ accepts
exactly when $A$ is derivable, and let $D$ be a total computable decoder from a set of codes to
closed formulas with $D(E(A))=A$ for every $A$. Then the checker $V_D(q,p)\defeq V(E(D(q)),p)$ has
an accepted certificate for the code $q$ exactly when $D(q)$ is derivable, by the assumption applied
to $D(q)$, and every formula has a code. Likewise, a partial verifier $F$ that returns only derivable
closed formulas and returns every closed theorem for some input gives the checker that accepts
$(A,p)$ exactly when $F(p)$ returns $A$. The decoder, the encoder and the comparison of statements
belong to the measured program. Declaring further raw spellings to denote a formula while the program
still checks the old goal is not such a decoder, as \cref{cert:cor:second-dummy} shows for the
padding aliases.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Fresh names from the printed length.} \pkg{scope\_free\_audit} removes the floor from the
stream program $L_{\mathrm f}$ of \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor} in another way than
\cref{cert:def:opaque}: a fresh name is a negative integer with more decimal digits than the printed
form of the data for which it must be fresh. The idea is that of an unselected experiment of
\pkg{selfseed\_scalar} (\texttt{long\_names.py}, $272/317/566$), which records neither a proof nor a
compiler of certificates. Let $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ be $L_{\mathrm f}$ with the range test
$a<d$ in place of $k\le a<d$, without the floor and its decrement, with the name
$p\defeq-10^{\ell}$ computed after the terminal test and before the replacement test, where $\ell$
is the number of characters of the printed form of $[g,a,r]$ (decimal integers, the words of the
Booleans, brackets, commas and spaces) for the context $g$, the goal $a$ and the record $r$ of the
call, with the image of the hypothesis $(c(g,s)\lor s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2)\land p$ and the new goal
opened at $[p]$, and with the initial context $[0]$ in place of the byte string whose element is $0$.
In this part a tree is \emph{closed} if it is closed at a floor below each of its negative scalars;
by \cref{cert:lem:fresh} the choice of that floor does not matter.

\begin{lemma}[Printed names]\label{cert:lem:printed}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Let $K\le 0$ be below every negative scalar of a finite family of trees of integers, Booleans
and arrays. On trees of the family, with images that are trees of the family, openings of them, $0$,
\texttt{False} or the missing image, the opener of $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ is $O_K$
(\cref{cert:def:opener}), with the same failures.
\item Let $\ell$ be the length of the printed form of $[g,a,r]$, for trees of integers, Booleans and
arrays, and $p=-10^{\ell}$. Then $p$ is not a scalar of $g$, $a$ or $r$, and it is not a parameter of
the reading of a tree obtained from subtrees of $g$, $a$ and $r$ by openings at images that are such
subtrees, $0$ or \texttt{False}, and by repetition.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(a) The range tests $k\le a<d$ and $a<d$ differ only at scalars below $K$. An opening reaches only the
scalars of its tree and of its image, and inserts only $0$ and validated images, so it meets no scalar
below $K$; \texttt{False} is $0$ in every comparison. (b) The printed form of an integer $n$ contains
the decimal digits of $\lvert n\rvert$, so $\lvert n\rvert<10^{\ell}=\lvert p\rvert$; a Boolean is
not negative. The parameters of the reading of an opening are parameters of its tree or of its image
(\cref{cert:lem:opening}(c)), and those of a repetition are those of the repeated tree.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fresh names from the printed length]\label{cert:prop:printed}
Consider streams of integers and arrays whose first line is the canonical encoding of a closed
public formula.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If a call of $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ with goal $a$ and context $g$ returns, and $a$ and the
members of $g$ are closed, then the auxiliary reading of $a$ is derivable in $\Teq$ from the readings
of the members of $g$. If $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ accepts, its goal is a theorem of $\mathcal U$.
\item Every closed public theorem of $\mathcal U$ has a stream certificate that
$L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ accepts.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) Fix a returning call and an integer $K\le 0$ below every negative scalar of its goal, of its
context, of the records read by it and its nested calls, and of the names they compute; these are
finitely many. By \cref{cert:lem:printed}(a) every opening of the run is $O_K$, and the readings are
those at the floor $K$, at which $a$ and the members of $g$ are closed. Argue as in the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}(a), with the image of \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(a) and with
one floor $K$ throughout, by induction on returning calls. The opening of the goal, the terminal test
with its candidate, the name $p$, the replacement test with the opening of the source instance, and
in an introduction the opening of the matrix at the witness, are evaluated before the major call;
the major call is made with $g$; then the hypothesis and the new goal are opened at $p$. That proof
uses of its eigenparameter only its freshness for the readings of $a$, of the members of $g$, of the
major and of the opened matrix, and the closedness of the hypothesis and of the new goal, and it uses
the floor at which the matrix is opened only to make the eigenparameter fresh for it. The major is
$s$, $[s,u]$ or $[t,t,t]$ for the record $r=[s,u,t]$, and the opened matrix is $O_K(B;t,2)$ for the
child $B$ of $a$; so \cref{cert:lem:printed}(b) gives the freshness of $p$, and
\cref{cert:lem:opening}(a) the closedness, since $p\ge K$. A name computed inside the major call may
equal $p$; the induction hypothesis for the major call concerns only the reading of its goal, which
does not mention it. The image \texttt{False} of a case hypothesis is retained at the depth $3$ and
reads $0$; it prints as a word, which only lengthens later printed forms. The initial member $0$ reads
$0=0$. At the root the goal is closed, and \cref{cert:rem:imported} concludes.

(b) As in the proof of \cref{cert:prop:opaque}(b): normalize a derivation of the theorem in
$\mathcal U$ so that every parameter free at a node is the eigenparameter of a rule below it whose
minor premise or premise contains the node, compile as in the proof of
\cref{cert:prop:return-reservation}(b), and choose the names online: where $L_{\mathrm r}$ uses the
floor left by a major call at a step $C$, $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ uses $p_C$, computed from the
context, goal and record of $C$, which are fixed when the record is emitted and do not mention the new
name; later records refer to $p_C$ explicitly. The map $\nu$ from the eigenparameters to these names
need not be injective, but it is injective on the parameters free at each node: two of them are the
eigenparameters of steps $C_1$ and $C_2$ with $C_2$ in the continuation of $C_1$; by the
normalization the eigenparameter of $C_1$ is free in the conclusion of the premise of $C_2$ that
contains the node, whose context, goal and hypothesis other than the eigenparameter of $C_2$ are
readings of the context, goal and record of $C_2$; so $p_{C_1}$ is a scalar of these, and
$p_{C_2}\neq p_{C_1}$ by \cref{cert:lem:printed}(b). Each call compares and opens only trees whose
parameters are free at its node, and a renaming that is injective on these commutes with the openings
and preserves equality, membership and the tests of arity. The run of $L_{\mathrm f}$ on the
certificates of $L_{\mathrm r}$ meets no scalar below its floor, since the floor only decreases and a
canonical record mentions only names allocated before; so its openings are those without the floor.
Hence the run of $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ follows call by call that of $L_{\mathrm f}$, which
accepts the certificates of $L_{\mathrm r}$ with the same records and calls
(\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(a)).
\end{proof}
% ed.: the package proves (a) by a direct case analysis after choosing a floor below the negative
% leaves, writing the image of a case hypothesis as 0 where the program has False; the reduction to
% the proof of cert:prop:return-reservation, the treatment of False under printing and the use of
% the floor at which the matrix is opened are supplied here. For (b) the package keeps an injective
% environment of names and renames a dormant parameter that already carries the new name; the
% normalization of cert:prop:opaque(b) is used instead.

\begin{remark}[Two removals of the floor]\label{cert:rem:printed}
$L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$ (\cref{cert:prop:opaque}) and $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ both remove the
floor of $L_{\mathrm f}$ and both choose the name of a step from data fixed before the major call.
The opaque names are arrays that contain those data and are fresh by size; the printed names are
negative integers, parameters of the sorted reading, and are fresh by their number of digits. The
package's proof of (b) keeps an injective environment from eigenparameters to names and renames a
dormant parameter that already carries the new name; it also describes an alternative in which a
packet $[[b,b],0,0]$ before a step proves $b=b$ and adds the neutral member $[b,b,b,b,b,b]$, whose
printed form contains every active name $b$. The names grow quickly: the digits of a new name exceed
the printed length of the state. In the stream of $209$ bytes for an induction with a used
hypothesis the largest name has $214$ digits; the package's untrusted producer stopped at a limit of
$2000000$ digits on the larger inherited induction certificate, which was therefore not run. The
measured runtime refuses by default conversions between decimal text and integers of more than
$4300$ digits; the package runs the program with this limit removed, a setting outside the measured
module that, like the recursion limit, bounds physical executions only. The package records that a
variant with the fixed name $-1$ accepts a false theorem whose witness captures the name, and that
$L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ rejects it. $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ ($249/272/549$,
\texttt{scope\_free/checker.py}) has ten tokens and eleven nodes more than $L_{\mathrm f}$
(\texttt{scope\_free/control239.py}) and the same bytes, and $L^{\mathrm o}_{\mathrm f}$
($246/269/546$) is below it in all three coordinates; both read one record per line and recognize
arrays by attempted traversal.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The audit of sources}

The audit in \pkg{orbit\_closure} measures every Python source of the ninety
package trees at one pinned revision: $5825$ paths with $1380$ distinct
contents, read and normalized as data and not executed. Its normalization
removes docstrings at every level of nesting; this correction of an earlier
pass changes $173$ measurements and none of the minima. Among the complete
compact checkers of the guarded calculus it finds the minima $256$ tokens,
$276$ nodes and $506$ bytes, attained by three programs: the loop profile
with a global floor of \pkg{kernel\_refinement} ($256/283/594$), a loop
profile of \pkg{deterministic\_opening} ($263/276/580$), and the compact
program of \pkg{deterministic\_introduction} ($267/303/506$). All $106$
programs that are smaller than one of these coordinates are classified one by
one as builders, drivers, generators, measurement programs, converters or
diagnostics, none of which is a complete checker. The audit thereby
establishes two points that the package reports had left open: the triple
$256/278/516$ that \pkg{smaller\_envelope} and \pkg{frontier\_review} take as
their baseline was not the minimum of the corpus at that revision, because
the programs of $276$ nodes and of $506$ bytes, which lie in subdirectories
of their packages, had been overlooked; and no smaller complete checker is
hidden among the sources. \pkg{progress\_independence} extends the audit to
the $1416$ Python files of three later archives with the same outcome below
$506$ bytes. Its second write-up repeats the census of the repository at the
same revision ($5825$ paths, $1380$ distinct contents, $1194$ after
normalization, $25$ short-listed paths reviewed one by one) with the same
minima $256/276/506$, and names one more program attaining $256$ tokens: the
compact token profile of \pkg{uniform\_progress} for $\mathcal U$, with $283$
nodes and $593$ bytes, one byte shorter than the program of
\pkg{kernel\_refinement} and differing from it only by a default value for the
context. \pkg{three\_field} checks by a live inventory that no path of that
revision is missing from the census, and pools the repository with the
incoming archives, which gives the minima $251/273/499$ of
\cref{cert:sec:envelope}. The audit is a census of a finite corpus. It does not verify any
checker, it is not a lower bound, and it keeps the compact contract apart
from the validated ones.

Two later packages repeat the census. \pkg{502\_scope\_audit} replays the
census of \pkg{orbit\_closure} at a later revision with an identical Research
tree ($1380$ blobs, $5825$ paths, the ledger of $106$ utilities) and finds
$256/276/506$, and $253/275/506$ with the incoming archive of
\pkg{smaller\_envelope}. \pkg{verified\_refinements} gives an independent
census at the revision that contains \pkg{three\_field} as an incoming
archive: every Python file of the Research tree without a size cutoff,
including frozen copies and controls ($5935$ paths, $1446$ blobs, all
verified by their hashes and parsed without being executed), and every Python
member of the three incoming archives ($36$ paths, $33$ sources). One blob
cannot be measured, because removing the only docstring of a class leaves an
empty body that the normalization does not repair; with a statement inserted
it has $368/414/1853$, far above every minimum, and it is recorded as an
exclusion. Among the $792$ candidates of the Research tree the minima are
$251/275/502$, attained by three programs; with the incoming archives they
are $249/271/495$, attained by the three programs of \pkg{three\_field}
named in \cref{cert:sec:envelope}. The four Research sources with a JSON
import and fewer than $495$ bytes verify manifests, and none of the $112$
further files below the envelope in some coordinate reads standard input and
prints the acceptance message. The census confirms the envelope that the
packages of \cref{cert:sec:after-first} improve; like the earlier audits it
is a census of a finite corpus and not a lower bound.

Three further packages audit the revision that contains nine incoming
archives, among them those of \cref{cert:sec:after-first}.
\pkg{immediate\_application} inspects $3991$ Python paths and normalizes
$1508$ distinct sources, with four exclusions at the level of paths recorded
and not discarded; it treats a search for programs that read the input and
print the acceptance message as a filter only, and excludes generators of
checkers, deliberately unsound negative controls and packed programs.
\pkg{scope\_and\_frontier} screens the integrated tree of artifacts ($2490$
Python paths, $1314$ blobs, each matched by its hash to the census carried by
the incoming material), retrieves and remeasures every source of fewer than
$1300$ raw bytes ($781$ paths, $333$ blobs) without a discrepancy, finds the
minima $251/275/502$ there, and classifies $59$ small and $19$ larger sources
below the minima in some coordinate as builders, converters, measurement or
reproduction drivers; with the incoming archives the minima are
$248/270/487$, attained by three programs of \pkg{single\_binder} listed in
\cref{cert:sec:sizes}, two of which read only the first line. It notes that
the program of $492$ bytes of \pkg{verified\_refinements} does not lower the
byte minimum below that of $487$ bytes. \pkg{refinement} remeasures the
programs of \pkg{three\_field} and \pkg{constantfree} at its own, earlier
revision, with the minima $249/271/495$. None of the three finds a smaller
complete checker in the corpus.

The packages of \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor} audit one later revision, which
contains sixteen incoming archives. \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers} measures
$3629$ occurrences of Python sources, among them members of nested archives and
compressed sources, with $1725$ distinct normalized modules and no failure of
parsing; it excludes a program of $460$ bytes that its package marks as unsound
(the program without the parity factor of \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf}) and the
compressed loader of \cref{cert:sec:representation}, whose expanded source would

each checked by its hash and length before it is parsed and never executed:
$3601$ occurrences, $1813$ distinct normalized modules and no failure of parsing;
and a check that a candidate is not, after normalization, the text of a program
of the corpus. All three state the minima $240/259/462$ at that revision,
attained by $L_{\mathrm r}$, $L_{\mathrm m}$ and $T_{+,\mathrm p}$, which combine
the stream programs and the two encodings of \cref{cert:sec:offset};
\pkg{prefix\_conditional} also states $245/263/462$ for the programs that read the
whole input, from $G^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm b}$, $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm m}$ and
$T_{+,\mathrm p}$. These censuses compare sources; they do not verify a checker,
and they are not lower bounds.

\pkg{conditional\_image} and \pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement} audit the revision
\texttt{95d3dd192e}. The first covers the $157$ directories of the tree of

modules and no failure of parsing or decoding), removes leading docstrings, and
excludes unsound negative controls, packed launchers, generators and drivers of

which the packages of \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor} state for their earlier
revision, and not the smaller minima of the archives
(\cref{cert:rem:conditional-image}). The second covers the tree of artifacts and
the Python members of the five incoming archives, without expanding nested

acceptance message, which it uses as a filter only; it excludes the unsound
program of $460$ bytes, the compressed loaders and, as a program of another
contract, the tolerant program of \pkg{tolerant\_certificates}, and finds, before
its own programs, the minima $319/358/599$ under the strict contract and $239/256/461$ for the compact
programs, admitting the stream programs and across the two encodings. Like the

\pkg{checked\_reduction} and \pkg{validated\_three\_field} audit the revision
\texttt{798856e4ef}, with the five incoming archives. The first screens $3594$
occurrences of Python sources, members of compressed files and of the archives among
them, and finds no earlier program with the singleton image and no normalized

Both start from the minima $317/356/597$ of the programs of

programs, admitting the stream programs and across the two encodings. These are

\pkg{304\_token\_refinement}, \pkg{neutral\_modes}, \pkg{scope\_phase} and

across the two encodings. The first parses $3632$ occurrences of Python sources
($2013$ distinct contents) without executing them, lists $379$ small programs that

\pkg{validated\_phase}, \pkg{shared\_witness\_bundles}, \pkg{exact\_bindings},

\pkg{exact\_masks} matches $3542$ Python entries of the artifacts and $131$ members of the
