Some remarks on the reading of the table.

The controls of \pkg{uniform\_premise}, \pkg{monus\_and\_neutral\_major} and
\pkg{prevalidated\_elimination} are the three programs of
\pkg{certified\_zero}; \pkg{uniform\_premise} lists in addition an older
program of $266$ tokens, $296$ nodes and $689$ bytes that shares the minimum
of nodes. The control of the byte profile of \pkg{neutral\_scope} is the byte
profile of \pkg{monus\_and\_neutral\_major}, and its loop profile is compared
with an older program outside this chapter, as is the loop profile of
\pkg{scope\_and\_closure} (a program of \pkg{coverage\_and\_kernel}). The
controls of the guarded-source rows are the programs of \pkg{guarded\_source}.
The control of $1000$ bytes was constructed by \pkg{validated\_core} by
restricting the control of $1049$ bytes to the smaller grammar. The control of
$780$ bytes is a program of \pkg{deterministic\_opening}; the control of $803$
bytes is the native goal-validated checker current at the time of
\pkg{checked\_refinement}, whose validator already omits the scope bounds in
the manner that the report attributes to \pkg{one\_context}. The two
experimental synthesis programs have a certificate format of their own. The
two three-field programs read certificates of \cref{cert:def:three-field}, and
their controls, which read four-field certificates, are the global loop of
\pkg{progress\_independence} and the local loop of \pkg{inferred\_major}; the
two programs of \pkg{single\_binder} marked with the double dagger read
three-field certificates as well, and so do their reframed controls; all
other rows of $\mathcal U$ read four-field certificates. No package states a
control for the two programs of \pkg{context\_order}; by
\cref{cert:prop:compositions} they are the compact program of
\pkg{deterministic\_introduction} ($267/303/506$) and the loop profile of
\pkg{deterministic\_opening} ($263/276/580$) allocating first. The native
program of \pkg{three\_field} and its control satisfy the same typed contract
for the grammar with equality and $D$. Under T/ed$_1$ the programs
$S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f}$ and $S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f}$ and the readable form of
\pkg{validated\_three\_field} read three-field records, and their named controls
four-field packets, so these comparisons are of sizes only; so do
$S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$, $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$,
$S^{\mathrm{TN}}_{3\mathrm f,\mathrm E}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm r}$, $S^{3}_{\mathrm e}$ and
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{e,m}}$, the last three closing computations as leaves. All other rows
of T/ed$_1$ read four-field packets, those of \pkg{checked\_reduction},
\pkg{neutral\_modes}, \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots} and \pkg{scope\_phase} with
the neutral major $[\,]$ (\cref{cert:prop:eager-validated,cert:prop:eager-three,cert:thm:floor-phase}).

For the rows added from \pkg{retained\_premises} onwards: the control of
$516$ bytes is the program so named in \cref{cert:sec:later}; the controls of
$495$ and $666$ bytes are the programs (iv) and (v) of
\cref{cert:prop:compositions}, which \pkg{checker\_refinement},
\pkg{single\_binder} and \pkg{verified\_refinements} reproduce with identical
text; the control of $641$ bytes is the list variant of \pkg{context\_phase}.
The typed wrapper of \pkg{retained\_premises} is compared by its package with
the goal-validated program of \pkg{checked\_refinement}, which satisfies a
weaker contract; under its own contract the comparison is with the other rows
T/g. The program of $629$ bytes validates the goal but not the whole input and
has the code G/$\mathcal A^{-}$; the programs of $646$ and $654$ bytes validate
both. The control of \pkg{guarded\_register} uses the same strict interface,
logical rules, representation of variables and acceptance convention, but the
scalar iterator in place of the calculus $J$; it is the only control of the
table in another calculus, and the comparison is the package's. The packed
rows are not checkers in the sense of the measurement protocol
(\cref{cert:sec:representation}).

For the rows of \pkg{refinement}, \pkg{immediate\_application} and
\pkg{scope\_and\_frontier}: the controls of $487$ and $646$ bytes are the
anonymous-function program of \pkg{single\_binder} and the native program of
\pkg{checker\_refinement}, and the control of $492$ bytes is the
inverted-entry program of \pkg{verified\_refinements}, each reproduced with
identical text. The control of $862$ bytes is constructed by
\pkg{refinement} by attaching the validator of the wrapper of
\pkg{progress\_independence} to the program of $495$ bytes; it is a complete
program of the contract T/g and is listed as a row. The readable program of
\pkg{refinement} is measured without the removal of docstrings; the other
programs of these packages contain none, or, in \pkg{scope\_and\_frontier},
are measured with them removed. The two-field reference is the only program of its
code. \pkg{immediate\_application} lists the two first-line programs of
\pkg{single\_binder} as the bearers of its envelope, and
\pkg{scope\_and\_frontier} lists in addition the programs of $629$, $646$
and $654$ bytes; these rows appear above. The unsound program of
\cref{cert:prop:inclusive-floor} has no reported size.

For the rows of the second delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation},
\pkg{479\_predecessor57}, \pkg{immediate\_opening}, \pkg{zero\_test\_split},
\pkg{checked\_frontier}, \pkg{atomic\_seed\_refinement} and \pkg{reductions}:
the control of $629$ bytes is the composed native program of
\pkg{verified\_refinements}, the controls of $646$ and $487$ bytes are those of
the preceding paragraph, the control of $643$ bytes is the native program of
\pkg{inferred\_major} with the flag as an order, the controls of $637$ and
$479$ bytes are the programs of \pkg{immediate\_application}, and the controls
of the stream programs are the first-line programs of \pkg{single\_binder}, all
reproduced with identical text. The program of $478$ bytes reads four-field
packets and, as its last element, the goal together with an initial floor
(\cref{cert:prop:header}); it is the only program of the table with this input
format and is listed under R/$\mathcal U$, where its claims concern canonical
goals. The two programs of \pkg{immediate\_opening} other than the program of
$479$ bytes are measured with their docstrings, which the package states. The
compositions of \pkg{atomic\_seed\_refinement} with a one-line lexical front end
and the variant of $640$ bytes of \pkg{checked\_presentation} are not delivered
and have no row; neither have the unsound native programs of
\cref{cert:rem:header-native}. The programs under T/ed also satisfy the goal
contract for their grammar; they do not change the line for
G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ below. The control of $666$ bytes of \pkg{equation\_aliases}
is constructed by the package and is a complete program of T/ed
(\cref{cert:prop:bit-products}(a)); so is the simplified tree checker of
$\mathsf K$ of \pkg{alias\_back} under K. The delivered tree checker of
\pkg{conditional\_copy} ($646/748/1844$) is listed with the calculus
$\mathsf K$ in the chapter on witnesses and not here. The checkers of the
calculus with aliases and backward substitution of \pkg{alias\_back}, and the
checker of the calculus with restricted aliases of \pkg{equation\_aliases},
check calculi of other chapters and are not listed.

For the rows of \cref{cert:sec:leaves}: the packages \pkg{shared\_leaf},
\pkg{uniform\_terminals}, \pkg{smaller\_presentations},
\pkg{vacuous\_specialization}, \pkg{ast269\_successor\_rigidity} and
\pkg{selective\_normalization} compare at the revision \texttt{12197d86f6},
\pkg{computation\_leaves}, \pkg{export\_specialization},
\pkg{476\_byte\_presentation} and \pkg{bounded\_conversion} at
\texttt{2fa4637b3b}, \pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements} at
\texttt{167c769308}, and \pkg{whole\_json\_refinement} at
\texttt{6c6a164d21}; all measure under CPython 3.12.14; the controls of \pkg{whole\_json\_refinement} are the
stream programs of \pkg{computation\_leaves}, which read the same certificates
line by line. The programs with direct leaves read certificates in which a
computation closes its goal, and the uniform terminal programs certificates
with a packet at every call; the four-field programs among them carry the
header $[A,0]$. The control of the strict program of \pkg{smaller\_presentations}
is the strict byte profile of \pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements}, which
\pkg{shared\_leaf} also includes unchanged; the controls of the uniform-zero
programs encode zero by the scalar $0$ and are of other codes. The program of
$465$ bytes is the unsound program of \cref{cert:sec:validated}. The checkers
of other calculi, listed at the end of the table, belong to the chapters on
the kernel (AQE), on discharge (PD), on witnesses (V and newest Use) and on
computation (bounded local conversion); \pkg{476\_byte\_presentation} notes
that the inherited checker E3 is smaller than PD.

For the rows of \cref{cert:sec:offset,cert:sec:validated-terminal} and of
\pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker}: \pkg{offset472} and \pkg{orbit\_equality}
compare at the revision \texttt{6c6a164d21}, \pkg{checker\_refinements},
\pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker} and \pkg{validated\_leaves} at
\texttt{6874c85a4c}, and \pkg{scope\_refinement} at \texttt{a0687e8082}; all
measure under CPython 3.12.14. The control of the program of
\pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker} is the local stream program of
\pkg{shared\_leaf}; the control of \pkg{offset472} is the program of $473$ bytes
of \pkg{vacuous\_specialization}, whose goals use the encoding of
\cref{cert:def:enc}; the control of $S_2$ and $S_3$ is $S_1$ and that of $N_2$
is $N_1$, both reproduced with identical text. \pkg{checker\_refinements}
delivers the programs of $476$ bytes and of $269$ nodes with the texts listed
above and the controls $272/306/477$ and $255/271/606$, and \pkg{orbit\_equality}
the programs of $615$ and $613$ bytes.

For the rows of \pkg{468\_presentation}, \pkg{ast261}, \pkg{checked\_presentations}
and \pkg{root\_conversion\_raw}: the first compares at the revision
\texttt{dd308d5d93}, the other three at \texttt{31e1e92208}; all measure under
CPython 3.12.14, and the readable form and the validated companion of
\pkg{468\_presentation} are measured with their docstrings removed. The matched control that
\pkg{468\_presentation} names is the uniform terminal program of $469$ bytes, whose
goals use the encoding of \cref{cert:def:enc}; the program of the same code is
$P_{+}$ of \pkg{offset472}, which the package uses as the control of its proof.
The controls of \pkg{ast261} and of the compact part of \pkg{checked\_presentations}
are the stream programs of \pkg{shared\_leaf} with identical text; the control
of the whole-input program is $G^{\mathrm w}$, constructed by the package and
listed as a row. The controls of the strict programs are those of the article
(\cref{cert:prop:checked-strict}); the package names others. The control of
\pkg{root\_conversion\_raw} is the program of $477$ bytes of
\pkg{head\_conversion}, reproduced with identical text.

For the rows of \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf}, \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf},
\pkg{scalar\_constancy}, \pkg{direct\_packets} and \pkg{procedure\_presentation}:
the first three compare at the revision \texttt{78edba03ee}, \pkg{direct\_packets}
at \texttt{c2e762c43a} and \pkg{procedure\_presentation} at \texttt{dd308d5d93};
all measure under CPython 3.12.14. The control named for $T_{+}$ is the program
$T$ of $469$ bytes, with identical text, whose goals use the encoding of
\cref{cert:def:enc}; \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf} and \pkg{scalar\_constancy} also
reproduce $P_{+}$. The readable forms of \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf} and of
\pkg{direct\_packets} (\texttt{code/readable\_checker.py},
\texttt{code/checkers/readable\_checker.py}) have no reported size, and neither
have the unsound programs of \cref{cert:sec:validated}; they have no row. The
controls of $L'^{\mathrm w}$ and $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm m}$ are the stream
programs from which they are obtained, as for \pkg{whole\_json\_refinement};
\pkg{direct\_packets} compares them with the whole-input programs of that package
($248/277/615$ and $254/268/613$). The last element of the inputs of
$T_{\mathrm d}$ is $[A,0,m,s,u,t]$; $T_{\mathrm d}$ is the only program of the
table with this format and is listed under R/$\mathcal U$, where its claims
concern canonical goals. The controls of \pkg{procedure\_presentation} are
reproduced with identical text ($T_{+}$, $S_2$, $N_2$, $S^{\mathrm T}_1$,
$S^{\mathrm N}_1$ and $L$); its program with comments,
\texttt{code/checker\_strict\_annotated.py}, has the syntax tree of
$S_{2,\mathrm p}$ and no row of its own. $L_{\mathrm r}$ and $L'_{\mathrm r}$ are
included unchanged from the earlier package \pkg{return\_reservation}, whose
revision \pkg{procedure\_presentation} does not restate; the earlier package
states the minima of $242$ tokens and $262$ nodes of the stream programs at its
revision.

For the rows of \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers}, \pkg{prefix\_conditional} and
the third delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation}: all three compare at the
revision \texttt{1156099ffe} and measure under CPython 3.12.14. Their controls
are reproduced with identical text: $L_{\mathrm r}$
(\texttt{code/inherited\_tokens240.py} of \pkg{procedure\_presentation}) in all
three, $L_{\mathrm m}$ of \pkg{direct\_packets} in all three, $T_{+,\mathrm p}$ in
all three, and $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm m}$, $G^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm b}$ and
$S_{2,\mathrm p}$ in \pkg{prefix\_conditional}; \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers}
also reproduces $L'_{\mathrm r}$. The matched control of each row is the program
from which it is obtained in \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}; the packages compare
the programs that lower the node minimum with $L_{\mathrm m}$ ($252/259/595$) and
$L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm m}$ ($258/263/595$), and $L^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm c}$ with
$G^{\mathrm w}_{\mathrm b}$ ($245/272/591$), as comparisons of size. The
certificates of $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$ and $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$ are those of
their controls rotated by $\rho$. \pkg{prefix\_conditional} also reports sizes
after compression, which for $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$ ($313$ against $310$) and
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$ ($391$ against $387$) are larger than those of the
controls; as elsewhere they are omitted.

For the rows of \pkg{conditional\_image} and \pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement}:
both compare at the revision \texttt{95d3dd192e} and measure under CPython
3.12.14. \pkg{conditional\_image} reproduces with identical text $L_{\mathrm r}$,
$L_{\mathrm m}$ and $T_{+,\mathrm p}$ (\texttt{reference/control\_tokens240.py},
\texttt{control\_ast259.py}, \texttt{control\_bytes462.py}); its control of runs
for both programs is $L_{\mathrm r}$, and it compares $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ with
$L_{\mathrm m}$ ($252/259/595$) as a comparison of size, as
\pkg{prefix\_conditional} does. Its two rows repeat the rows of
\pkg{prefix\_conditional}, whose matched control of $L_{\mathrm{c,m}}$ is
$L_{\mathrm c}$. The controls of \pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement} are named in
its rows; its sizes after compression ($398$, $399$ and $392$ against $392$, $391$
and $391$) are larger than those of the controls, as the package states, and are
omitted.

For the rows of \pkg{checked\_reduction} and \pkg{validated\_three\_field}: both
compare at the revision \texttt{798856e4ef}, measure under CPython 3.12.14 and
reproduce their controls with identical text, \pkg{checked\_reduction} also
$L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ (\texttt{compact/control\_ast256.py}) and

shows that the new devices cost source: the control without them has $545$

programs are smaller than the earlier published minimum of $558$ only because
the control already is. In \pkg{prevalidated\_elimination} the saving of $19$
bytes of the byte profile is decomposed by the package into $11$ for the
exchange of steps and the folding of the major call, $6$ for the unguarded
test, and $2$ for the spelling of the missing image. The typed checkers and

contracts, and \pkg{validated\_presentation} and \pkg{scope\_kernel} state
explicitly that their figures are not to be set against it.

\subsection{What the measurement does not control}\label{cert:sec:representation}

The protocol of \cref{base:sec:measure} measures the source text of a complete
program. It is not invariant under a change of representation of the same
checking procedure, and three packages exhibit this.

\pkg{502\_scope\_audit} wraps the program of $502$ bytes in a module that
imports the standard compression and encoding libraries and executes the
decompressed content of one fixed string literal of $395$ characters, a
compressed stream of $316$ bytes that expands to the exact source. The whole
module, literal included, has $18$ tokens, $19$ syntax-tree nodes and $459$
bytes, below every coordinate of every checker of the table. The same wrapper
applied to the program of $506$ bytes also gives $18/19/459$, with a stream of
the same length, so the device gives the newer program no advantage over its
control. The checking procedure, its runs and its correctness are those of the
expanded program; the package checks the identity of the expanded text with the
direct program byte for byte before running its fixtures, and notes that the
extra frame of execution can change the behavior under a recursion limit. The
measurement counts the syntax tree of the wrapper and not that of the program
it executes, and it does not say how generic decoders that enter the trusted
runtime are to be charged; \pkg{orbit\_closure} states that embedding a
compressed program changes the accounting question and does not supply a
mathematical reduction.

\pkg{single\_binder} replaces, in the global and the local three-field
profiles, the reading of the whole standard input by the reading of its first
line. On a certificate written on one line both programs parse the same tree,
and every later operation is literally unchanged; every certificate has a
lossless one-line serialization, and a converter that performs no proof
processing supplies it. The accepted byte streams differ: later lines are
ignored and a certificate spread over several lines must be converted. The
change saves one token and one node and costs two bytes, and the same change
applied to the two controls gives programs identical to the candidates. These
rows carry the double dagger.

The second delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation} goes one step further and
reads the certificate as a sequence of lines. The first line holds the JSON
text of the goal, and each later line the JSON text of one packet, in the order
in which the run consumes the packets; the program reads and parses the next
line exactly when its control removes the next packet. Its two programs read
neither the whole input nor only its first line: they read as many lines as the
run requests and nothing after them. For an array
$\sigma=[p_n,\dots,p_1,A]$ of raw trees let $\Lambda(\sigma)$ be the sequence of
lines $A,p_1,\dots,p_n$, each written on one line.

\begin{proposition}[Certificates read one record per line]\label{cert:prop:stream}
Let $P$ be one of the two first-line three-field programs of
\pkg{single\_binder}, and $P'$ the program in which every removal of the last
element of the array parsed from the first line is replaced by the parsing of
the next line of the input.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item For every array $\sigma$ of integers and arrays, $P$ accepts the one-line
serialization of $\sigma$ exactly when $P'$ accepts $\Lambda(\sigma)$, with
the same run.
\item If $P'$ accepts an input and reads its lines $A,p_1,\dots,p_r$, then $P$
accepts the one-line serialization of $[p_r,\dots,p_1,A]$, with the same run.
\end{enumerate}
Hence $P$ and $P'$ accept the same goals.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Pair the states of the two programs before the first read of the goal. The
invariant is: all variables of the two programs have equal values, and the
remaining lines of the input of $P'$ begin with the elements of the remaining
array of $P$ in the reverse of their order in the array. The only operation
that touches the input is the request of the next value; in $P$ it removes and
returns the last element of the array, in $P'$ it parses the next line, and
the two values are equal. Removal from an empty array and reading past the
end of the input both fail. Every other operation is literally the same,
including the order of evaluation: a major call requests its packets at the
same point of the run, before the continuation, in both programs, since both
consume one shared source. Induction on the run gives (a). For (b), the run of
$P'$ requests exactly $r+1$ values; on the array $[p_r,\dots,p_1,A]$ the run of
$P$ makes the same requests, each of which returns the same value, and makes no
further request.
\end{proof}

The accepted inputs of $P'$ are not the images of those of $P$: lines after the
last line read are not read and may contain any text, including text that is
not JSON, whereas $P$ parses its whole first line; an unused prefix of the
array becomes an unused suffix of the stream; and a line that is read must hold
exactly one JSON value. An untrusted converter reverses an array and writes one
value per line; it is not imported by either program. The global and the local
programs keep their own naming of parameters. The programs have $244/272/608$
and $249/267/606$, against $248/277/609$ and $254/270/608$ for the first-line
programs; the same change applied to the whole-input three-field programs of
\pkg{three\_field} ($249/278/607$ and $255/271/606$), which differ from the
first-line programs only in their reader, gives the same two programs. Against
the whole-input programs the change saves five tokens and six nodes at the cost
of one byte, and six tokens and four nodes at no cost in bytes. These rows
carry the double dagger.

The framing by lines is due to the second delivery of
\pkg{checked\_presentation}. \pkg{absorbing\_predecessor} lists the same two
programs with identical text among its unchanged baselines, and
\pkg{boolean\_constancy}, which credits the framing to
\pkg{absorbing\_predecessor}, writes the import of the reader as the import of
all public names of the standard JSON module. None of these names coincides
with a name used by the programs, and the reader is the same function, so the
runs are unchanged; the programs have $244/272/604$ and $249/267/602$, four
bytes fewer than the stream programs and the same tokens and nodes.

Two further packages rewrite one expression of a stream program;
\pkg{fused\_iteration} and \pkg{pred50} take their controls from
\pkg{absorbing\_predecessor} and from the second delivery of
\pkg{checked\_presentation}, which have the same text.

\begin{proposition}[Two rewritings of the stream programs]\label{cert:prop:stream-rewrites}
On inputs whose values are integers and arrays:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item in the local stream program, the floor argument
$(k\defeq k-1)\land(s\lor\lvert a\rvert=2)\land k$ of the opening of the new
hypothesis may be replaced by $(k\defeq k-1)\cdot(1$ if $s$ else
$\lvert a\rvert=2)$; the runs agree up to the replacement of \texttt{False}
by $0$ inside trees;
\item in the global stream program, the expression ``$c(s,{*}g)\lor 3s$ if $s$
else $J$'', with $J$ the opening of the introduction, may be replaced by
$(s\land c(s,{*}g))\lor 3s\lor J$; the runs are the same.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) Before the decrement $k\le 0$, so $j\defeq k-1$ is a nonzero integer. If $s$
is true-valued both expressions have the value $j$, and neither evaluates
$\lvert a\rvert$. If $s$ is false-valued, both evaluate $\lvert a\rvert=2$; if it
holds both have the value $j$, and otherwise the old one has the value
\texttt{False} and the new one $0$. The decrement is evaluated first in both. The
two values are equal, are both false-valued, and behave alike under every
operation of the program (arithmetic, comparison, membership, the failed
traversal of a scalar by the opener); the program applies no exact-type test to
internal trees. Relating trees up to this replacement, related states read the
same lines and take the same branches, as in \cref{cert:thm:context-phase}.

(b) Every returning call of the global program returns the null value, since
its body is a loop without a return statement. If $s$ is true-valued, both
forms make the same major call; after it returns, both evaluate $3s$, which is
a nonzero integer or a nonempty array, so the new form does not evaluate $J$.
If $s$ is false-valued, it is $0$ or $[\,]$ on this domain; neither form makes
the call, the new form evaluates $3s$, which is $0$ or $[\,]$, false-valued and
without effect, and both evaluate $J$ once.
\end{proof}

The programs have $249/265/605$ (\pkg{fused\_iteration},
\texttt{code/checker\_refinement/checker.py}) against $249/267/606$, and
$244/273/607$ (\pkg{pred50}, \texttt{code/checker/checker607.py}) against
$244/272/608$. They carry the double dagger. \pkg{pred50} also records two
rejected variants: a neutral $0$ in every context with an unconditional major
call ($244/270/604$), which accepts the literal goal $0$ at once and calls the
checker on the source $[\,]$, so that it is not an exact rewriting, and an
early decrement, which rejects valid certificates; neither is delivered.

None of these observations is a mathematical simplification. They show that the
three coordinates depend on the charged interface (input framing, decoders,
the success message) as well as on the checking procedure, and that a
comparison is meaningful only between programs with the same interface; this
is why every row names a matched control.

The envelope under R/$\mathcal U$ has the following history. The audit of
\pkg{orbit\_closure} finds $256/276/506$ in the corpus that precedes the
packages of \cref{cert:sec:later}. The inferred major gives $253$ tokens and
$275$ nodes; the truth-valued result gives $503$ bytes; eager selection with
the remainder selector gives $502$ bytes; the reuse of the packet tail gives
$251$ tokens. The archive \pkg{context\_order}, by allocating first, gives
$273$ nodes and $499$ bytes; these two programs were not in the table before,
and the envelope $251/273/499$ that \pkg{three\_field} and \pkg{constantfree}
take as their baseline is attained by the global loop of
\pkg{progress\_independence} and by the two programs of
\pkg{context\_order}. Three fields give $249$ tokens and $271$ nodes;
\pkg{constantfree} gives $250$ tokens and $498$ bytes with four fields; the
composition (iv) of \cref{cert:prop:compositions} gives $495$ bytes. The three
attaining programs differ in more than size. The two three-field profiles
recognize arrays by attempted traversal and read certificates of a different
format; their relation to their controls is
\cref{cert:prop:three-relation}. The program of $495$ bytes uses the
zero-product test and four fields and allocates first; its accepted stacks
are not compared with those of its control (the remarks after
\cref{cert:prop:compositions}). All three accept the
same canonical goals, the closed theorems of $\mathcal U$, by
\cref{cert:thm:three-sound,cert:thm:three-complete,cert:prop:compositions}.
No program attains two of the three minima $249/271/495$, and no single
program is below the earlier envelope $251/273/499$ in all coordinates; the
local three-field profile is below its own control in all three. Under T/ed
the native program of \pkg{three\_field} is below its control
$344/393/673$ in all three coordinates.

The packages of \cref{cert:sec:after-first} change only the byte coordinate
under R/$\mathcal U$. The inverted entry test of \pkg{verified\_refinements}
gives $492$ bytes with $267$ tokens and $298$ nodes, below the program of
$495$ bytes in two coordinates and equal in the third; the truth-valued
result of \pkg{checker\_refinement} and \pkg{single\_binder} gives $488$
bytes and costs $4$ tokens and $5$ nodes; the anonymous function of
\pkg{single\_binder} gives $487$ bytes and costs $9$ tokens and $9$ nodes. The
three programs have exactly the runs of their control
(\cref{cert:prop:truthy-first,cert:prop:inverted}), so their accepted stacks
are those of the program of $495$ bytes. The envelope is $249/271/487$; no
program attains two of its minima, and none is below $251/273/499$ in all
coordinates. The retained major of \pkg{retained\_premises} ($265/301/513$)
improves its own control of $516$ bytes in two coordinates and does not
change the envelope. Admitting the programs that read only the first line
would give $248/270/487$; those two gains come from the input framing alone
(\cref{cert:sec:representation}). Under T/ed the phase by an empty context is
below its control in all three coordinates and the native program of
\pkg{checker\_refinement} in bytes only; under G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ the composed
program of \pkg{verified\_refinements} is below its control $325/375/641$ in
all three coordinates. Under S the one program of \pkg{guarded\_register} is
below its control in the scalar iterator in all three coordinates; S is not
comparable with any other contract of the table. Smaller figures for T/eqd are
reported by packages outside this chapter. Since T/g is a stronger contract
than G/$\mathcal U$, the line for T/g also bounds the latter.

The packages \pkg{immediate\_application}, \pkg{refinement} and
\pkg{scope\_and\_frontier} change the envelope as follows. Under
R/$\mathcal U$ the immediate application of \cref{cert:prop:immediate}(a)
gives $479$ bytes with $272$ tokens and $304$ nodes; it is below its control
of $487$ bytes in all three coordinates and has one token more than the
program of $488$ bytes. The envelope is $249/271/479$, attained by three
programs, and $250/273/479$ among programs reading four fields. With the
programs reading only the first line it would be $248/270/479$, the triple
that \pkg{immediate\_application} states as its envelope;
\pkg{scope\_and\_frontier} states $248/270/487$, by the same convention, at
the same revision without the program of $479$ bytes. Its program of $500$
bytes, the third delivery of the program of $488$ bytes by \pkg{refinement}
and the readable programs do not
change the envelope, and the two-field reference is the only program under
its own code. Under T/ed the immediate application of
\cref{cert:prop:immediate}(b) gives $637$ bytes with one token more than its
control, and the envelope is $334/387/637$. Under T/g the program on the
kernel of $488$ bytes and its matched control give $419/469/855$; the matched
control is below the wrapper of \pkg{progress\_independence} in all three
coordinates, and the program of $855$ bytes is below that wrapper in bytes
only. So the earlier envelope $421/471/869$ under T/g is improved in all three
coordinates by one program, which is a control and not a delivered result;
\pkg{refinement} lists both programs and does not remark on this. Under
R/$\mathcal U$ and T/ed no program attains two of the minima, and no
delivered program of the three packages is below the earlier envelope of its
contract in all three coordinates, as all three packages state.

The packages of \cref{cert:sec:native-refinements} and the second delivery of
\pkg{checked\_presentation} change the envelope as follows (the program of
$479$ bytes of \pkg{alias\_back}, which states $248/270/479$, is that of
\pkg{immediate\_application}). Under
R/$\mathcal U$ the program of \pkg{zero\_test\_split} gives $478$ bytes with
$271$ tokens and $305$ nodes, one token less and one node more than its control
of $479$ bytes; the envelope is $249/271/478$, attained by three programs, and
$250/273/478$ among programs reading four fields. The package states
$248/270/478$, admitting the first-line programs. Without the program of $478$
bytes, whose last element carries an initial floor, the envelope is
$249/271/479$ as before; \pkg{immediate\_opening} and \pkg{479\_predecessor57}
state $248/270/479$, by the same convention as \pkg{immediate\_application}.
Admitting the programs marked with the double dagger, the minima would be
$244/267/478$; the second delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation} states
$244/267/479$, at a revision without the program of $478$ bytes, and its
token and node gains come from the framing of \cref{cert:prop:stream}.
\pkg{checked\_frontier} and \pkg{reductions} state $248/270/487$, at a revision
without the program of $479$ bytes. Under G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ the delivered
programs give $607$ bytes (\pkg{479\_predecessor57}) and $370$ nodes
(\pkg{atomic\_seed\_refinement}), and the envelope is $324/370/607$, attained by
three programs; the parity program is below its control of $643$ bytes in all
three coordinates, and every other new program of this contract improves only
the byte coordinate of the program of $629$ bytes, with more tokens and nodes.
Under T/ed the parity program of \pkg{atomic\_seed\_refinement} is below its
control of $637$ bytes and below the program of $646$ bytes in all three
coordinates and gives the byte minimum $628$. The programs of $637$ and $641$
bytes of \pkg{equation\_aliases} are below their control of $666$ bytes in all
three coordinates, and the program with the reader in the initial call gives
the node minimum $386$, one below the $387$ of the programs of $654$ and $628$
bytes; the program of $633$ bytes has two tokens and four bytes fewer than its
control of $637$ bytes and the same number of nodes. The envelope is $334/386/628$, attained by three programs, and no
program attains two of its minima. The package states $338/386/633$, from its
own three programs. The program of $645$ bytes of \pkg{checked\_frontier}
improves only the byte coordinate of its control. Under T/g the strict
companion of \pkg{immediate\_opening} does not change the envelope. Under K
the two-field stream of \pkg{alias\_back} is below its matched tree control in
all three coordinates; the two read different certificate formats.

\pkg{absorbing\_orbit}, \pkg{absorbing\_predecessor} and
\pkg{boolean\_constancy} change the envelope as follows. Under
R/$\mathcal U$ the eager selector of \pkg{boolean\_constancy} gives a second
program of $478$ bytes ($274/304/478$), two tokens more than its control and
the same number of nodes, and incomparable with the program of
\pkg{zero\_test\_split}; the envelope $249/271/478$ is unchanged and is now
attained also among programs whose last element is the goal. With the
programs of the double dagger the minima remain $244/267/478$; the wildcard
import saves four bytes of each stream program and changes neither tokens nor
nodes. \pkg{boolean\_constancy} states $244/267/478$ by this convention, and
\pkg{absorbing\_predecessor}, which delivers no new checker, $244/267/479$.
Under T/ed the program of \pkg{absorbing\_orbit} ($343/390/632$) and the
native eager selector ($346/392/636$) improve their control of $637$ bytes,
the first in all three coordinates and the second in bytes only; neither
changes the envelope $334/386/628$.

\pkg{checked\_reductions}, the second delivery of \pkg{refinement},
\pkg{zero\_alias}, \pkg{fused\_iteration} and \pkg{pred50} change the envelope
as follows. Under R/$\mathcal U$ the composition of the header with the eager
selector gives $477$ bytes with $273$ tokens and $305$ nodes, two tokens more
than its control of $478$ bytes and the same nodes; the envelope is
$249/271/477$, attained by three programs, and $249/271/478$ among programs
whose last element is the goal. With the programs of the double dagger the
minima are $244/265/477$; the program of \pkg{fused\_iteration} lowers the node
minimum of the stream programs from $267$ to $265$, and that of \pkg{pred50}
saves one byte and costs one node against its control. Under
G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ the compositions give the envelope $321/369/596$, attained
by three programs; the program of $618$ bytes is below the program of $629$
bytes in all three coordinates, and so is the program of $622$ bytes, which
attains the node minimum. Under T/ed the compositions give the envelope
$330/379/621$, attained by two programs; the program of $641$ bytes of
\pkg{checked\_reductions} attains two of the three minima and is below the
phase by an empty context in all three coordinates, and the program of $621$
bytes is below its control of $628$ bytes in all three coordinates. The
packages state $244/267/477$ (\pkg{checked\_reductions}), $330/379/623$ and
$323/369/600$ (\pkg{checked\_reductions}, for T/ed and G/$\mathcal A^{-}$),
$330/380/621$ and $321/369/596$ (\pkg{refinement}), $249/265/605$ as a single
program (\pkg{fused\_iteration}) and $244/267/478$ unchanged (\pkg{pred50}),
each at its own revision. The program of \pkg{zero\_alias} ($331/373/625$) is
the only program of T/ed$_1$; it is below the earlier envelope $334/386/628$ of
T/ed in all three coordinates, as the package states, but it is not below the
present envelope $330/379/621$ in tokens or bytes, and the two contracts encode
the public zero differently (\cref{cert:prop:zero-alias}), so no program of
one contract supersedes a program of the other. The program of
\pkg{tolerant\_certificates} ($315/361/603$) is the only program of
Gt/$\mathcal A^{-}$; it had fewer tokens and nodes than every program of
G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ and T/ed at that point (the native node profile of
\cref{cert:prop:abstraction}, with $358$ nodes, has since fewer nodes) and
more bytes than the program of $596$ bytes, and
since its contract differs from both (\cref{cert:thm:tolerant}) it is not
compared with them. The program of $596$ bytes keeps its row under
G/$\mathcal A^{-}$: that contract states correctness for integer and array
certificates only, and the failure of termination on inputs with nonempty
strings, which \pkg{tolerant\_certificates} records for it, lies outside that
statement, as it does for the program of $629$ bytes.

The packages of \cref{cert:sec:leaves} change the envelope as follows. Under
R/$\mathcal U$ the program of $477$ bytes of \pkg{head\_conversion}, now
listed, has one token fewer and one node more than the program of $477$ bytes
of \pkg{checked\_reductions}. The direct leaves give $474$ bytes (four fields)
and, with the double dagger, $243$ tokens and $264$ nodes, which is the triple
$243/264/474$ that \pkg{computation\_leaves} states against $244/265/477$; the
eager selector gives $476$ bytes on the program of $477$ bytes and $473$ bytes
on the program of $474$ bytes, each with two more tokens than its control. The
uniform terminals give $469$ bytes, with two more tokens and two fewer nodes
than the control of $474$ bytes, and, with the double dagger, $242$ tokens and
$262$ nodes; \pkg{shared\_leaf} states $242/262/469$ and
\pkg{uniform\_terminals}, without the program of $262$ nodes, $242/263/469$,
both by this convention. \pkg{smaller\_presentations}, at the same revision
without these two packages, states $242/264/473$, and
\pkg{vacuous\_specialization} $243/264/473$. Among programs without the double
dagger, the whole-input reader gives $248$ tokens and $268$ nodes, and the
floor product on the whole-input local three-field program $269$ nodes;
\pkg{whole\_json\_refinement} states $248/268/473$ and
\pkg{ast269\_successor\_rigidity} and \pkg{selective\_normalization}
$249/269/477$ for this group, each at its own revision. The envelope is
$248/268/469$, attained by three programs; no program attains two of its
minima, and no single program is below the earlier envelope $249/271/477$ in
all coordinates: the program of $469$ bytes has $272$ tokens. Under
G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ the bitwise depth gate and the identity test with
\texttt{False} give $317/358/594$, attained by three programs of
\pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements}, each below its control in all three
coordinates except the node profile, which has the tokens of its control and
more bytes; \pkg{476\_byte\_presentation} states $319/367/594$ from its own
programs. Under T/ed the same changes give $328/368/616$, attained by three
programs; the token profile is below its control in all three coordinates, the
byte profile has the tokens of its control and fewer nodes and bytes, and the
node profile has more tokens and bytes than its control; \pkg{476\_byte\_presentation} states $328/378/619$. The direct-leaf
native and strict programs of \pkg{computation\_leaves} are larger than their
controls. Under T/ed$_1$ the uniform-zero program of $608$ bytes and the
program of \pkg{zero\_alias} are incomparable, and the envelope is
$331/373/608$; under the new code G$_1$/$\mathcal A^{-}$ the envelope is
$328/369/581$. \pkg{smaller\_presentations} compares these programs with
$594$ and $616$ bytes and states the byte improvements $594\to 581$ ($587$
without the identity with unary plus) and $616\to 608$; these compare
different encodings of the public zero (\cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}). In none
of these contracts does one program attain all minima.

The packages of \cref{cert:sec:offset,cert:sec:validated-terminal},
\pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker}, \pkg{checker\_refinements} and
\pkg{orbit\_equality} change the envelope as follows. Under R/$\mathcal U$ the
programs without the double dagger are unchanged: those of
\pkg{checker\_refinements} and \pkg{orbit\_equality} are programs already listed.
\pkg{checker\_refinements} states $249/269/476$ for the whole-input programs and
$244/265/476$ with the stream programs, at a revision without the programs of
$469$ and $473$ bytes, and \pkg{orbit\_equality} the improvements $249\to 248$ and

\pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker}, which has five tokens fewer, five nodes more
and ten bytes more than its control; the package states
$241/262/469$ by this convention. The program of \pkg{offset472} ($271/307/472$)

at a revision without the program of $469$ bytes and across the two encodings;
the program has one token fewer and one node and three bytes more than the
program of $469$ bytes of R/$\mathcal U$. Under T/ed$_1$ the envelope becomes
$322/364/606$, attained by three programs, each of which improves one
coordinate: $S^{\mathrm T}_1$ and $S^{\mathrm N}_1$ are below their T/ed controls
in all three coordinates, a comparison across the encodings of zero, and $S_2$ and

Under G$_1$/$\mathcal A^{-}$, $N_2$ saves two bytes against $N_1$ with one node
more; with the programs of T/ed$_1$ the envelope is $322/364/579$. In none of these

Under T/ed$_1$ the envelope becomes $319/358/605$, attained by three programs,

\paragraph{Superseded programs.} A program of the table is \emph{superseded}
if another complete program of the table with the same contract code, and
with the same recognition of arrays (both or neither marked with a dagger), is
no larger in each of the three coordinates and smaller in at least one;
programs marked with the double dagger are not compared. Under
R/$\mathcal U$ the following are superseded: the byte program of
\pkg{three\_field} ($267/299/495$, \texttt{code/checker\_bytes.py}), and with
it the programs that it superseded, namely the compact program of
\pkg{deterministic\_introduction} ($267/303/506$), the two truth-valued
programs of \pkg{inferred\_major} ($278/310/503$, $278/309/505$), the eager
program of \pkg{frontier\_review} ($268/306/513$), the program of $502$ bytes
of \pkg{checked\_presentation}, \pkg{progress\_independence},
\pkg{crossed\_uniqueness} and \pkg{502\_scope\_audit} ($269/301/502$,
\texttt{code/checker\_502.py} in the last) and the eager program of $503$
bytes of \pkg{progress\_independence} and \pkg{crossed\_uniqueness}
($270/303/503$), all by the inverted-entry program of
\pkg{verified\_refinements} ($267/298/492$, \texttt{compact/checker.py});
the remainder program of \pkg{smaller\_envelope} ($264/304/515$), the
remainder program of \pkg{crossed\_uniqueness} and \pkg{502\_scope\_audit}
($266/301/505$, \texttt{code/checker\_505.py} in the latter), the byte
program of \pkg{context\_order} ($265/301/499$) and the retained-major
program of \pkg{retained\_premises} ($265/301/513$,
\texttt{code/checker\_retained\_bytes.py}), by the byte program of
\pkg{constantfree} ($264/299/498$). Newly superseded are: the
anonymous-function program of \pkg{single\_binder} ($276/308/487$,
\texttt{code/bytes487.py}), by the immediate application of
\pkg{immediate\_application} ($272/304/479$, \texttt{code/checker479.py});
and, by the inverted-entry program of \pkg{verified\_refinements}
($267/298/492$, \texttt{compact/checker.py}), the program of

Newly superseded with the packages of \cref{cert:sec:native-refinements} is

\texttt{code/checker\_readable.py}), by the immediate application
($272/304/479$). The programs of $478$, $479$, $488$ and $492$ bytes (the

superseded: each pair of them is incomparable (the program of $478$ bytes of

$L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ carry the double dagger and are not compared, and their controls
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi}$ (\texttt{reference/checker\_sorted\_556.py} of \pkg{scope\_free\_audit}) and
$L_{\mathrm f}$ (\texttt{scope\_free/control239.py}) keep their status. Under R$_{\vdash}$/$\mathcal U$
every program carries the double dagger, and nothing is superseded; the control $L^{\vdash}_{\mathrm c}$
(\texttt{compact\_refinement/code/control\_tokens238.py} of \pkg{composition}) keeps its status. The
other programs that the packages carry as controls (\texttt{controls/} of \pkg{checker\_research},
\texttt{reference/} of \pkg{stack\_clock}, \texttt{reference/frontier/} of \pkg{composition}) are programs
of the table reproduced with identical text and keep their status; among them the copies of
$S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\tau}$ (\texttt{controls/exact\_threshold.py} of \pkg{checker\_research})
and of $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ (\texttt{reference/frontier/intrinsic\_exact.py} of \pkg{composition}) are
copies of programs superseded before.

\section{Further questions}\label{cert:sec:questions}

The packages state the following questions, limits and unproved claims. Items
that several packages raise are merged.

\begin{question}[Verification of the checkers]
Formalize the sorted reading, the opening lemma
(\cref{cert:lem:opening}), the zero-instance supply
(\cref{cert:lem:supply,cert:lem:zeroinstance}), the invariants of successful
runs and the compilation of derivations, for the actual programs on raw
input and not for a model restricted to canonical input, and connect them to
the derivability relation of the Lean development. All soundness and
completeness proofs of this chapter are written proofs; no package verifies a
program in a proof assistant. A smaller target is an operational semantics
of the fragment of the host language used by the programs, enough to certify
the simulations of \cref{cert:sec:after-first,cert:sec:context-floor}
(consumption of packets, order of evaluation, immutability of contexts, the
boundary of scopes, the binding of assignment expressions in comprehensions
and anonymous functions); a suite of fixtures is not a substitute. A formal
simulation of the raw codec, the input stream, the two phases of scope and
the public grammar would also make later changes easier to assess; formal
equivalences between calculi do not verify the control flow of a program.
(\pkg{certified\_zero}, \pkg{uniform\_zero},
\pkg{neutral\_scope}, \pkg{kernel\_refinement}, \pkg{compact\_update},
\pkg{validated\_presentation}, \pkg{checked\_refinement},
\pkg{scope\_kernel}, \pkg{checker\_refinement},
\pkg{immediate\_application}, \pkg{refinement}, \pkg{scope\_and\_frontier},
and the packages of \cref{cert:sec:native-refinements} and the second delivery
of \pkg{checked\_presentation}, none of which formalizes the reader, the
framing of the input or the order of evaluation on which
\cref{cert:prop:stream,cert:prop:native-append} rely; and \pkg{conditional\_image},
which asks to connect the raw opener, its behavior on exceptions, the allocation of
fresh names and the guarded public decoder to the formalized native calculus;
\pkg{exact\_threshold}, \pkg{local\_validation}, \pkg{retained\_validation} and
\pkg{reconstructed\_introduction}, which ask for the invariant of successful calls together with the
JSON values of the reader, Boolean equality, the order of the scans across the major call and the
irrelevance of unused fields, in particular that scans without an image are typed and that
floating-point holes give the same template to both openings; and \pkg{structural\_parameters},
which asks to connect the opaque opener and the stream protocol, with malformed annotations, the
replacement of Booleans, parsing and the consumption of a record by an early terminal, to the
formalized public calculus; \pkg{scope\_free\_audit}, which asks for a formal theorem on its stream
protocol and its compiler of certificates, including the repair of collisions of dormant names;
\pkg{stack\_clock}, which asks to formalize the opener and the successful call trees of the control and to
prove the invariant of \cref{cert:prop:same-runs}(c) against a specified execution semantics; and
\pkg{checker\_research}, whose proof of \cref{cert:prop:same-runs}(a) concerns one expression and
imports the correctness of its control.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[One program for all coordinates]
Is there, under a fixed contract, one complete program that is below the
current minima in tokens, syntax-tree nodes and bytes simultaneously, and in
particular one that recognizes arrays by an exact test?
(\pkg{certified\_zero}, \pkg{prevalidated\_elimination},
\pkg{kernel\_refinement}, \pkg{checked\_refinement}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Lower bounds]
Is there a class of presentations, with a fixed implementation language,
input contract, treatment of malformed input and charged dependencies, for
which a lower bound on checker size can be proved? The recorded sizes are
upper bounds attained by particular programs, and a census of existing
programs is not a lower bound. A benchmark of this kind should report the
size of certificates and the work of checking together with the size of the
checker; \pkg{three\_field} adds understandable source and resource usage to
such a comparison, since the present measure does not order mathematical
concepts uniquely. (\pkg{uniform\_zero}, \pkg{neutral\_scope},
\pkg{validated\_presentation}, \pkg{scope\_and\_closure}, \pkg{three\_field},
\pkg{conditional\_image}, which asks for a restricted family of presentations or
implementations with a provable lower bound and for the size of certificates to be
reported beside the size of the checker; \pkg{304\_token\_refinement}, which asks for a
class fixing the runtime, the permitted libraries, the input grammar and the behavior
on malformed input; \pkg{neutral\_modes} and \pkg{scope\_phase}, which ask for the
length of certificates, the depth of recursion and the time of checking on a stated
family of theorems as separate measures, since direct leaves repeat computations that
a retained hypothesis shares; \pkg{exact\_threshold}, which asks for the growth of certificates, the
time of checking and the structure of the canonical compiler beside the three coordinates;
\pkg{local\_validation}, which asks which lower bounds can be proved once the language, the
primitives of the runtime, the semantic interface and the contract of validation are fixed; and
\pkg{structural\_parameters}, which asks for a measure on an abstract machine; \pkg{checker\_research},
which asks to fix a finite instruction language and a contract of validation and prove a minimum within
it; and \pkg{scope\_free\_audit}, which asks for a benchmark that requires a correctness certificate for
each candidate checker and reports the size of the program, the size of certificates and the time of
checking separately.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Removing a rule and shrinking a validated checker]
Can a rule or a term constructor be removed, with every omitted instance
recovered and both formula round trips of \cref{base:thm:bridge} preserved,
in such a way that a fully validated checker under a fixed contract also
becomes smaller? The packages of this chapter re-encode an unchanged basis.
(\pkg{uniform\_zero}, \pkg{neutral\_scope}, \pkg{compact\_update},
\pkg{kernel\_refinement}, \pkg{validated\_presentation},
\pkg{checked\_refinement}, \pkg{conditional\_image},
\pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement}; the last two state that their programs remove
no rule and give no simpler basis, as do \pkg{304\_token\_refinement},
\pkg{neutral\_modes}, \pkg{scope\_phase} and \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots}, which
ask the question again: the first whether a change of the native basis, and not an
exchange of tokens for bytes, can lower a fully validated checker in all three
coordinates, the last that a complete checker of the new basis, with its contract of
syntax, be measured, since counting families of rules or moving a checker into an
uncounted library does not decide it; \pkg{exact\_threshold}, \pkg{local\_validation},
\pkg{retained\_validation}, \pkg{reconstructed\_introduction} and \pkg{structural\_parameters} state
that their programs remove no rule, and the first asks for a reduction of the basis that surpasses
full normalization, deterministic witnesses, variable-only substitution on normal forms and the
three-family clause presentations rather than renaming them; \pkg{checker\_research},
\pkg{scope\_free\_audit} and \pkg{stack\_clock} state that their programs remove no rule and ask
whether another arithmetic basis can simplify substitution, freshness and the recognition of
computations together, with both formula round trips and the comparison of domains shown and every
decoder and validator measured, since fewer displayed axioms need not mean less checking code.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Constructed majors]
Can the construction of the major from the fields of the packet
(\cref{cert:def:uzero}) be obtained without the six additional bytes of
source that it costs against a matched control, and without an unaccounted
condition on witnesses or a changed induction rule? (\pkg{uniform\_zero}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Definitional zero computation]
Is there a layout of binders in which the reduction of $\It{0}{a}{v}{h}$ to
$a$ needs no lowering of indices, so that the zero computation can be made
definitional at a lower total cost than the dispatch it removes?
(\pkg{uniform\_zero}, \pkg{prevalidated\_elimination}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Closures as a separate kind of resource]
Can closures be represented so that a certificate cannot forge them and a
raw tree does not read as a closure in one use and as an object formula in
another, at a lower total cost, so that one simpler dispatcher serves all
eliminations and the introduction? \Cref{cert:prop:closure-progress} shows
that the present identification forces the test on the root of the matrix.
(\pkg{kernel\_refinement}, \pkg{checked\_refinement}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A checkable class for the closure criterion]
Is there a class of matrices that satisfies the criterion of
\cref{cert:thm:criterion}, is cheaper to validate than the guard
\eqref{cert:eq:guard}, and still gives full expressiveness of the public
language? Can a root-determined introduction coexist with the full language
with equality, $Q$ and $D$ under the ordinary readings, at a lower
total cost than the witness-selected rule? The equation $y=\Suc 0$ excludes
the direct replacement of cases by progress, not every redesign.
(\pkg{compact\_update}, \pkg{checked\_refinement}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Scope carried by the certificate]
Would a tree-shaped certificate that allocates opaque eigenparameters when a
binder is entered, so that raw input cannot name them, simplify the freshness
argument at a lower total size? Can the remaining length of the certificate
serve as the floor if the allocation is given a separate phase?
(\pkg{compact\_update}, \pkg{prevalidated\_elimination}.) The second question is answered for the
reservation at entry by \cref{cert:prop:same-runs}(c) (\pkg{stack\_clock}): with the goal opened before
its packet is removed, the number of unread packets less its initial value is the floor. The program is
larger than its control in all three coordinates; \pkg{stack\_clock} asks whether another
representation can use the same invariant and be smaller than one complete control in all three
coordinates.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Terms as atomic assertions]
Can an equation $s=t$ be replaced by an assertion $\delta(s,t)=0$ for a fixed
distance term $\delta$ at a lower total trusted cost, when the expansion of
$\delta$ and the proofs of reflection and of the round trips are counted?
(\pkg{validated\_presentation}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Whole-certificate typing during the kernel run]
\Cref{cert:lem:scope,cert:thm:fused} let the first opening of the kernel check
the scope and the grammar of the goal. Can the exact typing of the whole
certificate, including unused packets, be shared with the kernel in the same
way? (\pkg{kernel\_refinement} asks for the sharing of public syntax
validation with the opening traversal; for the goal this is answered by the
results of \cref{cert:sec:validated}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[The envelope of \cref{cert:sec:envelope}]
Is there one complete program under the raw contract for $\mathcal U$ that is
below $248$ tokens, $268$ nodes and $469$ bytes at once (or, with the framing
by lines, $241/262/469$), or at least one that attains two of these minima?
The same question was asked by the packages of \cref{cert:sec:leaves} for the
earlier envelope $249/271/477$ and its variants; each of them states that it
does not achieve it. In particular, can one three-field program
improve all three coordinates of the earlier envelope $251/273/499$, and is
there a three-field byte program that allocates first and selects eagerly?
The records of $477$, $478$, $479$, $488$ and $492$ bytes still read four fields, the
token and node gains of three fields are offset by longer byte encodings, and
the byte gains of \cref{cert:sec:after-first} cost tokens and nodes relative to
the program of $495$ bytes, except the inverted entry test, which costs
neither; the immediate application improves its own control in all three
coordinates but not the program of $488$ bytes in tokens, and the initial floor
of \cref{cert:prop:header} saves a token and a byte and costs a node. The
framing of \cref{cert:prop:stream} lowers tokens and nodes of the three-field
programs but changes the accepted inputs. The same question is open under
complete input validation, where the envelope $328/368/616$ under T/ed is
attained by three programs, and the envelope $419/469/855$ under T/g by two
programs, and under the goal contract, where $317/358/594$ under
G/$\mathcal A^{-}$ is attained by three programs; under T/ed$_1$
($300/337/580$), G$_1$/$\mathcal A^{-}$ ($300/337/573$), T/ed$_1^{\sharp}$ ($358/408/687$)
and T/ed$_1^{\natural}$ ($317/358/614$) by three programs each, under
G$^{\natural}_{\top}$/$\mathcal A^{-}$ ($303/338/576$) and T/ed$_1^{+2}$ ($299/335/583$) by three,
and under G$_{1,\top}$/$\mathcal A^{-}$ ($288/331/556$) by two.
Under the shifted encoding of \cref{cert:def:offset} there was one program
($271/307/472$) when the last question was asked: can the offset be combined with the
uniform terminals of the program of $469$ bytes? $T_{+}$ of
\cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal} does this, and the envelope there is now
$266/298/460$, attained by two programs. No package answers the other questions. (\pkg{inferred\_major},
\pkg{smaller\_envelope}, \pkg{frontier\_review}, \pkg{checked\_presentation},
\pkg{progress\_independence}, \pkg{three\_field}, \pkg{constantfree},
\pkg{retained\_premises}, \pkg{checker\_refinement}, \pkg{single\_binder},
\pkg{502\_scope\_audit}, \pkg{verified\_refinements},
\pkg{immediate\_application}, \pkg{refinement}, \pkg{scope\_and\_frontier},
\pkg{checked\_presentation}, \pkg{479\_predecessor57}, \pkg{immediate\_opening},
\pkg{zero\_test\_split}, \pkg{checked\_frontier},
\pkg{atomic\_seed\_refinement}, \pkg{reductions}, \pkg{equation\_aliases},
\pkg{alias\_back}, \pkg{absorbing\_orbit}, \pkg{absorbing\_predecessor},
\pkg{boolean\_constancy}, \pkg{zero\_alias}, \pkg{checked\_reductions},
\pkg{fused\_iteration}, \pkg{pred50}, \pkg{offset472},
\pkg{checker\_refinements}, \pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker},
\pkg{scope\_refinement}, \pkg{validated\_leaves}, \pkg{orbit\_equality}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Soundness without the alphabet precondition]
Is the program of $596$ bytes, or any program of the goal contract with the
array test $a\cdot 0\neq 0$, sound for every input accepted by the JSON reader?
An audit in \pkg{tolerant\_certificates} sketches a successful-run induction for
the program of $596$ bytes and finds no accepted false goal in $44416$ bounded
trials ($1448$ of them ending at the recursion limit), but proves no theorem;
such a program would still not terminate on some inputs with nonempty strings.
Can the erasure of \cref{cert:thm:tolerant} be combined with the products of bits
and the program of $596$ bytes so that one program of the tolerant contract is
also below $596$ bytes? (\pkg{tolerant\_certificates}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[The defined alias]
Can the identification of \cref{cert:prop:zero-alias} reduce the compact
programs, whose goal grammar is not validated, so that the saving in the
validator does not disappear? Can a validator recognize exactly the image of the
canonical encoder, including the absence of references to dummy slots, so that
completeness holds for every accepted spelling? Can the soundness of runs,
with the reserved constants and the order of contexts, be mechanized against
the programs? (\pkg{zero\_alias}.) The second question is answered by the validator of
\cref{cert:prop:exact-image} (\pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots}), at the cost of
$S^{\mathrm x}$ in \cref{cert:sec:sizes}, and \cref{cert:cor:second-dummy} shows that
without such a validator completeness fails for the spelling \texttt{[[5],[1]]} of
$\Suc 0=\Suc 0$. Whether \texttt{[[3],[1]]}, which refers to the inner dummy slot of a
successor, has a certificate is not decided; can a normalization or an identification
of dummy references be proved at a cost below that of the exact validator?
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Retained majors with a global floor]
Is there a sound program with a global floor and retained majors? The proof of
\cref{cert:thm:retained-sound} uses that both openings of (3r) are evaluated
at one floor; a global-floor program would have to fix both instances before
the call on the minor premise or receive another argument. Can the append
property of \cref{cert:thm:retained-complete} be extended from compiler-normal
certificates to arbitrary accepted runs, and can the translation of
certificates of the control be made total on aliased certificates? The
package delivers no global program and claims neither extension.
\Cref{cert:prop:delayed-equality,cert:prop:delayed-closure} justify the
delayed opening of the target after a minor proof at a lower floor, through the
existential closure of the target; the other branches of such a program, the
repair of collisions, the invariants of a global floor and completeness remain
to be proved, and \pkg{checked\_frontier} delivers no such program.
(\pkg{retained\_premises}, \pkg{checked\_frontier}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A measure invariant under representation]
How are encoded programs, generic decoders admitted to the trusted runtime,
the framing of the input and the success message to be charged, so that the
measure does not reward a change of representation of one checking procedure
(\cref{cert:sec:representation})? A measure that the packed programs of
\pkg{502\_scope\_audit} do not beat, and under which the one-line framing of
\pkg{single\_binder} is neutral, would make the comparison of
\cref{cert:sec:sizes} independent of these choices. \pkg{scope\_and\_frontier}
proposes to publish separate envelopes for canonical input, for validated
public goals and for validated arbitrary input, and to identify a smaller
reader or a change of framing separately from a new presentation. The framing
by lines of \cref{cert:prop:stream} and the initial floor carried by the goal
in \cref{cert:prop:header} are further changes of the interface that the
measure rewards. (\pkg{502\_scope\_audit}, \pkg{single\_binder},
\pkg{orbit\_closure}, \pkg{scope\_and\_frontier}, \pkg{checked\_presentation},
\pkg{zero\_test\_split}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Reductions that are not rearrangements]
Can a new calculus, rather than a rearrangement of expressions of the host
language, reduce the fully validated programs of $628$ and $654$ bytes (the
program of $628$ bytes, like those of $637$ and $645$ bytes, rewrites the
program of $646$ bytes)? Two
directions are named. Focused use of a clause, fused with the immediate
opening of its paired conclusion, could remove an arbitrary paired major; the
unresolved case is an introduction by progress that is immediately consumed
by an elimination at an arbitrary counter, for which ordinary
$\beta$-reduction does not supply the missing induction. An interpretation
on a proper domain or with several sorts is permitted by the task, but its
domain predicate, its interpretation of equality and both internal composites
would have to be charged and proved. Can the single-binder universal
(\cref{base:def:single-binder}) remove a primitive operation of a checker?
(\pkg{checker\_refinement}, \pkg{single\_binder}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Closures as hypothesis leaves]
\Cref{cert:prop:typed-repair} makes progress at closures conservative when
closures are concluded only by hypotheses. Can certificates be normalized so
that every subproof of a closure is a hypothesis leaf, with a compiler for the
weighted-slot encoding and a proof about its decoder, at a cost below the
saving of the shorter selector? (\pkg{verified\_refinements}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[One verified decoder with the refinements as corollaries]
Can the sorted reading and the soundness induction for the compact checker be
formalized once, so that the refinements of \cref{cert:sec:later} (the
inferred major, eager selection, the remainder selector, the phase by
context, three fields, allocation first, retained majors, the rewritings of
\cref{cert:sec:after-first}) become verified corollaries, and can
the acceptance relation of the programs, with its totalized decoder and its
scope transitions, be linked to the Lean calculus by a verified compiler? The
root-opening lemma (\cref{cert:lem:root}) is proposed as a starting point;
for three fields the decisive step is the zero-instance supply
(\cref{cert:lem:supply,cert:lem:zeroinstance}) for a compound conclusion,
together with the sorted reading of administrative aliases.
(\pkg{smaller\_envelope}, \pkg{frontier\_review},
\pkg{checked\_presentation}, \pkg{three\_field}, \pkg{verified\_refinements}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Validation together with the kernel]
Can an invariant shared between parsing, binding and proof checking make the
typing of the whole certificate and the goal grammar cheaper together with
the kernel, with a soundness statement for the whole accepted input language?
\Cref{cert:thm:typed-native} still types the input by a separate test before
the kernel runs, and so do the programs of
\cref{cert:prop:native-first,cert:prop:empty-phase}. Could a grammar make the
validation intrinsic to the presentation, with an exact theorem set for every
accepted public input? (\pkg{frontier\_review}, \pkg{verified\_refinements}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[The floor as part of the phase]
The length of the context determines the floor at the entry of a call
(\cref{cert:rem:floor-shortcut}), but the continuation uses the next floor,
and the checker of \cref{cert:thm:context-phase} gives up the relation
altogether. Is there a design in which the floor is not a separate argument
and the transition to the next floor and the immutability of contexts are
still accounted for, at a smaller total size? The exact reconstruction of
\cref{cert:prop:context-floor} costs eight bytes, and one bound in terms of
the length of the context is refuted by \cref{cert:prop:inclusive-floor};
\pkg{scope\_and\_frontier} asks that every proposal reject the stack of that
proposition before its size is compared. (\pkg{frontier\_review},
\pkg{context\_phase}, \pkg{immediate\_application}, \pkg{scope\_and\_frontier}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Raw acceptance under inference]
Exhibit a stack that a control accepts and the corresponding inferred-major
checker rejects (\cref{cert:prop:inferred-change}(c)), or show that none
exists for one of the two profiles. For the program of $502$ bytes, decide
whether its accepted stacks are comparable with those of its control when
formulas of arity $0$ or at least $4$ occur (\cref{cert:prop:remainder}).
Neither point is settled by the packages, which claim only the equality of
the accepted canonical goals.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Raw acceptance under three fields and allocation first]
Is there a stack $\sigma$ of triples that a three-field checker accepts while
its control rejects $E(\sigma)$, or conversely
(\cref{cert:prop:three-relation}(a))? Such a stack must reach a computation
packet with a true-valued predecessor at a goal of arity other than $2$ whose
two openings differ. Is there a stack that the control of $506$ bytes accepts
and the checker that allocates first rejects
(\cref{cert:prop:context-first})? \pkg{three\_field} and \pkg{constantfree}
claim neither inclusion, and only the first failure of an inclusion, in
\cref{cert:prop:context-first}(c), is exhibited.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A native three-field checker]
Can the validation of the whole input and of the full native goal grammar
(\cref{cert:prop:compositions}(v)) be shared with the three-field dispatch
without losing the introduction by cases at equations? \pkg{three\_field}
proposes the introduction packet $[\varepsilon,0,w]$ with $\varepsilon=0$ for
progress and $\varepsilon=[\,]$ for cases; by \cref{cert:lem:separation} the
test fails, so $s=\varepsilon$ remains available to the mode test, but such a
checker must skip false-valued major calls, as the global profile does, or
keep the corresponding neutral resources in its initial context. No such
program is given or measured; the native program of \pkg{three\_field} reads
four fields. (\pkg{three\_field}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Canonical unused fields]
Can the compiler of certificates make every unused field canonical without a
substantial increase in the size of certificates? Completeness
(\cref{cert:thm:three-complete,cert:prop:compositions}) needs only the
existence of a canonical certificate, not the preservation of every raw
spelling. (\pkg{three\_field}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A smaller two-field checker]
The two-field protocol (\cref{cert:def:two-field}) removes data from the
packets but adds code that recognizes computation equations and the role of
the major; its reference program ($295/352/742$) is far above the envelope
under R/$\mathcal U$. Is there a two-field program, or a strict interface for
it with a validated goal and typed input, that is smaller than the
corresponding four-field programs? The proof of
\cref{cert:thm:two-field-sound} is for trees of integers and arrays; the
package claims nothing about other JSON values as aliases. (\pkg{refinement}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Computation leaves and terminal packets]
Is the shortcut recorded by \pkg{shared\_leaf}, which selects the mode of an
introduction from the arity of the generated hypothesis, unsound for some
certificate, or is it sound under an invariant of reachable states? The
package rejects it because an auxiliary matrix with $Q$ at its root would
receive progress, without exhibiting a false accepted goal.
\Cref{cert:prop:mode-collision} (\pkg{468\_presentation}) shows that the arity
cannot separate the two cases, and with \cref{cert:prop:closure-progress} that
the calculus the shortcut implements derives induction for every native formula;
no certificate accepted by a program with the shortcut for an underivable public
goal is exhibited, and none is claimed. The uniform
terminals, the direct leaves and uniform zero are combined in one validated
program by \cref{cert:prop:validated-terminal} (\pkg{scope\_refinement},
\pkg{validated\_leaves}). Can the uniform zero of \cref{cert:prop:uzero-native}
reduce the compact programs, whose goals are not validated? Do the raw acceptance sets
of the direct-leaf and uniform terminal programs and of their controls stand
in an inclusion beyond those of \cref{cert:prop:leaf-compositions}? The packages
claim only the equality of accepted canonical goals, and the example after
\cref{cert:prop:terminal} shows that the raw sets differ.
(\pkg{computation\_leaves}, \pkg{shared\_leaf}, \pkg{uniform\_terminals},
\pkg{smaller\_presentations}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A uniqueness lemma for the bridge]
\pkg{whole\_json\_refinement} proves, in \texttt{relative\_recursion\_uniqueness.tex},
that if $f(0,\vec p)=g(0,\vec p)$, $f(\Suc z,\vec p)=H(z,f(z,\vec p),\vec p)$,
$g(\Suc z,\vec p)=K(z,g(z,\vec p),\vec p)$ and $H(z,u,\vec p)=K(z,u,\vec p)$
are derivable under $\Gamma$ with $z,u$ fresh, then
$\Gamma\proves f(t,\vec p)=g(t,\vec p)$, by one induction for a literal equation,
and that both the extensionality of the simulated iterator and the
primitive-recursion case of the term round trip in the base chapter are
instances. This concerns the bridge of \cref{base:thm:bridge}, not the
checkers. Can the Lean development expose it as one theorem shared by $\iPRA$
and the scalar theory, and does the fusion form ($e(a)=b$ and
$e(H(n,u))=K(n,e(u))$ give $e(\mathrm{Rec}_H(n,a))=\mathrm{Rec}_K(n,b)$) shorten
the formal development? (\pkg{whole\_json\_refinement}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Spellings of zero in public goals]
Can the public grammar admit further spellings of a numeral, such as the
reserved scalars $1$ and $2$ (\cref{cert:cor:reserved}), together with a
mechanism that identifies them with the canonical spelling, at a total cost
below that of the validation they replace, with the cost and the correctness
of the mechanism counted? (\pkg{immediate\_application}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Normalization and opening in one traversal]
Can a checker based on a calculus with fewer rule families, such as the
evaluation and discharge calculi or the calculus with one rule of successor
evaluation in other chapters, be lowered to the arity encoding with a proved
translation? Deleting computation branches does not suffice: removing a zero
iterator below other binders requires rebasing the references to outer
variables, since a variable encoded by the scalar $5$ in the initializer, below
the dummy slot, is encoded by $3$ once the slot is removed, and the opener of
\cref{cert:def:opener} replaces only its hole by an image closed at the root
term depth. A shared certified traversal for normalization and opening would
have to perform this rebasing; no complete alternative is measured.
\pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots} records the same failure in the encoding with
uniform zero: \texttt{[[[1,7,3],5]]} encodes $\D{x}{y}(\It{0}{x}{u}{u}=x)$, the variable
$x$ being written $7$ in the initializer, below the dummy slot, and $5$ on the right;
a normalizer that returns the initializer unchanged produces \texttt{[[7,5]]}, in which
$7$ reads as the hole. (\pkg{scope\_and\_frontier},
\pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A representation without the parity of depths]
\pkg{refinement} examines a representation that gives numerical and formula
roots the same depth and removes the parity test from the opener. The local
semantic opening lemma (\cref{cert:lem:opening}) then fails: a scalar at a
formula position reads $0=0$, and replacing it by a numerical image can produce
a raw tree that reads as a false equation. Is there a reachability invariant
that excludes the offending spellings? The package exhibits no false accepted
certificate for every such checker, and establishes no such invariant; the
related result of \pkg{validated\_core} in \cref{cert:sec:validated} gives an
accepted false formula for one program without the parity test.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[The shifted codec in a proof assistant]
Can the family of shifted readings of \cref{cert:def:offset}, the eager opener,
the scope validation and the direct leaves be formalized together and connected
to the Lean development of the guarded calculus? What is the smallest program of
the shifted encoding when the program itself validates the public grammar and
the alphabet of the whole input? The only such program, the validated companion
of \cref{cert:prop:shifted-validated} ($690/773/2064$), is readable and makes no
claim of size. Can the deletion and insertion of terminal packets in the proof of
\cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal}, and the framing of the whole input, be
formalized against the native kernel? (\pkg{offset472}, \pkg{468\_presentation}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Separation in the weakened full-normal calculus]
In the calculus of \cref{disch:prop:unary} on full normal forms, exact
computation gives the interpolation (A) of \cref{cert:sec:unary-rigid}. Does the
weakened calculus prove the separation (S) of every pair of distinct elements,
without the reflection of equality of the stronger theory? If not, is there a
structure with a pair of distinct, related and inseparable elements that
validates all logical rules and induction and refutes transitivity?
(\pkg{ast261}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[One program below the envelope]
Is there one complete program, for one fixed public grammar, framing and
validation contract, below the envelopes $239/255/465$ under R/$\mathcal U$ with
the double dagger, $244/261/465$ without it, $266/298/460$ under
R$^{+2}$/$\mathcal U$, or $300/337/580$ under T/ed$_1$ ($309/345/580$ among the
programs that read four-field packets, $300/337/626$ among the three-field programs) in
all three coordinates? Under the exact contracts the envelopes are $358/408/687$
(T/ed$_1^{\sharp}$) and $317/358/614$ (T/ed$_1^{\natural}$); under the goal contracts
they are $303/338/576$ (G$^{\natural}_{\top}$/$\mathcal A^{-}$), where among the four-field
programs $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ attained all three minima until
$S^{\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ of \pkg{checker\_research} lowered the bytes to
$576$ (and $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ attained all three minima of the code until the program of $358$
nodes of \pkg{names\_and\_scopes}), and $288/331/556$ (G$_{1,\top}$/$\mathcal A^{-}$, or $288/331/553$
with the double dagger); in the shifted encoding $299/335/583$ (T/ed$_1^{+2}$).
\pkg{checker\_research}, \pkg{scope\_free\_audit} and \pkg{stack\_clock} ask for one program with one
fixed public language, statement interface, parser and convention of acceptance below
$238/255/455$, or below the envelope of a fixed comparable contract with exact binding and the whole
trusted boundary charged, and record that their programs do not reach it:
$S^{\natural\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta}$ improves one coordinate of its control,
$S^{3}_{\mathrm{E,i},\phi,\ell}$ all three of its control but not the tokens and nodes of the envelope
of its code, and $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ and $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm{i},\lambda\beta,\kappa}$ none
(\cref{cert:prop:same-runs,cert:prop:printed}); \pkg{composition} records that its programs of
$236$ tokens attain neither $255$ nodes nor $455$ bytes. \pkg{exact\_threshold}, \pkg{local\_validation},
