\pkg{retained\_validation}, \pkg{reconstructed\_introduction} and \pkg{structural\_parameters} ask
for one program below $239/255/460$, or $238/255/455$ with the seeded header, with its parser,
binding discipline and public reading charged, and record that they do not reach it;
\pkg{retained\_validation} asks whether the field inference of its token and node profiles can be
combined with its byte profile, and \pkg{structural\_parameters} whether one strict program can
combine its three gains.
\pkg{validated\_phase}, \pkg{exact\_bindings}, \pkg{exact\_masks},
\pkg{intrinsic\_validation}, \pkg{names\_and\_scopes} and \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} ask
it for one program below the compact envelope under a fixed theorem or sequent contract,
with every reader and decoder charged, and record that they do not reach it.
(Before these packages the envelope under T/ed$_1$ was $302/340/584$, $311/345/584$
among the four-field programs and $302/340/646$ among the three-field programs.) \pkg{checked\_reduction} asks it below $239/255/461$, admitting
the stream programs and across the two encodings, and \pkg{validated\_three\_field}
for the strict programs; \pkg{neutral\_modes} asks it below $239/255/460$,
\pkg{scope\_phase} for one strict program below $306/348/589$ or below the new
envelope, and \pkg{304\_token\_refinement} and \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots}
record that they do not reach it. $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e,\pi}$ attains two of the minima
among the four-field programs, as $S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ did before it. \pkg{neutral\_modes} and \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots} compose the
eager opener with the three-field records and with the sentinel, the structural binding
and the conditional major, and \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots} the opened
candidate with $S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm E}$ (\cref{cert:prop:eager-three}); the loop of
\cref{cert:prop:loop-continuation} and the reservation at entry of
\cref{cert:thm:floor-phase} are not composed with each other; \pkg{validated\_phase}
composes the reservation at entry with the eager opener
(\cref{cert:prop:phase-guards}), and no package composes the loop with it. (Before these four packages the envelopes were $239/255/465$,
$244/261/465$, $267/298/461$ and $306/348/589$, $315/348/589$ among the four-field
programs. Before \pkg{checked\_reduction} and
\pkg{validated\_three\_field} the envelopes were $239/256/465$, $244/261/465$ and
$317/356/597$. Before \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers},
\pkg{prefix\_conditional} and \pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement} these were
$240/259/465$, $245/263/465$ and $319/358/600$; before \pkg{direct\_packets} and
\pkg{procedure\_presentation} $240/261/469$, $245/268/469$ and
$319/358/605$. \pkg{conditional\_image} asks the question for the stream programs
across the two encodings, below $239$ tokens, $257$ nodes and $462$ bytes, at a
revision whose census lacks $L'_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ and $T^{\rho}_{+,\mathrm p}$.) The packages of \cref{cert:sec:offset},
\cref{cert:sec:leaves} and \cref{cert:sec:validated-terminal} each improve one
coordinate with one program. Uniform terminals repeat a computation at each of
its uses where a supply was shared; how do certificate length, depth of recursion
and checking time compare? (\pkg{468\_presentation}, \pkg{ast261},
\pkg{checked\_presentations}, \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf}, \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf},
\pkg{direct\_packets}, \pkg{procedure\_presentation}, \pkg{conditional\_image},
\pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement}, \pkg{checked\_reduction},
\pkg{validated\_three\_field}, \pkg{304\_token\_refinement}, \pkg{neutral\_modes},
\pkg{scope\_phase}, \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots}, \pkg{validated\_phase},
\pkg{shared\_witness\_bundles}, \pkg{exact\_bindings}, \pkg{exact\_masks},
\pkg{intrinsic\_validation}, \pkg{names\_and\_scopes}, \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs},
\pkg{exact\_threshold}, \pkg{local\_validation}, \pkg{retained\_validation},
\pkg{reconstructed\_introduction}, \pkg{structural\_parameters}, \pkg{checker\_research},
\pkg{scope\_free\_audit}, \pkg{stack\_clock}, \pkg{composition}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Exact syntax at the compact frontier]
The exact contract of the actual-binding encoding costs $17$ tokens, $21$ nodes and $34$
bytes above the sorted contract in the envelopes ($317/358/614$ against $300/337/580$), and
that of the padded encoding more ($358/408/687$). Can a codec make every admitted slot genuine with a capture-avoiding opening as
short as the present one, so that no mask or separate validator is needed; can a cheaper
total normalization identify the padding aliases of \cref{cert:cor:second-dummy} with
their canonical spellings and keep completeness for every admitted spelling; and can the
spelling \texttt{true} of $1$ admitted by $S^{\natural}_{\mathrm i}$ be rejected without an
extra pass? Can a parser with an explicit unbounded semantics match the size of the reader
of \cref{cert:lem:reader} without its fixed limit of nesting? Under the goal contracts,
without the test of the printed form, the actual-binding encoding costs $15$ tokens, $7$ nodes and
$20$ bytes above the padded one ($303/338/576$ against $288/331/556$; $26$ bytes before
\pkg{checker\_research}). Can actual binding be
represented without the test of the index of a child and without masks of dummy slots, keeping a
complete public spelling of every admitted formula? \pkg{retained\_validation} reports an
unsuccessful experiment with a normalization of even codes to the code of zero: a source $0$ then
also selects the computation of zero, so a direct successor computation at the predecessor $0$ is
lost, and a scalar truth admitted under $D$ creates a separate obligation of completeness; no
program is delivered. Can noncanonical raw expressions be normalized or compared in the traversal
that checks the inferences, so that every admitted spelling whose reading is a theorem has a
certificate, with every decoder and comparison charged? \pkg{exact\_threshold} records that soundness
together with completeness for the canonical encodings does not give this, by
\cref{cert:cor:second-dummy}, and that an interface that outputs the decoded conclusion is
legitimate only if its decoder is counted. \pkg{checker\_research} asks for an encoder and a total
decoder with $D(E(A))=A$ whose decoding is fused with the opening, checking against $E(D(q))$ for every
finite public code with the decoder measured (\cref{cert:rem:decoded}); \pkg{scope\_free\_audit} asks for a
small public grammar that is intrinsically typed and complete for every admitted spelling without the cost
of actual binding, with an explicit quotient by the decoder; and \pkg{stack\_clock} asks for a certificate
language in which terms and formulas can be built only in their lexical scopes, with the interpreter of the
construction, the readout of the theorem and every check counted. (\pkg{exact\_bindings}, \pkg{exact\_masks},
\pkg{intrinsic\_validation}, \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs}, \pkg{exact\_threshold},
\pkg{local\_validation}, \pkg{retained\_validation}, \pkg{checker\_research}, \pkg{scope\_free\_audit},
\pkg{stack\_clock}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Synthesis of the conclusion with a shared discharge test]
Can a checker that computes and prints the formula it proves (\cref{cert:rem:decoded}) share the test of
the inferred successor instance of an introduction with the test that a discharged parameter does not
escape, with a proof of soundness and a compiler for all native derivations? \pkg{checker\_research} records
that an experimental synthesis program of the repository, with the comparison against an external goal
removed and the inferred formula printed, measures $318/374/746$; the completeness of its calculus is not
established and it is not offered as a checker. The package also records, without delivered programs, that
a recoding in which the scalar $0$ is again the numerical zero gave no simultaneous improvement, that an
interface with functions of the host language as input would still need unforgeable proof values, comparison
modulo renaming and a test of escaping parameters, and that reading malformed aliases as zero without a
decoder does not give completeness for every spelling. (\pkg{checker\_research}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Names from finite trees]
Can the fresh names of \cref{cert:def:opaque} contain a smaller summary than the whole context, for
instance a tree whose subtrees are exactly the parameters in scope, with a proof that the summary
is preserved? Can certificates share the subtrees of names as a graph, with a checked condition of
acyclicity, since a cycle would destroy the argument by size of \cref{cert:lem:opaque}(b)? The
names lengthen certificates (from $12726$ to $635588$ bytes for an induction with a used
hypothesis). Can the allocation by trees be combined with a smaller protocol to give one program
below $239/255/460$, and can a measure on an abstract machine show the removal of the floor
independently of the punctuation of the host language? Is \texttt{[[7],[1]]}, the shifted spelling
of the goal of \cref{cert:cor:second-dummy}, without a certificate for the programs of
\cref{cert:prop:shifted-strict}? The package records the spelling and claims no completeness for
it; no proof for the shifted programs is given. (\pkg{structural\_parameters}.) The printed names of
\cref{cert:prop:printed} need no tree in the certificate but grow in digits faster than the printed state:
the package's producer stopped at $2000000$ digits on an inherited induction certificate, which was not
run. Can one shared traversal perform the substitution, collect a bound for the parameters in scope and
validate the active input, with less source than a global floor or names of growing length, a bound
supplied by the certificate being checked and not trusted? (\pkg{scope\_free\_audit}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Readings modulo annotations]
\Cref{cert:lem:retraction} fails for $O^{+}$ (\cref{cert:sec:validated-terminal}).
Is the no-image opening $O^{+}$ idempotent on the quotient of raw trees by
equality of their readings at the positions that have one, and can the
composition of validators with the kernel be formalized on that quotient?
How much longer do certificates become when a retained computation is replaced
by a leaf at each of its uses and every hypothesis leaf consumes a packet?
(\pkg{validated\_leaves}.)
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Injectivity over coverage]
Does $\mathsf Q_F$ of \cref{cert:sec:qf-rigid}, or $\mathsf T_F^{-}$, prove
$\Suc x=\Suc y\to x=y$? For $\mathsf T_F^{-}$ the answer is yes:
\cref{seed:thm:tf-inj} (\pkg{selfseed\_injectivity}) compares two paired
stopping orbits and closes with an induction on a literal equation.  For
$\mathsf Q_F$, which has no induction, the question stays open:
\cref{cert:thm:qf-rigid} excludes, in a model of $\mathsf Q_F$, a merging of
two predecessor chains at a standard positive numeral; whether a merging at a
nonstandard element is possible is open.  (\pkg{scope\_refinement}.)
\end{question}

The following items are limits and claims that the packages state without a
proof in their reports, and results of these packages that belong to other
chapters.
\begin{itemize}
\item \pkg{context\_phase} contains a theorem that is not checker mathematics:
the native kernel without the introduction of $Q$ by progress does not derive
$\Q{y}(\It{y}{0}{u}{u}=0)$, nor the public formula
$\D{x}{y}(\It{y}{0}{u}{u}=0)$, which the full kernel derives; the proof
expands a countably saturated structure coherently with substitution in
iterator bodies, after a method of Friedman. The package
\pkg{progress\_independence} treats the same independence question. The
theorem belongs to the chapter on obstructions and is not proved here.
\item \pkg{smaller\_envelope} contains a finite-menu obstruction: restricting
the witnesses of the introduction to $0$ and parameters and the targets of
closure elimination to compositions of a fixed finite collection of
primitive recursive operations loses native consequences. It belongs to the
chapter on obstructions as well, together with the questions that the package
attaches to it (an infinite but structurally generated family of targets; a
quotient or another numerical domain) and the related question of
\pkg{frontier\_review} whether introduction and specialization can both be
automatic without an enumerator of terms.
\item \pkg{checked\_presentation} asks whether successor-only uniqueness of
iteration gives a discriminator for actual equality, and whether the
remaining question of independence of induction has a model of the binding
iterator that respects substitution; \pkg{smaller\_envelope} and
\pkg{frontier\_review} ask whether the arithmetic bridge (predecessor, formula
selectors) or the term signature can be simplified together with the checker.
\pkg{verified\_refinements} asks the first question again and records that
the existing investigations derive zero equations and facts about addition
from successor-only uniqueness, but not the reflection of arbitrary
equalities or cancellation, and that importing a predecessor built with
other updates would be circular. These concern the rule basis and not the
checkers.
\item The correctness of the exact-integer test by identity with the
conversion to an integer in \pkg{context\_phase} is an argument about one
runtime and its standard reader; the package says so and supplies the variant
with the explicit type test.
\item \pkg{prevalidated\_elimination} states that the sets of stacks accepted
in the base order and in the prevalidated order are incomparable. The report
displays only the stack of \cref{cert:prop:incomparable}; a stack accepted
only in the base order is among its test data and is not exhibited.
\item \pkg{validated\_core} compares its programs with controls whose
validators are not printed in the report; \cref{cert:thm:validated-core}
assumes, as the report states, that the control tests $J$ on the whole input
and $P$ on the goal.
\item The statement that the checkers of the guarded-source protocol accept
exactly the public theorems rests on the equivalence theorem of
\pkg{guarded\_source} (\cref{cert:rem:imported}), which is a written proof
outside this chapter; \pkg{kernel\_refinement} and \pkg{compact\_update} ask
for its formalization together with the restriction on substitution.
\item \pkg{neutral\_scope} asks whether the self-seeded basis of another
package can derive its additional equality and constructor principles, or
whether a complete checker that charges them can still be smaller than the
checkers of the initialized iterator; it reports no result.
\item \pkg{kernel\_refinement} asks whether its replacement of one occurrence
in the left side of an equation helps a readable or verified checker; it
does not help the compact programs.
\item The experimental synthesis programs of \pkg{prevalidated\_elimination}
are not established as complete checkers.
\item \pkg{three\_field} asks whether a reduction of the rule basis can also
reduce a complete checker, and records that replacing induction for literal
equations by induction for equations at zero leaves a gap in the reflection of
equality on the whole domain, which a new argument would have to close. This
concerns the rule basis. The package gives a written proof of its protocol and
finite tests ($132$ runs of the three-field programs, $484$ of the four-field
programs, $26$ compiled proofs); no new formalization in a proof assistant is
claimed.
\item The main result of \pkg{constantfree} concerns another calculus and
belongs to another chapter; its compact part contains only the compositions
(iii) and (vi) of \cref{cert:prop:compositions}, supported by its written
argument and by $61$ fixtures run $244$ times.
\item The finite tests of the new packages are implementation evidence: $484$
executions in \pkg{checker\_refinement}, $692$ in \pkg{retained\_premises}
(with $16$ compiled certificates), $726$ in \pkg{single\_binder}, $414$ in
\pkg{502\_scope\_audit} (with a separate comparison of $152$ executions for
the packed program), and $452$ in \pkg{verified\_refinements}. None claims a
formalization in a proof assistant.
\item The counterexample of \cref{cert:prop:entry-scan} refutes one deletion
of three tokens; \pkg{502\_scope\_audit} states that it is not a lower bound
of $253$ tokens and does not exclude a different scope discipline.
\item The obstruction of \cref{cert:thm:vs} and the bound of
\cref{cert:thm:finite-signature} concern the rule basis and the term
language; they may belong with the obstructions of another chapter.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{immediate\_application}, \pkg{refinement}
and \pkg{scope\_and\_frontier} are implementation evidence: $484$ executions
of $121$ fixtures in the first, $898$ executions of $112$ fixture entries in
the second (with the separate searches over computation leaves described after
\cref{cert:thm:two-field-complete}), and $252$ executions of $42$ inputs in
the third, all in ordinary and optimized execution and without discrepancy.
The proofs of \cref{cert:prop:immediate,cert:prop:context-floor,cert:thm:two-field-sound,cert:thm:two-field-complete,cert:prop:reserved}
and of \cref{cert:rem:refinement-488} are written proofs, reviewed within the
packages; none of the three packages claims a formalization in a proof
assistant or a new build of the Lean development, and each states that the
requested simplification below the envelope is not achieved and that no
minimality is claimed.
\item The proofs of \cref{cert:prop:immediate,cert:rem:refinement-488} assume
ideal executions: the nested result lists of the truth-valued programs retain
more memory than the controls, and a fixed recursion or memory limit can
separate the programs.
\item \pkg{immediate\_application} restates the obstruction to the shorter
progress selector (\cref{cert:rem:closure-progress-sources}) and records,
without a measured program, that the compositions with the floor recomputed
from the context that it tried were larger than their controls.
\item The guarded scalar $J$ of \pkg{guarded\_register} and its correctness
belong to the chapter on canonical and counter-aware recursors; this chapter
records only its size.
\item The finite tests of the packages of \cref{cert:sec:native-refinements}
and of the second delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation} are implementation
evidence: $730$ executions and $224$ executions of the readable program, with
$40$ further executions of an independent review of the stream programs, in
\pkg{checked\_presentation}; $632$ in \pkg{479\_predecessor57}; $438$ in
\pkg{immediate\_opening} (with a comparison on $39$ certificates of
\pkg{502\_scope\_audit}); $546$, with $12$ checks of the unsound native
variants, in \pkg{zero\_test\_split}; $440$, with $15$ comparisons of event
traces, in \pkg{checked\_frontier}; $448$ in \pkg{atomic\_seed\_refinement};
and $222$ in \pkg{reductions}; $336$ executions of the strict programs in
\pkg{equation\_aliases}; and $886$ checker processes in \pkg{alias\_back},
including the two-field stream; $424$ in \pkg{absorbing\_orbit}; $266$ runs
of the unchanged baselines in \pkg{absorbing\_predecessor}; and $2576$ in
\pkg{boolean\_constancy}; $204$ executions of the candidate programs, $242$ of
the inherited cases and $2$ negative controls in \pkg{zero\_alias}; $1530$
executions of the validated programs, $454$ of the compact program and $20$
independent checks in \pkg{checked\_reductions}; $880$ in the second delivery of
\pkg{refinement}; $40$ in \pkg{fused\_iteration}; $108$ in \pkg{pred50}; and
$388$ command-line runs with $44416$ bounded trials against a false goal, all
rejected, in \pkg{tolerant\_certificates}; each package reports no unexpected
outcome.
\pkg{boolean\_constancy} also records that the stream programs do not read an
unused malformed suffix, and that its program of $478$ bytes rejects the
boundary certificate of \cref{cert:prop:eager-selector}(c). None of these packages claims a formalization in a
proof assistant, a minimal program or a program below all coordinates of an
envelope; all proofs of \cref{cert:prop:stream,cert:prop:native-append,cert:prop:native-int,cert:prop:parity,cert:prop:bit-products,cert:prop:gate-or,cert:prop:eager-selector,cert:prop:late-compositions,cert:prop:zero-alias,cert:thm:tolerant,cert:prop:stream-rewrites,cert:prop:k-stream,cert:prop:header,cert:prop:delayed-equality,cert:prop:delayed-closure}
are written proofs for ideal executions, and the truth-valued results retain
more memory than the controls.
\item The certificate of $30$ packets that \pkg{zero\_test\_split} reports
against the native programs with an initial floor
(\cref{cert:rem:header-native}) is part of its test data and is not displayed
in its report; the unsoundness of these variants is therefore recorded here as
the package's claim, with the mechanism described in the remark.
\item The argument of \cref{cert:prop:header} uses the scalar $0$ of a canonical
goal (\cref{cert:lem:guarded-zero}). The predicate $P^{\mathrm{ed}}$ of
\cref{cert:def:public} admits closed guarded goals without the scalar $0$,
such as $[[3],[[3]]]$, which reads $\Suc 0=\Suc\Suc 0$. Whether a goal-validated
program with the initial floor and this predicate is sound is not settled; no
package claims it.
\item \pkg{479\_predecessor57} does not claim that its program of $607$ bytes
and the program of $629$ bytes accept the same stacks; the equality of the
accepted canonical goals is \cref{cert:prop:native-int} with
\cref{cert:prop:native-composition}. Its arithmetic part, a predecessor term of
$57$ nodes, belongs to another chapter.
\item The scoped export of \cref{cert:prop:delayed-equality} is a lemma about
one step; \pkg{checked\_frontier} states explicitly that it does not establish
a checker, and an independent review of the lemma is part of its evidence.
\item The finite tests of the packages of \cref{cert:sec:leaves} are
implementation evidence: $856$ checker runs and $45625$ raw computation packets
examined by an independent numerical decoder (of which $27355$ satisfy the
premises of \cref{cert:lem:leaves}, with no disagreement on $109420$ numerical
instances) in \pkg{computation\_leaves}; $174$ executions and $21$ exact
translations in \pkg{shared\_leaf}; $584$ executions, $54$ translations and
$3122$ checked fixed points of the no-image opening in \pkg{uniform\_terminals};
$956$ executions on $319$ fixtures and $92$ recodings in
\pkg{smaller\_presentations}; $112$ in \pkg{whole\_json\_refinement}, with $4$
runs of the negative control; $80$ each in \pkg{ast269\_successor\_rigidity}
and \pkg{selective\_normalization}; $264$ in \pkg{export\_specialization};
$1224$ for the compact program and $880$ for the implementation appendix in
\pkg{476\_byte\_presentation}; and $2328$ in \pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements}.
All proofs of \cref{cert:sec:leaves} are written proofs for ideal executions;
no package claims a formalization in a proof assistant, and every package
states that no single program is below all coordinates of its envelope.
\item The census figures of these packages (for instance $4942$ paths and
$1788$ distinct sources in \pkg{shared\_leaf} and \pkg{smaller\_presentations},
$3102$ paths in \pkg{computation\_leaves}, $2894$ files in
\pkg{ast269\_successor\_rigidity}, $297$ small acceptance modules in
\pkg{whole\_json\_refinement}) are censuses of finite corpora at their
revisions, not lower bounds.
\item \pkg{ast269\_successor\_rigidity} and \pkg{selective\_normalization}
also contain calculi (a successor-rigidity presentation with a Boolean
equality split, and a selectively normalized six-rule presentation with a
readable checker of $2662/2904/13172$) that their compact programs do not
implement; they belong to other chapters. \pkg{abstraction\_and\_refinements}
measures its AQE checker against a matched control and states that AQE costs
$82$ tokens, $77$ nodes and $302$ bytes there.
\item The finite tests of the packages of \cref{cert:sec:offset,cert:sec:validated-terminal}
and of the checker parts of the other new packages are implementation
evidence: $102$ cases in $612$ executions and $521284$ rejected single-packet
certificates of four false goals in \pkg{offset472}; $344$ executions in
\pkg{checker\_refinements}; $84$ in \pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker}; $304$
executions, $32$ translations and $740$ differential inputs in
\pkg{scope\_refinement}, which also found no false equality among $3172$ public
computation goals formed from $55937$ raw packets; and $172$ executions in
\pkg{validated\_leaves}. \pkg{scope\_refinement} measures $5084$ source paths
with $1821$ distinct contents at its revision, a census and not a lower bound.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{468\_presentation}, \pkg{ast261},
\pkg{checked\_presentations} and the compact part of \pkg{root\_conversion\_raw}
are implementation evidence: $560$ executions of $140$ cases on $T_{+}$ and its
control, $264$ executions of the readable form, $346$ executions of $173$ cases on
the validated companion and $13$ structural comparisons of $T_{+}$ with $P_{+}$ in
the first; $84$ executions of $21$ inputs in the second; $808$ executions of the
strict programs on $141$ fixtures, $19$ translations, $160$ executions of the
compact programs and $416$ comparisons of traces in the third; $396$ executions of
$66$ cases in the fourth. All proofs of
\cref{cert:thm:shifted-terminal,cert:prop:shifted-validated,cert:prop:terminal-rewrites,cert:prop:checked-strict}
are written proofs for ideal executions; no package claims a formalization in a
proof assistant, a minimal program or a program below all coordinates of an
envelope. The censuses of \pkg{468\_presentation} ($3204$ source entries of the
artifacts, $1922$ distinct blobs, $1667$ distinct normalized modules with the
incoming archives) and of \pkg{ast261} ($4716$ source occurrences, $1705$ distinct
normalized modules) are censuses of finite corpora, not lower bounds.
\item \pkg{468\_presentation} asks whether a different domain or a different
interface of recursion and logic can remove enough obligations of a checker to
go below the present envelope, with definable isomorphisms for both composites of
the interpretations, and whether matrices with $Q$ at their root can be
eliminated from the auxiliary derivations by a conservative typing whose checking
costs less than the selector of the mode (\cref{cert:prop:mode-collision}). It
gives no result on either. \pkg{checked\_presentations} records that the route
through successor-only uniqueness of iteration does not settle the bootstrap of
predecessor and reflection of equality, and claims nothing about it.
\item \pkg{fixed\_points\_and\_checker} contains a countermodel showing that the
preservation of every fixed point does not replace induction for literal
equations; \pkg{validated\_leaves} contains a bound for adaptive iteration with
several registers; \pkg{orbit\_equality} contains an equality term of $45$ nodes
from the absorbing orbit and a diagonal-iterator lemma for the predecessor of
$45$ nodes. These belong to other chapters and are not proved here.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf}, \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf},
the checker part of \pkg{scalar\_constancy}, \pkg{direct\_packets} and
\pkg{procedure\_presentation} are implementation evidence: $108$ executions,
$8$ executions of the boundary review and $28$ checks of the readable form in the
first; $140$ executions, $8$ translations of successful traces and $4$ executions
of the negative control in the second; $100$ executions in the third; $71$
executions for $T_{\mathrm d}$, $255$ for the other programs, $11$ checks of the
readable form and $40$ executions and $20$ direct cases of an independent review
of the structural binding in the fourth; and $1150$ executions in the fifth
($344$ for the procedure forms, $304$ for the byte-string extension, $120$ for
$T_{+,\mathrm p}$, $256$ independent and $126$ for $L_{\mathrm r}$ and
$L'_{\mathrm r}$). All proofs of
\cref{cert:prop:direct-packets,cert:prop:match,cert:prop:return-reservation,cert:prop:procedure,cert:prop:validated-devices}
are written proofs for ideal executions; no package claims a formalization in a
proof assistant, a minimal program or a program below all coordinates of an
envelope. The agreement of the readable forms with their programs is not proved.
The censuses ($4815$ occurrences and $1189$ distinct candidate sources in
\pkg{468\_shared\_leaf}, $5232$ occurrences and $1881$ distinct sources in
\pkg{scalar\_constancy}) are censuses of finite corpora, not lower bounds.
\item \Cref{cert:prop:match}(a), and with it \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(b)
for the programs with structural binding, depends on the order in which the
measured runtime tests a sequence pattern and binds its capture names; the
language reference does not fix this, and neither \pkg{direct\_packets} nor the
packages of \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor} claim the statement for other
runtimes.
\item \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf} asks whether the position of a packet in the
certificate can generate fresh names without a separately threaded floor. A
clock given by the number of remaining packets fails, since the new name must be
excluded while the witness is validated and admitted when the branch is
formed. The reservation at return of \cref{cert:prop:return-reservation} meets
both conditions with a threaded floor; whether a representation without one
exists is open. The package also asks whether the test of the whole input, the
guard test and the scope traversal can be shared in a compact program with
complete public validation, without losing termination on arbitrary input.
\item \pkg{468\_shared\_leaf} and \pkg{shifted\_shared\_leaf} ask for one program
below $241$ tokens, $262$ nodes and $468$ bytes (the question on one program below
the envelope above), for a formalization of the eager opener, the dummy slots, the shared candidate and the induction on
successful calls, linked to the formalized bridge, for a restricted language of
checkers in which lower bounds on size are provable, and for the size of the
shortest certificates under these encodings. They give no result on any of
these.
\item \pkg{procedure\_presentation} records that a scalar presentation with
conversion only at the root is not proved complete: a selection by $Q$ with two
rows of equations expresses an implication between them, not both; the usual
macro for the conjunction uses the parity, and a numeral becomes a selector with
an unreduced iterator in its counter, which conversion at the root does not
evaluate. This is the failure of a route of proof, not a theorem of nonexistence.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers}, \pkg{prefix\_conditional}
and the third delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation} are implementation evidence:
$300$ executions of $30$ fixtures on five programs, ordinary and optimized, and
$5$ instrumented runs in which a future witness is rejected before the record of
the neutral major is read, in the first; $560$ executions in $280$ paired
comparisons, $56$ comparisons of the consumption of records and the final floor of
the stream programs, and $172$ executions of $43$ cases of
$S^{\rho}_{2,\mathrm p}$ in the second; $256$ executions and $224$ comparisons of
call traces up to the replacement of \texttt{False} by $0$ in the third. All proofs
of \cref{cert:prop:returned-floor} are written proofs for ideal executions; no
package claims a formalization in a proof assistant, a minimal program or a
program below all coordinates of an envelope, and the censuses of the three
packages ($3629$ and $3601$ source occurrences, and $3022$ entries of the tree
with the members of sixteen archives) are censuses of finite
corpora, not lower bounds.
\item \pkg{prefix\_conditional} states that the program with the rejected mode test
of \cref{cert:rem:rejected-mode}, through the native logical macros and a dummy
witness of the existential, would admit induction for arbitrary formulas and not
only for literal equations. It gives neither a derivation nor a certificate of a
goal that the calculus does not prove, and the claim is not proved here.
\item \pkg{fused\_blocks\_and\_checkers} asks for a mathematical change that brings
a complete checker below $462$ bytes with both formula round trips kept, noting
that counting families of rules does not decide this, since calculi with three
families can have much larger checkers; for a formalization of the relation of
\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(a) against the formalized native kernel; and for a
fixed language of checkers and contract of validation in which a lower bound on
size can be proved. The third delivery of \pkg{checked\_presentation} asks
whether the opening traversal can normalize computations far enough to remove a
family of rules without a larger mechanism for moving terms through binders;
whether the interpreter of compact certificates and its total auxiliary reading,
including malformed trees and the allocation of fresh names, can be connected to
the formalized theory by a machine-checked theorem; whether the validation of
formulas and of certificates can share an invariant with the checking of proofs,
so that the strict programs approach the compact envelope with the same accepted
public language; and which reductions give a gain that is robust across
implementations, measured by a fixed abstract machine, decoder and public
grammar. No package gives a result on these questions.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{conditional\_image} and
\pkg{validated\_arity\_refinement} are implementation evidence: $208$ executions of
$26$ cases on four programs (the two programs, the control and the readable form),
ordinary and optimized, including an instrumented attempt to capture a future
witness that every program rejects after two records, before the record of the
neutral major is read, in the first; $296$ executions of $37$ fixtures on
$S^{\mathrm T}_{2,\mathrm b}$, $S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm b}$ and their controls,
$172$ executions of $43$ fixtures on $S^{\rho,\mathrm e}_{2,\mathrm p}$ and its
control, and $4810$ evaluations of the two sides of
\cref{cert:lem:arity-erasure} for $0\le n\le 64$, $-12\le d\le 24$ and
$v\in\{0,1\}$, in the second. The proofs of
\cref{cert:rem:conditional-image,cert:prop:arity-erasure} are written proofs for
ideal executions; neither package claims a formalization in a proof assistant. The
statement of \pkg{conditional\_image} that reading the floor before the major is
proved, or skipping a neutral major that reserves a name, can permit capture is
given without a counterexample; the related observation of \pkg{scope\_refinement}
is in \cref{cert:rem:examined-variants}.
\item \pkg{conditional\_image} asks whether the validation of the public grammar and
of the whole input can be joined to the conditional image without making the
smallest validated program larger. No package composes the conditional image of
\cref{cert:prop:returned-floor}(a) with a validated program; the conditional
selection of \cref{cert:prop:checked-strict} concerns the new goal and not the
image.
\item The replacement of \cref{cert:lem:arity-erasure} applies verbatim to the
arity guard $v\le p$ of the native program $N_{2,\mathrm p}$, whose flag has the
same discipline (root call with $v=1$, reset in the membership test, default $0$
elsewhere); its reader admits arbitrary JSON values in the packets, and the guard
is then also reached for strings, whose length is again a nonnegative integer.
No package delivers or measures this program, so its effect on the envelope of
G$_1$/$\mathcal A^{-}$ is not known.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{checked\_reduction} and \pkg{validated\_three\_field}
are implementation evidence: $120$ isolated executions, ordinary and optimized, of
$30$ groups of fixtures ($7$ accepted, $23$ rejected) on $L''_{\mathrm{f,m}}$ and its
control, $30$ instrumented traces with the same consumption of records and final
floor, and $1064$ comparisons of the openings of the two images, and $584$ isolated
executions on the three eager programs, their controls and the readable form, in the
first; $710$ isolated executions ($320$ comparisons within the four-field family and
$390$ runs of the three-field programs and their readable form, in suites of $37$,
$43$ and $65$ cases), in the second. The proofs of
\cref{cert:prop:eager-validated,cert:prop:singleton-image,cert:prop:validated-three}
are written proofs for ideal executions, and neither package claims a formalization
in a proof assistant. \pkg{checked\_reduction} asks for one of the partial semantics
of the eager opener and of the canonical construction, including the distinction
between the scalar $0$ and the empty array as neutral truths and the order of
evaluation at the validation of the public goal; \pkg{validated\_three\_field} for
one of the early test of the endpoint, the zero-instance supply and the two phases
of validation, and asks whether the three-field programs admit an exact simulation
of a four-field control that allocates before it exports, on all stacks, including
noncanonical annotations; its theorem is the equality of the accepted goals.
\item \pkg{checked\_reduction} states that reading the scalar formula of
\cref{cert:rem:lazy-phase} as a new public truth constant does not repair the lazy
program without the phase: the would-be formula $[0]$ meets the length test of the
introduction at a scalar, so completeness would need a separate proof. No such
proof or program is given, and the altered theory is not used. Both packages also
ask for a change of the native axioms that removes a rule or a scope operation
while keeping both formula round trips and bringing a validated checker below the
compact records, for a fixed small language of implementation with a declared
budget for the parser in which claims of simplicity become less dependent on the
host syntax, and for encodings that make more malformed inputs impossible without a
larger decoder; no package gives a result on these questions.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{304\_token\_refinement}, \pkg{neutral\_modes},
\pkg{scope\_phase} and \pkg{composition\_and\_dummy\_slots} are implementation
evidence: $260$ isolated executions of $65$ cases on $S^{\mathrm T}_{3\mathrm f,\ell}$ and
its control, ordinary and optimized, and a comparison of $9461$ states of the recursive
and the looping program on $21$ cases, in the first; $514$ isolated executions, $180$
on the compact programs and $334$ on the strict ones, in the second; $806$ isolated
executions, four of them of the unsound variants of \cref{cert:rem:floor-order} and of
the end of \cref{cert:sec:validated}, in the third; $420$ executions of the four
compositions, $102$ of $S^{\mathrm x}$ and $13$ checks of its grammar in the fourth. The
proofs of \cref{cert:prop:loop-continuation,cert:prop:eager-three,cert:prop:parity-dispatch,cert:thm:floor-phase,cert:prop:exact-image,cert:prop:second-dummy}
are written proofs for ideal executions, and no package claims a formalization in a
proof assistant.
\item \pkg{304\_token\_refinement} asks whether the loop of
\cref{cert:prop:loop-continuation} can be combined with the structural binding of
$S^{\mathrm N}_{2,\mathrm m}$: after a failed pattern a loop can keep the fields of the
previous iteration bound, so the argument of \cref{cert:prop:match}(a) does not
transfer, and no such program is given. It examines a binder with two bodies,
$K_{x,y}(A,B)\defeq\exists x\,(A(x)\to\forall y\,B(x,y))$, which exposes implication and
quantification at the cost of a second formula, records that its ordinary introduction
does not supply the coverage that the native introduction by cases gives, and gives no
checker. It asks whether, in the raw native language, variable-only substitution without
the joining at a compound root keeps every theorem with an empty context; the
obstruction of \cref{cert:sec:vs} concerns open sequents. It also asks for one
formalization of the sorted decoder and its accepted inputs, connected to the Lean
development of the native calculus.
\item \pkg{neutral\_modes} asks for a systematic description of the freedom that
\cref{cert:prop:parity-dispatch}(a) exhibits on the branches whose generated hypothesis
reads $0=0$: a small language of dispatchers, each with a proof of soundness on
successful openings and of the transfer of canonical stacks. Semantic neutrality does
not justify deleting an eager validation that completeness needs. Its question whether
full validation fits below the strict program of $589$ bytes is answered by
$S^{\rho}_{\mathrm e}$ ($584$ bytes). It asks for a formalization of the raw decoder,
the partial opener, the independence of the mode and the relation of canonical stacks.
\item \pkg{scope\_phase} asks for a formalization of the invariants of
\cref{cert:lem:floor-phase} (the goal exported before the reservation, the strict
decrease of the floor during a major call) for the lazy and the eager openers, which
distinguishes the witness opened before the major call from the endpoint test made
after it; and for a typed representation that enforces the separation of sorts more
cheaply than the parity of depths without moving it into an uncounted decoder: the
counterexample at the end of \cref{cert:sec:validated} shows that arrays at the root
of templates and at the positions of formula sort of goals and hypotheses do not
suffice.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{validated\_phase}, \pkg{shared\_witness\_bundles},
\pkg{exact\_bindings}, \pkg{exact\_masks}, \pkg{intrinsic\_validation},
\pkg{names\_and\_scopes} and \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} are implementation evidence:
$768690$ bounded comparisons of the guards, $287$ isolated runs of $112$ three-field and
$63$ four-field fixtures and a boundary suite of $236$ executions in the first; $252$
executions of $63$ cases in the second; $30456$ grammar comparisons and $450$ executions
for the actual-binding programs, and $45124$ grammar comparisons, $12000$ opener
comparisons and $764$ executions for the signed masks, in the third; $224880$ grammar
comparisons and $1006$ executions in the fourth; $12742$ grammar comparisons, $13120$
opener checks, $10866$ mutated goals and $232$ executions in the fifth; $750$ executions in
the sixth; $212$ executions of the sequent program and $1454$ of the expression readers in
the seventh; each reports no unexpected outcome. The proofs of
\cref{cert:prop:phase-guards,cert:prop:actual,cert:prop:intrinsic,cert:prop:masks,cert:lem:reader,cert:prop:sequent}
are written proofs for ideal executions, and no package claims a formalization in a proof
assistant or a new build of the Lean development.
\item \pkg{validated\_phase} records that deleting the factor $d\bmod 2$ of the eager
fallback, which would admit numerical replacement at positions of formula sort, is
unsound, citing a counterexample of \pkg{scope\_phase}, and that weakening the test
$\lvert a\rvert=2$ to the truth value of $a[1{:}]$ grants progress at closures
(\cref{cert:prop:closure-progress}). It and \pkg{exact\_bindings} report diagnostics in
which the witness is opened after the major call and a future parameter is captured, as in
\cref{cert:rem:floor-order}; these variants are not programs of the table.
\item \pkg{exact\_bindings} asks for a formalization of the actual-binding reading and of
the opener with its contract on positional arguments, and for measures of certificate
length, time and memory besides source size; \pkg{exact\_masks} asks whether the mask and
the floor can share one coordinate and whether cyclic or shared proof representations can
remove a rule family with a local trace condition that keeps exactly quantifier-free
induction; \pkg{intrinsic\_validation} asks for a formal semantics of the partial opener
over the evaluation of the host language, including Boolean equality and exceptions;
\pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} asks whether an environment representation can merge
iteration with opening and remove the computation leaves. No package gives a result on
these questions. \pkg{exact\_masks} also notes that a total primitive recursive evaluator
of all primitive recursive programs is impossible by diagonalization, so it is not a
route to a smaller basis.
\item \pkg{nontraditional\_inputs} states that the seeded variants are complete only for
contexts that contain the neutral seed; no completeness statement for other
representations of the context is given. The extension of \cref{cert:prop:sequent} to
contexts with free parameters, which would need a common initial scope, is not treated.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{exact\_threshold}, \pkg{local\_validation},
\pkg{retained\_validation}, \pkg{reconstructed\_introduction} and \pkg{structural\_parameters} are
implementation evidence: $14$ translated derivations, $12742$ grammar comparisons, $13120$ opener
checks, $490$ positive variants, $10866$ mutated goals, $232$ executions and $90$ trials of the
threshold and of the capture of a future parameter in the first; $1486$ executions, $3242$ grammar
comparisons and the recompilation of $14$ canonical derivations in the second; the suite of the
first with $5$ capture variants and $37800$ further opener checks in the third; $43$ regression
cases, $14$ positives with the spelling \texttt{true}, $4350$ grammar comparisons, $504$ checks of
active scalars and $22$ executions in the fourth; $8$ translated certificates, $62$ executions and
$492$ checks of opaque fixed points, and $756$ executions of the strict programs and their controls,
in the fifth; each reports no unexpected outcome. The proofs of
\cref{cert:lem:local-validation,cert:prop:intrinsic-forms,cert:lem:opaque,cert:prop:opaque,cert:prop:shifted-strict}
are written proofs for ideal executions; the translators of the packages are untrusted producers of
certificates, and no package claims a formalization in a proof assistant.
\item \pkg{exact\_threshold} asks whether the raw calculus with variable-only substitution of
\cref{comp:thm:rawvsub}, which differs from the native one on open sequents, derives every closed
native theorem; a candidate is $\Q{y}(\It{y}{0}{u}{u}=0)$, whose usual proof by induction joins the
successor computation with the hypothesis of induction at a compound source. Its note records that
an interpretation by three colors fails at \rulename{DI}: for $B(x,y)\defeq(\It{y}{x}{u}{u}=x)$ the
premise of \rulename{DI} by cases holds at a defective witness while no witness of the ordinary
domain makes $\Q{y}B(x,y)$ true. Neither direction is proved. \pkg{retained\_validation} repeats the
remark of \pkg{exact\_masks} that a total evaluator of all primitive recursive programs is
impossible by diagonalization.
\item \pkg{local\_validation} contains a basis with forward iteration and induction on equations
that lets one parameter change (\texttt{tail\_induction.tex}), which derives the backward
computation equation and is admissible in $\iPRA$; it is not treated in this chapter and no checker
is given for it. The package asks whether the successor equation of forward iteration implies the
backward one under ordinary induction on equations alone, and whether this induction can be given
by an inexpensive native binder or packet rule.
\item The finite tests of \pkg{checker\_research}, \pkg{scope\_free\_audit}, \pkg{stack\_clock} and the
compact refinement of \pkg{composition} are implementation evidence: $76$ certificate cases and $32$
executions in the first; $476$ executions of the first-line program and its control and $47$
expectations of $L^{\mathrm n}_{\mathrm f}$ in the second; $6913$ run comparisons, $6554$ grammar
comparisons, $17280$ opener comparisons, $4704$ mutated exports and $408$ executions in the third; and
$35$ cases in $280$ executions in the fourth; each reports no unexpected outcome. The proofs of
\cref{cert:prop:same-runs,cert:prop:printed} are written proofs for ideal executions; the producer of
certificates of \pkg{scope\_free\_audit} is untrusted, and no package claims a formalization in a proof
assistant. Both \pkg{checker\_research} and \pkg{scope\_free\_audit} report finite audits of the sources
of the repository in which their programs do not occur; these are not claims of priority.
\item None of the packages claims a minimal program, a simplest
axiomatization, or a result about the arithmetic basis. The statements of
\cref{cert:sec:notes} about eager iteration concern the theory
$T_{\mathrm e}$ and the standard model; they are not lower bounds for
axiomatizations of $\iPRA$.
\end{itemize}
